page_shift=14. Bionic linker_phdr.cpp uses MADV_DONTNEED to identify ELF padding
and caps representable padding at the kernel's 64 KiB ELF segment alignment.
These are positive SDK consumers for page emulation and migration behavior.
The complete shared MM/proc review remains unfinished; the following corrections
are recorded without treating their entire files or all candidates as final.

7f9fdddd5bf8 uses one inode-size snapshot for each pair of real/emulated EOF
checks. With two independent reads, concurrent truncation could select the
anonymous-tail fallback even when emulation was disabled. At 4 KiB, both limits
now derive from the same size, so that fallback is unreachable. The locked
filemap path also drops the folio reference before returning its tail result;
ACK omitted that put. NEED_ANONPAGE moves from 0x080000, inside the native
hardware-poison hstate-index mask, to the unused 0x008000 bit. This is an internal
source-built flag, not a userspace ABI or a preserved vendor-binary encoding.

c75a4c447656 restores native LTS do_anonymous_page rejection of VM_SHARED.
ACK had relaxed that guard to permit shared file tail emulation. Source tracing
shows a read fault can install the global zero page into such a VMA; its later
write fault enters do_wp_page's shared branch, where vm_normal_page returns
NULL for the zero page, wp_pfn_shared has no pfn_mkwrite callback for ordinary
file mappings, and wp_page_reuse makes the existing PTE writable. That violates
the global zero-page invariant. Anonymous tail folios also do not meet shared
file page_mkwrite/reverse-mapping expectations. This conclusion is source
analysis; no exploitation or runtime mapping test was performed.

The restoration is a deliberate partial adaptation: private mapping tail
emulation remains, but shared mappings beyond real EOF's final 4 KiB page
return SIGBUS even inside an emulated 16 KiB page. This preserves LTS protection
at the cost of ACK's shared-tail extension. It is not a claim that no Android
consumer needs that extension. A compatible shared-tail design remains an
unresolved compatibility question, recorded in the in-progress review evidence.

dbae496171a5 parses page_shift into a temporary, validates it and only then
publishes it. A rejected duplicate parameter can no longer replace a previously
valid enabled shift with a negative/out-of-range value. The maximum is the
actual __MAX_PAGE_SHIFT=14 used by the fixed x86 vDSO data/clock layout and
vdso2c's 16 KiB image alignment. Reject previously advertised 32/64 KiB emulation
rather than extending the SDK layout to unsupported modes. The pinned SDK
page_shift=14 and the existing 8 KiB mode remain accepted; no kernel or vDSO
runtime behavior is certified by this parser check.

check_page_emulation.py executes extracted current and pre-correction parser,
EOF-check, folio-cleanup and shared-guard code with host-side shims under ASan
and UBSan. It reproduces the old negative controls and verifies corrected
boundaries/state/cleanup; it also checks the actual fault-flag constants against
the native hstate mask. These checks are not a whole-MM concurrency test. All
source evidence is local; no additional remote resource was accessed.

20063c97d296 makes map_files lookup, revalidation and readlink recognize the
same padded data boundary emitted by readdir. It also accepts the native full
VMA range, retains every existing ptrace/mm_access/capability check, and skips
zero-length entries for wholly padded VMAs. The helper rejects mismatched starts,
intermediate ends and empty ranges. The extracted-helper host checks reproduce
the prior exact-range mismatch and verify both supported aliases; procfs runtime
and permission enforcement have not been tested. This fixes an internally
inconsistent interface rather than inferring absence of a userspace consumer.

Build 40 completed at 20063c97d296 with 145 verified signatures, including
the shared-VMA guard restoration and proc range fix. The protected-export
table check again matched 659 names and all 145 module flag/CRC tables.
No emulator has been booted. Later source edits require their own build.


d65b1b963b41 adapts ACK 1b1a4ce9cfb1 and 58e399dbc4c1 pagemap emulation.
The ACK seek wrapper returned the requested offset on mem_lseek errors, and
multiplied signed offsets without checking overflow. Scaling the whole read
count also allowed the native int copied counter to exceed its MAX_RW_COUNT
bound in emulated modes. Keep offsets/counts in userspace bytes and convert
only entry indices and bounded page-walk chunks in pagemap_read. This restores
native mem_lseek and removes the pread64-specific hook from fs/read_write.c.
The same file-operation convention now applies to other VFS read paths. Emit
a complete final entry when task_size ends partway through an emulated page,
rather than copying a fraction of its eight bytes. Native ptrace/open and
CAP_SYS_ADMIN PFN masking, page-table locking and newer LTS pagemap fixes remain.

GrapheneOS system/memory/libmeminfo/procmeminfo.cpp:365-390 and 466-575 positively
consume pagemap using pread64 offsets derived from getpagesize(). The ACK
aggregate policy is preserved: any constituent can set present/file/soft-dirty/
exclusive, while PFNs and swap identity are zero because constituents need not
be contiguous. This cannot supply libmeminfo's optional PFN-based accounting
or swap-offset information in emulated mode. UFFD-WP and guard-region metadata
are also absent from that ACK aggregate mask; broader compatibility of those
flags is unresolved, not inferred unnecessary from the SDK consumer search.
The ordinary 4 KiB mode retains native entries. check_pagemap_emulation.py runs
extracted reader/seek code for 4/8/16 KiB modes with simulated walks, allocation,
capability and copy operations, including error cleanup and old-code controls.
These host ASan/UBSan checks do not verify real procfs permissions or MM races.

aa723abf2d05 replaces the two unprotected padding-flag operations with an
atomic compare-exchange update and validates the count against the field width
and stable VMA bounds. Concurrent MADV_DONTNEED hints hold only a read lock;
the old clear/set sequence can combine counts (one and two become three).
Native LTS also permits VM_MAYBE_GUARD to be set atomically under a read lock,
so an ordinary whole-flags write can lose that update. The new operation
preserves native flags and retries on contention without acquiring mmap_lock
under the per-VMA lock. Split callers retain their existing lock/ownership
rules. vma_pad_pages still rejects invalid bounds; this is not a claim that
the old count race alone allowed arbitrary out-of-range page-table access.
check_padding_flags.py reproduces the old interleaving, forces a guarded
compare-exchange retry, and checks 14,962 split cases in both split directions.
It does not execute kernel concurrency or certify the whole padding feature.

Build 41 completed at d65b1b963b41, including the pagemap offset adaptation,
with 100 common and 45 virtual-device modules. All 145 signatures verified and
the tampered-payload control failed as expected. Protected exports/module
flag-CRC checks passed. Build 42 is checking the later atomic padding update.


c0cbe76ff03a adapts ACK 6312e8d3e0d swap-header handling. Local Android Toybox
mkswap.c obtains sysconf(_SC_PAGE_SIZE), writes last_page in those units and
places the signature at that page's end. Both fs_mgr.cpp and mmd/src/zram/setup.rs
invoke /system/bin/mkswap. At page_shift=14, ACK's signature-only move therefore
makes capacity one quarter of the formatted area. It also clears the signature
before a possibly failing header read and leaves its original page allocatable.

The adaptation validates emulated-format magic using a private header copy,
scales last_page and bad-page ranges into native page units, and reserves all
header pages in both swap-map and cluster metadata. Keep native LTS version,
endianness, file-size, bad-page-count, regular-file bad-block rejection and
CAP_SYS_ADMIN/LSM/inode checks. The native unsigned-int swap capacity limit is
retained after scaling. The existing bad-page table bound remains the native
first-header-page bound; support for larger tables is not added. A native 4 KiB
signature takes precedence if both signatures exist. Such a stale dual-signature
file is ambiguous; this does not certify arbitrary reformatting without clearing
old signatures. Android's freshly configured zram is the positive current use.
Neither cached signature is moved or cleared by the new emulation helper.

check_swap_emulation.py executes extracted helper, header validator, swap-map
and cluster-reservation code under host ASan/UBSan with simulated I/O/allocation.
It covers 4/8/16 KiB formats, both endiannesses, capacity limits, malformed and
out-of-range fields, bad-page expansion, header reservation, native-format
preference, error cleanup and old-source controls for quarter capacity and erased
magic. These are not real swap-I/O, filesystem-extent or runtime tests. No swap
was enabled, no OS was built, and no remote resource was used for this review.

Attempt 42 exited successfully, but it was started before the local common
fast-forward command was observed to finish. Its source-ordering assurance is
insufficient, so it is not added as a verified artifact run. Attempt 43 starts
only after the local fast-forward to c0cbe76ff03a completed and includes the
atomic padding and swap adaptations. This was a local sequencing issue, not a
new remote access.


d7f2fd2bd53e balances nr_rotate_swap using the SWP_SOLIDSTATE classification
recorded at activation. ACK queried the hibernation callback again at swapoff;
a changed result or unloaded callback could miss the decrement (or decrement
without a matching increment). Both callback sites remain, but accounting uses
the stored classification. The non-solid-state path explicitly clears that flag.
The host swap check covers all device/rotational/hook-result/initial-flag
combinations and reproduces the old missed decrement. Generic swap callbacks
are trusted kernel-module policy code and must obey the existing non-sleeping
locks and entry/accounting contracts; no runtime hook consumer is asserted.

c476769367dd rate-limits page-compat and padding diagnostics reachable from
unprivileged invalid mmap/mprotect/madvise requests. Native printk rate limiting
bounds log work while preserving alignment validation, error returns and hints.

Retaining ACK page-size emulation carries an explicit ASLR tradeoff: at
page_shift=14, its random-page-bit settings are reduced by two while x86
arch_rnd shifts by 14. The randomization byte span is preserved but has two fewer
possible placement bits than 4 KiB mode. The pinned SDK's page_shift=14 requires
16 KiB-aligned mappings; native 4 KiB mode is unchanged. This is not a claim of
unchanged ASLR entropy. Perf's default free mlock allowance also grows from
512+4 KiB to 512+16 KiB to accommodate one reported metadata page; native
capability/resource checks remain. Local libbpf/src/ringbuf.c uses getpagesize()
for producer mapping offsets, but the original offset-only helper did not cover
its combined data layout. The later BPF ring layout adaptation replaces that
helper and documents both observed consumers. Other BPF/perf integrations have
separate reviews.

Build 43 completed at c0cbe76ff03a with 145 verified signatures and matching
protected-export/module-flag tables. It includes atomic padding and swap format
translation. Build 44 checks the later rotation-accounting and rate-limit edits.


5bc7751ba8de fixes emulated mincore results for short lengths and native VMA
boundaries. Round the query to reported userspace pages and carry residency OR
state across native walks; emit exactly one byte per reported page. ACK divided
native counts at each boundary and could return success without writing the
required result byte. Native LTS owner/write-permission masking of file residency
and the non-swap-entry ordering remain unchanged. Host extracted-code checks
cover 240 range/chunk/VMA-split cases, holes, invalid buffers, allocation and copy
failures under ASan/UBSan, with old-source negative controls. Page walking and
permissions are simulated, so no runtime or side-channel test is claimed.

The complete mlock, mprotect and msync net deltas are limited to emulated page
alignment and associated headers. Keep native mlock accounting in real 4 KiB
units, capability/resource limits, mprotect LSM/VM_MAY/pkey/deny-write-exec checks,
and msync file-reference/writeback/error ordering. Newer LTS mlock zone accounting
and the pointer-based VMA flag update are retained. No downstream GrapheneOS
hardening is added by these reviews.


b39eeed468ef balances vendor madvise bypass cleanup: release the acquired
madvise lock and always drop the caller-owned VMA-name reference. Initialize
raw callback result slots. Callbacks retaining the name must acquire their own
reference. The host ASan/UBSan test reproduces the old lock/reference leaks and
checks ordinary, bypass, failure and callback-retained-reference paths; kernel
locks and real callbacks are not executed. The restricted process_madvise hook
still precedes native flags, iovec, ptrace and CAP_SYS_NICE checks for source
compatibility. A trusted callback replacing the operation must perform appropriate
validation itself. The normal path keeps these checks, native file-pageout
side-channel masking, sticky guard flags and TLB/lock cleanup.

The mremap review retains native DONTUNMAP anonymous-offset and lock-accounting
fixes. Proc stat retains exec_update_lock and ptrace filtering while reporting RSS
in the page units expected by Android. Exec retains native CLOEXEC/FUSE ordering,
post-de_thread POSIX timer cleanup and futex teardown, with reference-counted
DMA-buf accounting installed for the new task/files/MM. No runtime task or VMA
race test is claimed. All evidence for these reviews came from local checkouts.

Build 44 completed at c476769367dd: kernel images and 100 common plus 45 virtual
device modules generated. All 145 signatures verified, the altered-payload control
was rejected, and protected export/module flag/CRC tables matched. Build 45 checks
the subsequent mincore and madvise corrections at b39eeed468ef.


2b8ae3b1fce2 corrects initial stack accounting in emulated-page mode: the
16 KiB VMA now contributes four native pages to stack_vm/total_vm, instead of
one. Native accounting and limits use actual 4 KiB pages. Its stack-randomization
adaptation preserves the native byte span used by x86 stack_maxrandom_size and
mmap gap sizing, then aligns offsets to the emulated page size. ACK had multiplied
the offset span by four without enlarging that reserved randomization gap. The
local ACK e01cd7fd276e intent is alignment, which this retains. At 16 KiB the
native span has two fewer possible placement bits; this is the explicit alignment
tradeoff, not unchanged entropy. Native 4 KiB results remain exact. The focused
host test checks setup failure cleanup/accounting and 12,589,056 x86 mask inputs,
including old-source controls for both mismatches; no kernel exec or ASLR runtime
test is claimed. The obsolete __GENSYMS__ include guard is removed, with no
historical symbol CRC preservation contract.

Build 45 completed at b39eeed468ef with mincore and madvise cleanup corrections.
Both kernel images, all 100 common modules and all 45 virtual-device modules were
generated. All signatures and module/protected-export tables passed the same
checks, including rejection of the altered-payload signature control.


745944398038 derives the real-clock pointer used in vDSO time namespaces from
VDSO_TIMENS_PAGE_OFFSET. ACK 4840288585a9 spaces x86 data areas 16 KiB apart,
while the old reader still added 4 KiB and reached SIGBUS padding. The host check
extracts both lookup and kernel vvar fault code and verifies their agreement,
native spacing, insertion failure and the old-source control. TIME_NS remains
disabled in the actual SDK config; the correction keeps the imported shared
layout consistent for configuration customization and enables no extra platform.
Native clock calculations, readonly PFN mappings and sealed system mappings stay.
This does not claim a running namespace/vDSO test.

The PFN API necessity question has stronger local evidence: libmeminfo/pageacct.cpp
explicitly detects x86 page-size emulation and bypasses kpageflags/kpagecount/idle
file access. It returns flags zero, mapcount one and no idle pages. Retain ACK
6acc26714c0e API suppression only in emulated mode, since multiple noncontiguous
native PFNs cannot be truthfully represented as one emulated-page PFN. Exact
sharing/PFN-based working-set diagnostics are therefore limited in that mode;
this is a documented representation limit with a matching Android fallback, not
an inference from a missing consumer. Native 4 KiB APIs remain. The actual build
has PROC_PAGE_MONITOR and INCFS enabled and IDLE_PAGE_TRACKING disabled. PG_error
is functional for incfs SetPageError, not a historical-KMI reservation; retain its
proc reporting and native LTS KSM mapping-bit classification. The multi-file
UFFDIO_MOVE locking series remains a separate review.

Zoneinfo watermarks and reserve values use reported-page units, matching local
lmkd meminfo conversion and calc_zone_watermarks. Per-node/event counters remain
native as in ACK; lmkd computes refault ratios using counters from the same source.
This mixed diagnostic format is recorded explicitly and is not a claim that all
VM statistics are converted. No userspace pressure test or OS build was run.
All these reviews used local checkouts; URLs printed in local commit messages
were not accessed remotely.


8d75f9fa7f08 corrects filemap callback lifetime hazards found in the full net-delta
review. Capture the page-cache index before write_end can release its folio;
retain an extra folio reference across the unlocked mapped-folio callback when
that tracepoint is enabled; and pass NULL VMA to readahead customization when
maybe_unlock_mmap_for_io released the fault lock. That hook already receives NULL
from native read-ahead call sites in mm/readahead.c. A callback must tolerate it
and cannot rely on a valid VMA after lock release. Other fault callbacks receiving
vmf after a dropped fault lock likewise cannot dereference its VMA then. Native
I/O/locking order remains and the mapped-folio callback still runs after unlock.
The host ASan/UBSan check executes the full extracted generic write function and
both hook blocks; simulated teardown reproduces heap-use-after-free in all three
old-source controls. These demonstrate source lifetime errors under modeled
teardown, not an executed kernel race or exploit.

69eb820a7f4c adapts reclaim_shmem_address_space to native filemap_get_folios
batches. folio_isolate_lru explicitly requires an elevated caller-owned reference;
the raw XArray/RCU walk did not take one. Drop lookup references before reclaim
consumes isolation references, reclaim each batch and reschedule, and saturate
the exported int count at INT_MAX. This avoids an unbounded isolated list and
preserves reference ownership with large folios and concurrent cache changes.
The host check covers 114 count/hole/value/off-LRU cases plus invalid mappings,
return saturation and an old missing-reference control. Actual XArray concurrency,
swap and MM reclamation were not run. These paths remain within the unfinished
filemap/shmem feature review, so this correction alone does not finalize every
commit touching those files. All evidence was local.


Build 46 completed at 745944398038 including stack and vDSO corrections. Both
images, 100 common modules and 45 virtual-device modules were generated; all 145
signatures verified and the tampered-payload control was rejected. Protected
exports and flag/CRC tables matched. Build 47 checks the subsequent filemap/shmem
lifetime changes at 69eb820a7f4c. The unchanged emulator build script remains the
only workflow run; no OS build, prebuilt import, emulator boot or flashing.


05ae9392c7c8 completes the caller-held invalidate_lock contract introduced by
ACK 4808e3cd8037. Its generic fsverity reader is not present in the selected LTS
filesystem implementation, so adapt the same shared-lock bracketing to both
ext4 and F2FS Merkle-tree readers, and retain the F2FS redirty_blocks hunk.
The metadata ioctl and compression caller chains do not already hold that lock.
This restores LTS exclusion of concurrent mapping invalidation; native verity
hash verification, error handling and filesystem algorithms remain intact.

The same adaptation pins existing-folio arguments for the OEM readahead callback
with filemap_get_folio before read_pages can yield to reclaim. A raw XArray
existence lookup gives no lifetime guarantee. If the referenced lookup fails,
skip that notification; callbacks for successfully allocated folios remain.
Callback modules must take their own reference if retaining an argument, obey
the caller's lock/NOFS context, and not consume its reference. The change adds
no lookup reference when the callback is disabled. Duplicate hook includes are
removed. The focused host test extracts complete readahead and filesystem
functions; ASan reproduces the old use-after-free with simulated cache teardown,
and lock shims detect all three old unlocked call sites. These are source-level
regression checks, not actual filesystem races or kernel lockdep execution.

The complete e82cb2953b8c hook patch is retained: all four allocation call sites
pass a caller-owned folio before page-cache insertion, with declaration and
export. It remains a trusted module interface; no vendor binary ABI is promised.
The complete 0025f977996f correction remains in forced readahead, pinning each
folio before changing its MGLRU OEM bit and dropping the reference afterwards.
The larger MGLRU policy series remains a separate review. All evidence was read
locally; links embedded in ACK commit messages were not contacted.

Build 47 completed at 69eb820a7f4c, generating both images and 100 common plus
45 virtual-device modules. All 145 signatures verified; the altered payload
was rejected. Protected exports and module flag/CRC tables matched. Build 48
checks 05ae9392c7c8 after the completed local common fast-forward and manifest
pin update. No dependency changes were needed for these corrections.


57131aee4cb9 restores native CMA allocation contracts: validate an optional CMA
pointer before reading its GCMA flag; preserve native GFP flags, including the
__GFP_COMP required by cma_alloc_folio; restrict the downstream mask only to
GCMA. Native LTS range/zone checks and activation-error cleanup remain. Preserve
incoming IRQ state in allocation/diagnostics, suppress sleeping retries without
__GFP_DIRECT_RECLAIM, and decide GCMA discard rescheduling from that flag rather
than exact equality with GFP_ATOMIC (which fails with NOWARN/NORETRY additions).
The actual SDK has HUGETLBFS disabled; this is a correction to the shared native
API, not a newly enabled hugepage platform.

The same commit fixes GCMA unmount versus its background eviction worker. Pin
one inode under the hash lock, release it before acquiring inode->pages, drain
and drop the reference. A linked hash entry owns an inode reference; the worker
cannot free the selected inode after that pin. The pages-to-hash lock order
remains. Emptying every XArray prevents later workers from erasing another page
through the dying gcma_fs; final inode RCU freeing does not dereference gcma_fs.
The former saved-next hash traversal had neither a lock nor a reference while
the worker could delete entries. Host extracted-function checks reproduce that
UAF and the earlier null dereference under ASan/UBSan, plus native-flag rejection,
atomic retry sleep and IRQ-state loss controls. They model locks and eviction;
real RCU scheduling, allocator concurrency and reserved pools are not executed.

b1ae0b82acce invalidates the inode cache when a compound folio is put or
invalidated. Merely skipping it can leave older base-page entries available
later with stale file contents. Large folios still fall back to filesystem reads;
ordinary base-page caching remains. Whole-inode invalidation may reduce hit rate,
but avoids implementing a new multi-page backend protocol and preserves data
coherence. Supplied mapping is used for invalidation when page->mapping can be
null. Host extracted wrappers reproduce old stale results and check disabled,
ordinary and compound paths. Native verity cache-hit verification remains.

CMA/GCMA/cleancache are retained for the existing Android configuration and
vendor source interfaces. Exact SDK GCMA reserved-pool provisioning is not yet
established: current config enables the code with default CMA size zero, and
local mm/cma.c is an actual consumer, while BTF declarations alone are not
runtime evidence. This uncertainty is recorded in unresolved-feature-questions.
No new memory pool or future platform is enabled. Cleancache backend registration
has no unregister mechanism; registered operation storage and code must remain
resident. The supplied GCMA backend is built-in. No historical layout is promised.

Build 48 succeeded at 05ae9392c7c8, including both images, 100 common modules and
45 virtual-device modules; all signatures and module/export tables checked and
the altered payload was rejected. Build 49 checks b1ae0b82acce after completed
common fast-forward and manifest pinning. All review and build inputs in this

The complete ashmem Rust driver, range/shrinker/backing-file wrappers and UAPI
were reviewed against local Android libcutils/bionic and native LTS helpers.
libcutils deliberately keeps ashmem for older application targets and gates
memfd on runtime/vendor/policy conditions, so the device API remains necessary.
Memfd compatibility ioctls retain native memfd_fcntl seal checks and only expose
shmem memfds through the existing SHMEM_FL_MEMFD gate. Pinning is a no-op for
memfd as required by local libcutils tests; real ashmem retains purge tracking.
The in-tree randomized kernel range test was read but was not executed.

9c710998db9a fixes legacy GET_PIN_STATUS input sizing. Its UAPI encodes _IO
(size zero), but the old C driver copies struct ashmem_pin for all three pin
commands. Rust previously bounded its reader to zero bytes and rejected the
query. Use the fixed two-u32 payload size without changing the ABI number.
The same commit uses the existing iterator helper for actual toggle-read byte
counts and partial-read positions. The host Rust check compiles numbers from the
actual C UAPI and executes extracted dispatch/read methods with usercopy shims;
old-source controls reproduce both failures. It uses the already pinned local
1.91.1.p3 Rust toolchain and performs no kernel syscall or Android OS build.

3ea7a304b5dc gates global ashmem toggle writes with CAP_SYS_ADMIN. The supplied
system/core/rootdir/ueventd.rc grants /dev/ashmem* mode 0666, and SELinux
private/file_contexts labels /dev/ashmem(.*)? as ashmem_libcutils_device. The
source policy grants write and, for allowed ashmem client domains, open access.
Those patterns also match ashmem_unpinning_enable and the ignore-protection
controls. The original control driver had no capability check, so relying on
the default miscdevice mode would not protect these global operations. Check
privilege before consuming input or changing state. Ordinary ashmem APIs and
control reads remain; administrative global policy changes still work. Host
extracted-write checks cover denied/no-input-consumption, privileged success,
invalid data and the old unprivileged mutation control. This is an integration
security correction, not a port of GrapheneOS kernel hardening. No compiled
SELinux-policy or running-device claim is made.

Native LTS shrinker_free callback completion protects Rust private-data cleanup;
range lists own backing files and serialize reclaim with pin/unpin. Registered
ashmem is built-in. The shared EOF-tail issue under page-size emulation remains
separately unresolved; this source review does not certify that corner case.

modules, all 145 signatures verified, tampered payload rejected and matching
export/module tables. Build 50 checks the ashmem changes at 3ea7a304b5dc after

Build 50 completed successfully at 3ea7a304b5dc with both kernel images, 100
common modules and 45 virtual-device modules. All 145 module signatures verified

659 protected exports plus all module flag/CRC tables matched. The original
emulator build command was used. Compilation and artifact checks do not verify
runtime ashmem behavior or SDK compatibility. No remote resource was accessed.

15bccdfefe6d skips mmap readahead for faults in ELF padding, including VMAs
containing only padding after split_pad_vma. Previously the executable path could
subtract a start inside padding from an earlier data end, underflowing ra->size
and requesting a huge prefetch window. Snapshot the data end once while the
fault lock is held; use that snapshot for both bounds and executable size.
Asynchronous padding faults also skip prefetch. This leaves real fault handling,
file access checks and the default-off VMA-boundary control intact. It prevents
unnecessary I/O/memory pressure without weakening an LTS fix. Extracted complete
functions with UBSan exercise data/padding/split VMAs, both control states,
executable/readaround/sequential/THP/async paths and old-source failure controls;
no actual page faults, disk I/O or kernel lock races are executed.

The default-off /sys/kernel/mm/vma_readahead_boundary/enabled interface is kept
for generic Android runtime compatibility. Local system/bionic/goldfish searches
did not establish a writer; this is uncertainty, not evidence of dispensability.
Owner-only writes, boolean validation and native static-key synchronization are
retained. Ordinary read callers default to no VMA limit. Native minimum-folio
alignment remains and can span a requested boundary at mandatory folio granularity;
the control is prefetch policy, not an access-control boundary. No benchmark or
runtime compatibility claim is made. Build 51 checks 15bccdfefe6d with the unchanged
workflow. All sources examined were local, including complete ACK patches;
commit-message URLs were not visited.

Build 51 completed at 15bccdfefe6d: both kernel images, 100 common modules and
45 virtual-device modules, all 145 signatures verified, altered payload rejected,
and 659 protected exports plus module flag/CRC tables matched.

20730118bf05 fixes partial-PMD smaps accounting. Android splits a padded ELF VMA
into displayed data/padding ranges, but the native PMD callback counted the full
huge page for either subrange. Count the requested byte range and use native
per-page accounting for partial PMDs; whole-PMD requests retain the original fast
path. Native absent/special-entry checks and migration-safe PSS accounting stay
intact. Host extracted accounting functions check all 511 splits across file,
anonymous, shmem and device categories, both present and migration entries; the
old implementation doubles resident bytes across the display split. No real
page tables or running-kernel concurrency were tested.

The per-VMA smaps/numa_maps/rollup series retains native pagewalk lock verification
and bounds. RCU is dropped for potentially sleeping walks while the real VMA
stays read-locked; reacquiring RCU resets the iterator. Fallback holds mmap_lock,
gate VMAs skip walking, and errors unwind references/locks. Padded display copies
are never inserted into the tree; data output uses the actual VMA. Partial and
COW shmem ranges use the page-table walker instead of whole-inode swap counts.
Local libmeminfo actively reads smaps and rollup; its excluded-name list only
contains vectors/vsyscall, not padding. ACK rollup excludes the padding region,
whereas individual smaps has its separate padding record; preserve that existing
policy without claiming an atomic cross-file snapshot. Extracted range tests
cover endpoints, COW selection and modeled RCU/mmap paths. Native LTS guard-bit,
PMD absence, pagemap scan/write-protection and PFN authorization fixes remain.

A separate large-folio prefault-padding interaction is recorded as pending in
unresolved-feature-questions.json; this review does not silently certify it.
Build 52 checks 20730118bf05. All evidence and toolchains used here were local;
source and commit-message remote links were not accessed.

The large-folio prefault-padding question above is now resolved at source-selection
level: five complete mapping/suitability helpers are byte-identical across ACK,
LTS and the port (large-folio-padding-comparison.json). Keep this common behavior
and ACK's requested-range clamp/padding-fault skip. Bionic's local DropPaddingPages
uses the hint to reduce reclaim work; the real VMA remains mapped. No new global
THP restriction is needed to preserve either input's behavior. The hint is not a
strict ban on larger-folio population, nor an access-control boundary. This does
not resolve the separately documented shared EOF-tail issue. No new runtime or
remote-resource claim is made.

Build 52 completed at 20730118bf05 with both kernel images, 100 common and 45
virtual-device modules. All signatures verified, the altered-payload control
was rejected, and protected-export/module tables matched.

110e353eded3 preserves PM_UFFD_WP and PM_GUARD_REGION during emulated pagemap
aggregation. ACK's original commit suppresses UFFD status on the premise that
x86 16 KiB emulation does not support it, but this port retains native userfaultfd
and actual configuration enables USERFAULTFD and HAVE_ARCH_USERFAULTFD_WP. Local
Linux selftests actively inspect that bit. Keep the existing any-subpage OR rule
and include native protection/guard status; enforcement paths and authorization
are unchanged. Extracted reader tests cover all 64 combinations of aggregate
status flags and demonstrate the earlier lost bits. Native-mode entries remain
untouched. No real userfaultfd/guard syscall test was performed.

The emulated entry still deliberately suppresses PFN and swap identities, matching
ACK: independent base pages need not have contiguous or shared identities. Local
libmeminfo's PFN fallback handles the former. Its ReadVmaStats and libsmapinfo
proportional-swap code really consume swap offsets; therefore pagemap-derived swap
identity/accounting remains unavailable under emulation. Reporting fabricated
zero offsets would falsely identify unrelated swap slots as shared. This is a
known ACK compatibility limitation, not evidence of no consumer or accurate
replacement statistics. Native 4 KiB pagemap and separate smaps byte accounting
remain. Likewise, ACK's ORed present/exclusive flags describe any subpage rather
than a guarantee of full-group residency/exclusivity. No new UAPI or Android OS
changes are introduced. Build 53 checks 110e353eded3. All resources used were local;
r.android.com/3424862 and other source-message URLs were not visited.

Build 53 completed at 110e353eded3 with both images, 100 common and 45 virtual
device modules. All 145 signatures verified against the build certificate, the
modified-payload control was rejected, and 659 protected exports plus module
flag/CRC tables matched. No running-kernel test was performed.

Full filemap/shmem source selection now records the prior reference-lifetime,
readahead, reclaim, cache-coherence and native LTS preservation checks together.
Generic vendor callbacks are retained with native lock/reference contracts;
module callbacks remain trusted kernel code, not a userspace security boundary.
The shared EOF-tail emulation candidate b3c77090341c is explicitly excluded from
this scoped finalization and remains unresolved. All evidence was local.

8a7a4fcdab67 corrects top-down emulated mapping alignment. The native iterator
returns an exclusive upper bound, and x86 mmap_base can be only 4 KiB aligned.
Rounding a selected address upward can move its end outside the chosen free
interval, with a possible overlap of an adjacent mapping. Round down before
checking the previous stack guard; retain the extra alignment reservation,
limit checks and bottom-up behavior. The complete extracted functions pass
27,648 host interval/guard/alignment cases, including 1,920 old-source failures
and unchanged native 4 KiB results. This is an interval shim, not a real Maple
Tree or mmap execution. Build 54 checks this source with the unchanged workflow.

The vm_map_pages batching optimization in ACK 73037b1bedd1 is omitted: the
existing API and per-page validity/protection/partial-failure contracts are
unchanged, so Android compatibility does not require replacing the LTS loop.
The result commit is recorded in explicit-code-decisions.json. This conclusion
comes from both complete insertion paths, not from an absent consumer search.

A separate DMA-buf OOM accounting issue is now explicitly recorded for further
review: nonfatal allocation failures can leave a later unaccount operation
without a matching increment and undercount still-live references. Native
file/buffer lifetime references are separate, but lmkd consumes these statistics.
Cross-file VMA lifecycle candidates remain provisional while that policy is
reviewed. All sources here were local; source-message URLs were not accessed.

Builds 54 (8a7a4fcdab67) and 55 (623c51beadea) each produced both images,
100 common and 45 virtual-device modules. All signatures verified, altered
payloads were rejected, and all export/module tables matched.

ab9389a3d802 initializes compaction/migration callback state and resets the
PSI bypass each work cycle. A completion-only callback must not receive an
uninitialized cookie, and a removed callback must not leave old policy active.
The current SDK also enables stack initialization, but explicit defaults avoid
depending on that compiler setting. Native compaction capture, free validation,
RCU progress and migration cleanup fixes remain. Source review and compilation
are the checks here; no migration or compaction runtime test was performed.

769c2a3ea3f5 resets memory.high hook results on each retry. Previously a callback
that skipped reclaim once could leave the skip/progress values active after it
was removed. The extracted control-path check restores native reclaim, checks
PSI entry/exit pairing, default delay bounds and CSS cleanup, and reproduces the
old stale bypass. It does not perform actual reclaim or PSI measurement. The
same commit omits two KMI enum-visibility marker objects while retaining the
callable memcg_page_state export and native enum headers.

ACK's intentional default to exclude memory.high throttle delays from PSI is
retained. Local lmkd registers system PSI pressure monitors and libprocessgroup
configures memory.high; treating artificial throttling as global memory scarcity
can alter Android killing behavior. Actual reclaim and native hard-limit checks
remain. Vendor hooks can opt back into PSI and tune reclaim/delay policy; they
are trusted kernel code and must preserve CSS ownership and LRU/list/folio
contracts. This policy choice does not claim measured LMK behavior. Build 56
checks the latest hook/marker changes. All evidence and tools were local.

Build 56 completed at 769c2a3ea3f5 with both kernel images, 100 common and
45 virtual-device modules. All 145 signatures verified against the build
certificate, the altered-payload control was rejected, and 659 protected exports
plus module flag/CRC tables matched. Compilation and artifacts were checked;
no runtime memory-pressure or migration test was performed.

38e13dcb4814 fixes restricted-CMA PCP integration. The original fallback overwrote
the caller's list pointer after an empty CMA lookup; ordinary pages could then
refill the CMA list while existing movable cache entries were ignored. Preserve
the original pointer. Give CMA a separate high-order list instead of sharing the
movable list, preserving the stated __GFP_CMA restriction at all PCP orders. Also
use the ordinary fallback-type bound in init_pageblock_migratetype so disabling
mobility grouping cannot relabel a CMA block as unmovable. Native allocation,
watermark, isolation and free validation remain. This changes internal layout,
which rebuilt modules consume; preserving historical KMI is out of scope.
The extracted selection/setup checks cover CMA/THP enabled and disabled and all
supported PCP high orders, reproducing all three old-source defects. No actual
CMA reservation or buddy-allocator concurrency was exercised.

72565355d3de resets vendor decisions per zone, folio, memcg and allocator retry,
initializes reclaim progress, and uses a page returned by the retry callback
instead of discarding it. MGLRU vendor reclaim now shares the native zero-progress
stop. This avoids stale skips and repeated no-progress work; trusted callbacks
still own their chosen policy and supplied pages' allocation/zeroing contracts.
The extracted MGLRU control checks pass 429 scan/protection/rotation/progress
cases and reproduce the earlier zero-progress repetition. Simple per-iteration
initializations were checked by source review and compilation, not a running
memory-pressure test. Build 57 checks both source commits with the original
emulator workflow.

The retained MGLRU behavior has concrete Android relevance. Local lmkd/reaper.cpp
calls process_mrelease; mmgrab/mmdrop protects the walker mm_struct without adding
an mm_users reference that delays reaping. The mm-list lock protects acquisition,
mmap read locking protects page-table/VMA walking, and exit_mmap takes its write
lock before destroying the tree. Offlined memcg pacing/rotation matches Android's
per-process cgroups (libprocessgroup/processgroup.cpp), retaining minimum/low
protection and a bounded residual scan. Native LTS large-folio reference checks,
dirty/writeback reclaim handling and tasks-RCU progress remain. Executable-file
and MADV_WILLNEED promotion preserve ACK's memory policy; their effect on actual
latency or killing was not measured. Root shrinkers run before memcg-specific
ones; shrink_slab's native root/non-root routing and reference rules remain.

Reclaim hooks are trusted kernel extension points. In particular, keep_reclaimed_folio
runs after reference freezing and must restore the supplied reference count when
it keeps the folio; asynchronous writeback/list bypass callbacks must take over
the skipped list/reference work. MADV_PAGEOUT private callbacks must consume or
move remaining list entries and maintain their begin/end lifetime. These are
explicit source contracts, not permissions granted to userspace. Zeroing and
free-validation bypass hooks likewise require replacement implementation of the
skipped obligations; the default paths keep native LTS checks. No claim is made
that arbitrary vendor callback code is safe. All evidence was local; commit-message
lkml/lore links were read as text and were not visited.

Build 57 completed at 72565355d3de with both kernel images, 100 common and
45 virtual-device modules. All 145 signatures verified, the altered-payload
control was rejected, and 659 protected exports plus module flag/CRC tables
matched. No kernel runtime test was performed.

133e0283da81 splits ACK 816567b17866: keep the lru_disable_count and
lru_cache_disable module exports, but restore the exact native LTS function.
The ACK optimization publishes the disable count after synchronization/draining,
leaving a window for concurrent LRU or buffer cache insertion while draining.
The native implementation publishes it first and waits out preexisting readers
before draining. Preserving its synchronization effect is more important than
this internal optimization; the Android source API remains available. Evidence:
full local ACK patch, lru_cache_disabled readers in folio_batch_add_and_move and
fs/buffer.c, and native function comparison. Source review and compilation are
the checks for this native restoration, not a runtime concurrency test.

6dcd44cc22af guards the memblock_end_of_DRAM export with ARCH_KEEP_MEMBLOCK,
matching the existing memblock_free export guard. The actual SDK disables ARCH_KEEP_MEMBLOCK, so memblock_discard can free
dynamically allocated region arrays that the function dereferences. MEMORY_HOTPLUG=y
keeps its __meminit code/static data; the concrete SDK hazard is therefore freed
backing arrays, not necessarily freed code. Without hotplug, code also has init
lifetime. This corrects the broader wording in the commit message. The built-in early
boot function remains available; a persistent module API is exposed only when
its storage actually persists. Optional memsize reporting and its counter
accuracy remain under separate review and are not certified by this export fix.
Build 58 checks the LRU and export changes. No remote source was accessed.

Build 58 completed at 6dcd44cc22af with both kernel images, 100 common and
45 virtual-device modules. All 145 signatures verified with the build certificate,
the altered-payload control was rejected, and 659 protected exports plus all
module flag/CRC tables matched. These are compilation/artifact checks.

## Swap I/O source policy and read capability

14b3570de295 adapts ACK 58ef7fcc48ec to use its separate read capability in
swap_read_folio, do_swap_page and shmem_swapin_folio. The combined setter is
unchanged, so ordinary swapon still enables both capabilities. A write-only flag
previously selected synchronous reads and cacheless allocation unintentionally;
its absence now selects the normal swapcache/async path. Filesystem swap retains
precedence. Native folio ownership, memcg charging, exclusivity checks and error
completion remain. Independent timing hooks now receive initialized cookies.

The synchronous read replacement hook is a trusted kernel source contract:
callbacks must provide a complete folio or report an error and perform the
completion/unlock obligations they replace. Native callbacks leave the full-size
bio, task reference and completion processing intact. Timing end observers have
a caller-owned folio reference, including zswap writeback and read readahead.
No SDK callback implementation is assumed; generic vendor interfaces are retained.

check_swap_io.py executes the actual dispatch functions with host I/O and tracing
shims under UBSan. All 387 combinations passed, including read-only/write-only
flags, filesystem precedence, independently registered timing callbacks, early
zeromap/zswap success/failure and pressure-accounting cleanup. The old write-only
false match is reproduced as a control. Real bios, PSI, completion races and
Android runtime were not tested. The two cacheless callers are also checked for
the same read capability. All evidence is local.

## UFFDIO_MOVE and reverse-mapping locks

ACK 05fd7cf26c48 and 8f8f7e4d811c are retained as a coordinated change. GrapheneOS
ART runtime/gc/collector/mark_compact.cc positively uses UFFDIO_MOVE for compacting
GC and probes its availability. Anonymous reverse-map walkers now lock the folio,
so moving an exclusive folio between different anon_vma roots can serialize on
that folio instead of the source anon_vma write lock. Reclaim/page-idle/DAMON
trylock failures retain best-effort behavior; memory-failure process collection
locks the folio. Existing migration, split, unmap and mkclean walkers already
hold it. RCU, anon_vma references and tree read locks still protect object lifetime.

56b1b2fc24a3 completes the huge-PMD move portion of this contract. Source comparison
confirms it matches the normalized ACK function with the two native LTS migration
changes retained: the updated predicate and waiting on the real source PMD rather
than a stack copy. PTE/PMD revalidation, exclusivity, DMA-pin checks and rollback,
notifier/TLB handling and protection reconstruction remain. The PTE move path also
keeps the LTS stale-EAGAIN reset. This removes a lock only with the corresponding
walker locking in place; importing the lock removal alone would be unsafe.

The huge-page review retains native LTS zero-folio publication/reclamation
synchronization, deferred-split queue/refcount fixes, dirty PMD propagation,
mapping unlock before dropping the inode pin, hwpoison flag ordering and memcg
charging. Generic split policy hooks are kept with their native locking/context
contracts. A bypass callback must perform the omitted split correctly or return
an error; it does not excuse invalid refcounts, XArray state or list ownership.

Evidence is full local source/patch and caller review plus function comparisons
in evidence/uffd-rmap-source-check.json. No concurrent UFFD/reclaim, memory-failure
injection, Android GC runtime or performance measurement was executed. Links in
local commit messages were not visited.

## Memsize current-target selection

8e8cbee1ce28 removes the optional ACK memsize report and its accounting calls.
Both report interfaces, including procfs, are compiled inside DEBUG_FS &&
ARCH_KEEP_MEMBLOCK. The existing SDK configuration enables DEBUG_FS but does not
retain memblock. The only x86 selector for ARCH_KEEP_MEMBLOCK is INTEL_TDX_HOST,
a separate host role outside this ordinary guest target. Neither memsize boot
parameter value can expose a report in the requested build. Full counter-reader
review shows these values feed only the report, not allocation, actual page
release or native memory statistics. This evidence, rather than absence of a
userspace search hit, supports the current-target omission.

The report's counters used requested ranges rather than actual reserved/removed
bytes; concurrent reporting mutated shared region records, and late-free detection
assumed 64-KiB sampling. Those concerns are not certified as safe for a future
target. The report is absent here, including its DT discovery, early bookkeeping
and nonfunctional report parameter. No platform is added to enable it.

Native LTS memblock implementation is restored except for the two existing
ARCH_KEEP_MEMBLOCK-guarded generic exports. mm_init.c and memblock.h are exactly
native LTS, preserving hotplug allocation failure/counter fixes and device memmap
initialization. free_reserved_area and dma_contiguous_reserve_area also regain
their native bodies. The independently retained CMA/GCMA bridge/export remains.
No upstream fix, allocator boundary or permissions check is weakened. Compilation
is the verification for removing the now-unreferenced reporting code; no boot or
report runtime test was performed. All evidence was local.

Build 59 completed at 56b1b2fc24a3 with both kernel images, 100 common and
45 virtual-device modules. All 145 signatures verified, the altered-payload
control failed verification, and all module flag/CRC tables plus 659 protected
exports matched. This validates compilation/artifacts for swap and UFFD source
changes; later memsize removal is checked separately in build 60.

## Process-memory denial, OOM and allocation diagnostics

907c42c0138b fixes an mm_users reference leak when ACK's process_vm access hook
rejects a request. Native mm_access authorization still runs first; hook denial
now drops its acquired mm before the task and temporary page array. This prevents
repeated denied operations from pinning a target address space. The actual core
function passes 38 host-shim tests for allocation/permission/hook failures and
partial copies; the old implementation reproduces the leak as a control. Real
ptrace/LSM access, page pinning and kernel races were not executed.

The OOM review retains generic victim/reaper policy hooks with native task/mm
lifetimes, eligibility and default behavior. Local lmkd sends SIGKILL and invokes
process_mrelease. Signal-hook opt-in reaping still requires successful native
permission checking/delivery and a task that will release its mm. NMI dump helpers
use task trylocks and skip memcg traversal; the actual SDK has NUMA disabled. This
is best-effort diagnostic source support, not proof of NMI safety on every config.

SLUB/vmalloc/page-owner/percpu diagnostics and source APIs are retained without
adding a hardware dump platform or historical binary layout. Iterating slab slots
is not a complete snapshot of live objects, and callbacks run under list locks
with IRQs disabled. Page extension users must retain their RCU lifetime; owner
metadata is racy diagnostic data. Replacement allocator/free callbacks must meet
the native size/alignment, initialized or frozen-reference, accounting and TLB
contracts they take over. Defaults preserve native checks and cleanup.

Native LTS NO_OBJ_EXT recursion handling, krealloc zeroing, hardened-usercopy
cache merging, CPU-hotplug/sheaf and sysfs/debugfs lifetime fixes are retained.
Vmalloc huge-page replacement retains LTS exclusion against ptdump. Zsmalloc keeps
the native handle-inclusive size-class fix plus ACK's reverse-order lock release;
nonmovable metadata excludes __GFP_CMA. No actual allocator-pressure race was run.

A concrete formatting limit was found in local libmeminfo/sysmeminfo.cpp:
ReadSlabInfo expects version 2.1 and exactly 16 row cells. Default slabinfo output
is preserved; vendor callbacks that append columns would break that reader and
are not claimed compatible. Vmalloc start/end observations are also not a paired
resource-lifetime API: end reports successful allocations only. All source and
consumer evidence in this review was local.

## Memory hook argument transport

3acc3712548b corrects two retained ACK memory hook interfaces.

The MGLRU abort hook introduced by ACK 3ebe18284e11 declared reclaimed progress
before the target but reversed them in TP_ARGS. The current native trace wrapper
forwards arguments twice before static dispatch, so one directly called callback
happens to see correct values. Multiple callbacks use an iterator with another
forwarding step and see reversed values; non-static dispatch has the same issue.
The corrected TP_ARGS makes policy inputs independent of callback count. This
avoids decisions based on a mistaken reclaim target/progress without changing
native reclaim behavior when no callback is registered. The separately retained
extended hook and LTS reclaim fixes remain unchanged.

ACK 108327bad812's contiguous-allocation failure hook narrows the end PFN to an
unsigned int while both native endpoints are unsigned long. The corrected
prototype preserves the full range on x86_64. Ordinary small SDK guests do not
reach that truncation boundary, but this is the contract of the retained generic
source API. A callback must rebuild with the corrected parameter type; historical
module binaries are explicitly out of scope. This diagnostic hook cannot bypass
the native isolation failure or turn it into successful allocation.

check_hook_arguments.py scans all 765 declarations for argument order, accepting
only the three documented block hooks' ignored-to-NULL adapters. It also compiles
the actual native tracepoint wrapper/iterator macros with host dispatch shims:
36 cases cover disabled/offline paths, one/two callbacks and 32/64-bit PFN ranges.
The corrected headers pass; old-header controls reproduce both defects. This is
argument transport validation, not live registration, RCU or static-call patching.
No claim is made that argument-name matching validates every hook's semantic
contract. All evidence was from local source; commit-message links were not opened.

Build 62 completed at 3acc3712548b, manifest cb65420, with both kernel images,
100 common and 45 virtual-device modules. All 145 signatures verified; the
modified-payload control was rejected. All flag/CRC tables and 659 protected
exports matched. This checks the memory-hook signature changes through the
existing emulator build; no kernel callback or emulator was run.

## Zram Android memory management and failure lifetimes

The full zram delta is retained for the existing Android memory daemon:
local mmd_setup.rs enables compressed_writeback, service.rs gates per-process
operations on it, and per_process_ioctls.rs implements range writeback, prefetch
and version 1. This is positive userspace evidence, not speculative platform
support. Native LTS write-limit exclusion/rounding, initialized disk-size access,
locked slot-handle capture, synchronous partial-page I/O and compressor cleanup
remain. The newer native deflate-window validation also remains.

8e348642e1f0 corrects three failure/lifetime problems in this retained code:

- A failed asynchronous read-request allocation previously returned success
  without filling the destination page. Propagate ENOMEM through the native
  parent-bio error path, so swap does not consume an unfilled successful read.
- A prefetched backing copy previously survived overwrite/discard because only
  swap_slot_free_notify erased it. Invalidate it in every slot_free call so later
  writeback cannot replace new data with an old cached backing block. Preserve
  cache retention for unchanged prefetched slots and their concurrent fault reads.
- ACK 243886974548 correctly waits for prefetch I/O before releasing the init
  lock and post-processing exclusion, but its zero-counter predicate can return
  before the last wake_up accesses the stack waitqueue. Use completion locking
  and a submission reference: completion cannot occur between submissions, and
  return waits until the last notifier finishes using the stack control. Both
  success and error paths release resources before notifying completion.

The same adaptation initializes writeback output fields before early errors.
That avoids returning stale user-provided range results or an uninitialized
legacy temporary. The SDK's INIT_STACK_ALL_ZERO already mitigates the latter;
the source now establishes initialization explicitly. Raw compressed writeback
continues zeroing the unused page tail before full-page storage writes.

The process ioctl deliberately uses CAP_SYS_NICE and get_task_mm, without a
ptrace access check. The earlier ACK commit message's no-address-disclosure
rationale is not valid for the final range API: next_addr and written counts
reveal memory-layout/activity metadata to an authorized caller. Retaining this
privileged memory-daemon authority is an explicit Android compatibility choice.
Global capability and device access checks remain; local mmd.te additionally
restricts current commands to mmd and denies legacy command 1 to all domains.
No claim is made that SYS_NICE plus device access cannot reveal target metadata.
Shared file pageout retains its file-owner/write permission check. Native PTE,
swap-device, task/mm lifetime and range validation remain. A stale PTE can make
selection approximate; it does not grant userspace raw memory access.

Prefetch is a best-effort hint: deferred I/O/population failures need not be
reported individually. The range budget counts matching candidate swap entries,
not guaranteed writes. Reported bytes count completed storage writes, and do not
count reuse of an already cached backing copy. ACK's access-time storage retains
seconds precision rather than LTS subsecond diagnostic precision.

35b41a30b529 supplies the native block compat pointer adapter because the existing
SDK configuration enables IA32 emulation. All four commands have identical
32/64-bit layouts/numbers, verified by compiling check_zram_uapi.c in both modes.
It reaches the same capability/device checks and does not change ABI structures.
The configured native mmd consumer is established; no 32-bit daemon run is claimed.

check_zram_lifetimes.py passes 21 extracted-function host-shim cases and a
controlled last-notifier interleaving check. Old-source controls expose read
allocation errors, retained stale cache entries, uninitialized outputs and the
premature-return interval. These tests do not execute kernel I/O, page walks,
static kernel lock primitives or Android services. All source/consumer and
primitive evidence was local; no commit-message or diagnostic URL was opened.

Build 63 completed at 8e348642e1f0, manifest f7b136a, with both kernel images,
100 common and 45 virtual-device modules. All 145 signatures verified and the
modified-payload control was rejected. All module flag/CRC tables and 659 protected
exports matched. This validates compilation and packaging of the zram lifetime
fixes; it does not execute swap I/O or the later compat ioctl dispatch addition.

## F2FS lock timing and priority interfaces

0e583bb7ddb4 adapts the ACK lock-timing/priority series onto native LTS F2FS.
Local GrapheneOS system/vold/IdleMaint.cpp implements SetMaxLockElapsedTime,
and VoldNativeService.cpp exposes it through the existing service. That is a
positive consumer for max_lock_elapsed_time. F2FS is configured in the SDK,
although its supplied data fstab uses ext4. Optional F2FS runtime use remains
unverified; retain the generic Android interfaces conservatively. This decision
does not establish the necessity of large-folio or dynamic device-alias changes.

The timing wrappers retain the native rwsem operations and use a per-acquisition
stack context. Separate contexts cover nested NAT/node-write and GC/checkpoint
locks. Failed trylocks undo a temporary priority boost, and all successful/error
releases use the context associated with their acquisition. The checkpoint's
special raw writer path remains unchanged. The vendor down-read hook remains
inside the native read-lock wrapper; a trusted callback that requests bypass
must still implement the required lock semantics.

Temporary priority adjustment defaults to disabled. Its sysfs mask identifies
which locks permit boosting; cp_rwsem, node_change and node_write boost readers
only. Already-higher-priority and RT tasks retain their policy. Nice restoration
is best effort: an observed intervening change suppresses restoration, but the
check and setter are not an atomic scheduler transaction. Root-owned writable
sysfs controls remain the authority for this optional policy. The separate
critical_task_priority control retains CAP_SYS_NICE, priority bounds 100..139,
and the later s_umount try-read exclusion against remount/thread teardown.
The GC task reference follows LTS's embedded gc_thread structure. Documentation
now states the implemented default of 120/nice 0 rather than ACK's incorrect 100.

Conflicts were resolved by preserving the LTS implementation and adding timing:

- Keep loff_t destination size/end variables, EOF validation and error exits.
- Keep the reverted cp_enable_rwsem absent. Do not restore its old lock ordering.
- Keep per-superblock cp_global_sem lockdep keys and their registration/cleanup.
- Keep f2fs_gc_range's optional per-section lock, six-argument API, pinning sync
  and retry behavior. Instrument inside that scope, rather than restoring the
  older outer GC lock and reversing later deadlock/ordering fixes.
- Keep native recovery-list initialization, node_write/NAT lock order and
  size-aware sysfs field access. No historical structure layout is preserved.

Initialize a missing delay-accounting sample explicitly to zero. The original
ACK helper leaves it uninitialized when current->delays is NULL. SDK automatic
stack initialization mitigates automatic objects, but explicit initialization
also covers reused/poisoned context storage. Timing remains diagnostic sampling,
not exact scheduler accounting under dynamic accounting changes. Fault-timeout
injection is disabled by the actual SDK config and is not imported with this
series; its independent candidate review remains separate.

check_f2fs_lock_trace.py passes 78 extracted-function checks: absent delay state,
all seven lock names, read/write policy, failed/successful trylocks, RT/higher
priority bypass, nesting and observed external nice changes. A poisoned-state
control using ACK's original helper fails. Eleven complete caller files equal
the baseline after removing only context declarations/arguments, normalizing
wrapper/init names, and removing the separately reviewed GC-thread nice setter.
This comparison preserves the actual underlying control flow and data handling;
it does not prove live lock balance or scheduler behavior. No F2FS filesystem,
real tracing callback, Android service or runtime fault injection was executed.

Rechecking 705b9a3653c4 found that the earlier broad tracing disposition did not
establish its code effect: LTS still used direct i_size reads in four events.
d20875dda4d4 applies the actual i_size_read patch. Besides the original 32-bit
tearing rationale, the current native 64-bit helper uses smp_load_acquire paired
with i_size_write, so use the native accessor consistently. No event layout or
userspace authority changes. This corrects the earlier review explicitly.

The device-alias question has also been corrected: LTS already supports static
aliases. The unresolved ACK extension is dynamic reserve/release/status and its
mount/rename cleanup, not the existence of static alias support. Local vold's
formatting support does not by itself resolve that dynamic feature's necessity.

100 common modules and 45 virtual-device modules. All 145 signatures and module
flag/CRC tables verified; 659 protected exports matched and the altered-payload
control was rejected. This includes zram compat dispatch; subsequent F2FS source

Build 65 completed at 0e583bb7ddb4, manifest 74a43a0, with both kernel images,

## F2FS immutable large-folio reads (ce7f50c5967d)

The SDK configuration includes F2FS and its IOSTAT interface. The supplied data
fstab uses ext4; actual use of the optional F2FS large-folio path has not been
established. Retain this generic ACK feature conservatively for Android source,
configuration and runtime compatibility, without enabling another platform.
Eligibility remains immutable, non-compressed regular files excluding quota
inodes. Write opens and mmap write faults are rejected for a large-folio-capable
mapping, and native LTS setattr-size rejection remains. Clearing immutable does
not clear that capability; write access requires inode eviction as documented
by ACK. The withdrawn inode-number/user.fadvise registration is not restored.

The original ACK completion decrements pending subpages and uses the status of
the last completing bio for the entire folio. A prior I/O, decryption or verity
failure can therefore be lost if the final bio succeeds. The port accumulates
failure under the folio-state lock. A submission reference holds the folio locked
until all mapping and hole verification have finished; mapping failures are
recorded before this reference is dropped. This also avoids publishing a valid
folio and then relocking/clearing it on the old error path. A pending bio is
submitted exactly once, including when a later readahead folio fails before
insertion. Folio membership is reset on every iteration, and all-hole folios
complete. These changes are security relevant because invalid or unverified data
must never be marked uptodate merely because another portion read successfully.

Native LTS fscrypt and fsverity APIs remain. Both bio consumers iterate folio
length/offset; holes call native fsverity_verify_blocks with their actual offset.
Native node-footer validation remains before completion. Large-file eligibility
excludes compression, and only compressed callers enable STEP_DECOMPRESS; the
new private-state pointer is not passed to the compressed-page magic checker.
GC's existing order-0 data path invalidates large folios and retries. Both size
and actual starting index are captured while locked and referenced, before the
put; using mapping_align_index with minimum order zero could start inside the
folio. Native single-page EOF checks precede the new consecutive-hole hint.

The selected trace/iostat interface uses the final ACK 16-bucket format, with
zeroed unused buckets and a compile-time capacity check. The SDK's 4-KiB kernel
does not exhibit the original ARM larger-base-page truncation; preserving the
current diagnostic format justifies selection here, not future-platform support.
No historical struct layout or binary KMI is retained.

`check_f2fs_largefolio.py` extracts the actual reader and completion functions.
9,406 deterministic host cases and 161,821 assertions pass with undefined-behavior
sanitization: mixed data/holes, invalid blocks, mapping/hole-verification errors,
I/O failure positions, immediate/forward/reverse completion, bio-capacity retry,
state reuse, mixed order-0 readahead, prior-bio flushing, and unsupported input.
A separate partial-error control passes with the port and fails with pinned ACK's
actual completion function. A stub also checks native node-footer error delivery.
These shims do not run kernel locking, real concurrency, storage, encryption or
verity algorithms. Compilation is recorded separately; no mounted filesystem or
runtime compatibility is claimed. Local host C headers under `/usr/include`,
including errno definitions, are used only for the host harness. All inspected
sources were local; commit-message URLs were not accessed.

## F2FS fault injection target boundary

The built SDK config has `CONFIG_F2FS_FAULT_INJECTION` unset. Native f2fs.h
therefore makes `time_to_inject` return false; fault state and sysfs registration
are conditional on that configuration. The new atomic-delay rename, lock-delay
simulator/sysfs control and pointer fix, skip-write trigger, and filemap lookup
wrapper additions cannot affect this build. Their individual final dispositions
record the complete patches and affected gates. Native fault-injection source is
left in place; this is not a claim that no debug consumer exists.

The helper-only killable timeout refactor/deadline change has only a native
atomic-injection caller, also compiled inactive. The ordinary congestion-timeout
change is separate and remains under review. Likewise, f2fs_fault_report emits
real error diagnostics independent of fault injection and is not covered by this
omission. Classification follows code reachability, not the word “fault”.

Native `block/blk-lib.c::__blkdev_issue_discard` unconditionally returns zero.
Retaining native F2FS return-value/error checks instead of ACK's cleanup and
FAULT_DISCARD deletion has the same ordinary discard outcome in the SDK. No
actual block error is suppressed by this decision; completion errors remain
handled by native bio completion. All evidence is local.

## F2FS diagnostics, retry policy and recovered names (0911085e16d9, 4a7c4f4e132f)

Retain generic fadvise and real error-report trace interfaces. NEED_FSCK reports
preserve caller function/line; an inline helper evaluates flag arguments once.
The checkpoint-stop event is added at the native checkpoint.c location. Native
flush, persisted stop reason and critical-error behavior are unchanged. These
reporting events are independent of the omitted fault-injection simulator.
Defragmentation reports successful moved blocks through read-only sysfs/debug
output. An initialized atomic counter prevents lost concurrent updates and keeps
ACK's unsigned 32-bit modulo range; it is diagnostic, not an allocation policy.

ACK's congestion retry policy now sleeps one jiffy, or 4 ms at the SDK's HZ=250,
while periodic discard submission remains 20 ms. This changes responsiveness and
wake frequency; no performance improvement has been measured. Native retry
conditions and scheduling calls remain. Individual last_time and NAT heuristic
samples receive READ_ONCE/WRITE_ONCE/data_race annotations. These do not provide
a cross-field snapshot or replace metadata locking. Documentation now correctly
states the already-existing rt,3 checkpoint I/O priority.

Native LTS already bounds recovery filename logs and skips node-footer validation
after a failed read. Keep those implementations. ACK dae54701fcc8 additionally
rejects zero-length recovered names and reports malformed lengths as filesystem
corruption; pinned LTS lacks that validation. Apply it in 4a7c4f4e132f before any
hashing or dentry reconstruction, preserving native encrypted/casefolded bounds
checks, recovery ordering and bounded diagnostics. No filesystem image was mounted
and no runtime test was performed. Evidence consists of full local patches,
source review and the separately recorded build; no new remote sources were used.

## F2FS verity API reconciliation

The SDK enables FS_VERITY and F2FS compression. The extra IS_ENABLED guard in
25111fb0cb80 is true for this target and does not alter its behavior. Retain the
native LTS info lookup and verification interfaces instead of 5117198dee04's
cross-function cached-pointer transport refactor. The native acquire lookup,
metadata eligibility, decompression need_verity selection, and context release
before recursive verity work remain. Six complete functions and both native core
verity files match pinned LTS, recorded in `evidence/f2fs-verity-native-comparison.json`.

Compressed mappings are excluded from large-folio eligibility. Native page-sized
verification, uptodate and unlock helpers therefore suffice for compressed
rpages; retaining the explicit failure clear from LTS is harmless and does not
remove validation. This accounts for 6b162dff9448 and 70bbe16075d3 without enabling
unsupported compressed large-folio behavior. In contrast, c9a415d6a8ef's hole
subpage-offset correction is required by the newly selected noncompressed large
folios and is adapted in ce7f50c5967d. Their bios use native length/offset-aware
verification, and all failures feed the accumulated completion status. This
review does not claim runtime or cryptographic validation, and uses no remote
resources.

## F2FS native lifetime and helper preservation

Native superblock cleanup clears s_fs_info immediately after freeing sbi, and
native drop_inode releases its temporary inode reference with atomic_dec under
i_lock, avoiding iput recursion. The ACK fixes 3f3b4e256b70 and 749c4bff73d5 are
already represented. Native decompression replaces the old 1f7ba8693d16 counter
scheme with one remaining-pages countdown, a separate context reference count,
and per-cluster verity work. The read counter is never reset for verification;
completion and each bio page hold distinct lifetime references. Retain this
newer design and its failure cleanup rather than restore the old per-bio counter.

The private unlock-parameter bool cleanup and F2FS_SUPER_BLOCK page-count spelling
have no required runtime effect here: callers pass the same truth values and
block-device pagecache folio orders are bounded (SDK maximum order 9), so the
native divisor is the same positive page count. These are per-patch source
findings, not blanket approval of the remaining F2FS delta. No runtime tests or
remote resources were used for these decisions.

## F2FS checkpoint-enable retry policy

Local `grapheneos-17/system/vold/Checkpoint.cpp:314` explicitly remounts F2FS
with `checkpoint=enable` when committing filesystem checkpoints. Retain ACK's
full-inode synchronous writeback and skipped-write retry accounting for this
Android runtime interface. The SDK enables F2FS, although actual SDK use of this
mount operation remains unverified; this is conservative retention of generic
configured behavior, not a claim about the emulator's mounted filesystems.

The adapted loop performs at most four full sync passes (one initial pass plus
three retries), stopping once dirty data is cleared or no new skipped writes
were recorded. Concurrent new dirties alone do not force more passes. This
replaces native LTS's chunked writeback/time check followed by a full sync. The
native 16-second check was not a hard wall-clock bound: writeback and the final
sync could block. The new pass bound is likewise not a time bound. It preserves
the purpose of avoiding an indefinite attempt to catch continuous new dirties,
while intentionally selecting ACK's Android checkpoint retry policy. Remaining
dirty/skipped pages are warned about; neither this code nor native LTS treats
that observation alone as a fatal error. Do not infer a persistence guarantee.
Native gc_lock ordering, checkpoint state transition, sync_fs error propagation
and checkpoint-thread flush are preserved. More full passes can increase latency;
no runtime latency or durability measurement is claimed.

Skipped-page accounting is active only during checkpoint enable and synchronous
writeback. It is an atomic heuristic count, not a census of distinct pages. A
vendor callback can bypass native writeback's internal retries: the adaptation
therefore counts its reported skipped pages even on success, and initializes
its result before invoking the hook. Native error returns are unchanged. The
withdrawn cp_enable_rwsem series stays absent: the final ACK revert documents
a folio-lock/checkpoint-lock inversion, and both pinned final trees omit it.
The separate disable-checkpoint changes add diagnostics without changing its
GC, error, lock or persistence control flow.

`check_f2fs_checkpoint.py` extracts the actual three relevant functions and
checks 38 deterministic cases / 338 assertions with host C shims and undefined-
behavior sanitization. It covers the pass bound, dirty/skipped policy, callback
and native errors, cleanup, and propagated sync failure. A negative control
omitting callback skipped-write accounting fails seven assertions. These tests
do not exercise kernel concurrency, locks, actual storage or remount latency.
The existing large-folio extracted-function checks still pass after the data.c
change. Inputs are local kernel/userspace sources and host C headers; no remote
resources were used.

## F2FS inode validation and write-range compatibility

Retain ACK's directory single-link rejection in inode loading and unlink before
the directory's two decrements, and quota-inode NAT diagnostics for reserved or
null block addresses. The latter marks NEED_FSCK and records the error; it does
not itself return a new error. Existing generic NAT validation and its fatal
EFSCORRUPTED result remain intact. The unlink label conflicts are resolved by
keeping the port's sync_fs error assignment and traced lock contexts while
adding the corruption cleanup path. No blanket ACK conflict choice was used.

Pinned-file overwrite validation and forced-buffered O_DIRECT cleanup now use
the position/count after f2fs_write_checks adjusts append and maximum sizes.
The original request remains available for tracing. This avoids validating or
invalidating the wrong file range. Keep native write errors, inode locking,
preallocation cleanup and sync ordering. FOP_DONTCACHE enables native generic
uncached reads; f2fs_file_write_iter explicitly rejects IOCB_DONTCACHE writes.
The local bionic UAPI exposes RWF_DONTCACHE; actual SDK invocation is unverified,
so this is conservative retention of the configured filesystem's public API.

ACK's unsigned journal-size parameter prevents signed narrowing of unsigned
entry counts before capacity comparisons. Its 32-bit max_io_bytes and bounded
seq_file_ra_mul types follow final ACK's control representation. The native
size-aware sysfs accessor writes the matching field width; seq_file_ra_mul
already accepts only 2..256. As in ACK, generic stores cast larger max_io_bytes
inputs to the field width: this is an I/O aggregation hint, not a block-address
validation boundary. No historical binary layout is preserved.

Separately reviewed helper-only changes retain native list initialization,
unused private parameters and constant-valued META arguments. Native quota
loading retains duplicate checks rather than removing them. The native fscrypt
mempool/on-stack request design makes the removed bounce-allocation ENOMEM path
unnecessary, but its defensive retry code remains. Native LTS already selects
the original inode's crypto context for COW data reads and migrates unaligned
swap extents before activation. Each decision identifies its actual functions
and callers. These are source-review findings; successful build results are
recorded by exact commit separately, and no runtime or mounted-filesystem test
