is claimed. All sources are local; no remote resources were accessed.

## F2FS GC freezer cleanup and critical-error handling

Retain ACK's GC/discard freezer checks, which let background work return toward
the existing freeze points without starting further migration/discard work.
Native lock release, bio submission, plug completion and error handling remain.
The GC inner-loop early exit needs an adaptation: do_garbage_collect first takes
one extra reference on every summary folio, then gets a second reference as each
is processed. ACK's early goto releases the current pair only, leaving later
prefetched references unreleased. The port instead skips remaining migration
and walks the outer loop to release each pair. This avoids accumulating pinned
folios on repeated freeze requests; no kernel runtime leak test is claimed.

`check_f2fs_gc_freezer.py` extracts the entire current GC function and exercises
972 deterministic host cases / 89,508 assertions: freeze positions, both data
and node GC, foreground/background paths, summary block geometries, prefetch
failures and non-uptodate summaries. All references, plugs and submissions are
checked. A control restoring ACK's early-exit behavior fails, including explicit
remaining-reference checks. The harness does not run actual reclamation,
locking, concurrency, storage or suspend. Host C headers/compiler are local.

Repeated page read errors now select the existing META/NODE/DATA stop-reason
values and call the native checkpoint-stop path, rather than setting CP_ERROR
alone. Corrupt node-footer writes use that path too. It retains native async
error persistence, mount-error policy, reboot handling and the port's report
trace; function relocation/static cleanup is omitted. Existing enum values fit
the native 32-entry reason array. Fault injection remains disabled in the SDK.

Unmount synchronizes then invalidates the primary and additional block-device
pagecaches while the device references remain owned by the superblock. This
prevents stale raw metadata reads after unmount; it adds no new access to those
devices. Error diagnostics include checkpoint status. Extension updates reject
an extension already in the opposite hot/cold list under the existing sb_lock,
retaining native length/count validation and superblock commit rollback.

The historical NAT marking/locking, GC cached-bio deadlock and dropbehind-revert
patches are already represented by the named LTS fixes in their individual
ledger entries. Their safe ordering is retained with the port's trace wrappers.
Native per-superblock lockdep-key lifetime is likewise preserved. No new remote
sources were accessed, and compilation artifacts remain the only platform-level
verification.

## F2FS direct-I/O statistics and checkpoint diagnostics

Retain ACK's direct-I/O latency accounting for the configured F2FS_IOSTAT path.
The completion wrapper saves iomap's private pointer before releasing the
statistics context and restores it before native iomap completion, for reads
and writes. The existing statistics helper otherwise restores the superblock
pointer on writes, which is not an iomap context. The typed post_read_ctx member
transports this opaque pointer without dereferencing it. Native iomap sets both
bi_private and its completion before either filesystem submit callback, including
zero-tail bios. Native encryption, error status, write hints and completion
reference handling remain. The new wrapper is installed only when statistics
are enabled at submission; disabling them in flight cannot skip context release.
The existing trusted vendor hook can suppress latency accounting but cannot skip
the wrapper's cleanup. This does not grant userspace a new access capability.

The extracted-function host check covers 64 cases with 912 assertions: reads,
synchronous/asynchronous writes, success/error status, hook suppression and all
enable-at-submission/completion combinations. Controls removing iomap pointer
restoration or allocating when disabled fail. It uses shims for the mempool,
latency update and native completion; no actual I/O, allocation pressure or
concurrency is tested. The SDK filesystem remains ext4; optional F2FS runtime
use is unconfirmed and these generic interfaces are retained conservatively.

Checkpoint timing separates merged I/O, NAT and SIT phases; debugfs reports each
current segment's block offset while retaining the earlier atomic defrag counter.
NAT flushing prefetches pages that cannot fit in the journal, under the native
nat_tree_lock. The existing flush function still decides placement, validates
metadata and handles errors; prefetch does not replace those checks. No block
layout, permissions or durability ordering changes are introduced. All evidence
is local, and build verification is recorded separately by source commit.

## F2FS writeback allocation, flush completion and write streams

Use the mapping's GFP mask for write-begin folios, retaining the final ACK
FGP flags and native __filemap_get_folio call. The omitted fault-injection
wrapper remains unnecessary with F2FS_FAULT_INJECTION disabled. This permits
movable/CMA allocation on the configured x86_64 target too; the original patch's
32-bit motivation does not establish an architecture-only boundary. At this
allocation point the current data folio and F2FS operation/node locks have not
been acquired. The write path owns an inode reference and inode lock, and the
native reclaim/writeback paths do not acquire that inode lock. The quota read
uses GFP_KERNEL: native dquot_acquire/commit/release bracket their dq_lock-held
operations with memalloc_nofs_save/restore, preserving the documented recursion
protection. Allocation pressure and actual reclaim concurrency are not tested.

Pinned-file writes omit the global ordering mutex because native pin checks
require preexisting mappings and force in-place writes; ordinary/compressed
write ordering is unchanged. Buffered overwrite can use a read-extent-cache hit
before inode-folio lookup only for non-inline, non-compressed data whose page
starts below EOF. Native subsequent block validation and write-end size handling
remain. Folio writeback waits stop scanning temperatures after submitting the
matching bio, then retain IPU submission and native ordered/stable waits. The
force-argument cleanup accompanies that helper; other callers retain full scans.

Multi-device fsync submits all dirty devices before waiting for each result,
returns the first error and releases all bios even after failure. The device
count is bounded by MAX_DEVICES=8 in native mount scanning; the small array uses
GFP_NOFS|__GFP_NOFAIL as ACK. This can wait under memory pressure and is not a
wall-clock guarantee. Single-device flush and checkpoint-cache flush paths stay
native. Extracted checks cover all dirty/error masks for 2..8 devices, inline
and deferred completion, successful-flush accounting and bio release; a control
breaking the wait loop after error is rejected. No actual storage durability
or failure injection is claimed.

Generic F2FS stream selection and explicit direct-I/O stream validation use the
existing native block limits and kiocb field. No new driver/platform is enabled.
Virtio-blk initializes no write streams and partition devices report zero, so
normal SDK I/O keeps stream zero and unsupported nonzero direct-I/O requests are
rejected. NVMe is also configured; its actual SDK use is unconfirmed. Retaining
the generic configured API conservatively avoids silently discarding requests
and preserves source/runtime compatibility without claiming an established FDP
consumer. Every device must support an explicit stream; automatic placement
uses each bio's target device. Native write hints and the statistics wrapper
remain. Host flush/stream checks pass 88,729 cases / 2,535,370 assertions;
statistics ownership checks also still pass. All sources are local.

## F2FS optional deferred write completion

Retain the max_atc_write_bio_size sysfs interface with ACK's UINT_MAX default,
which leaves completion synchronous. A smaller threshold defers large write
bios arriving in atomic context. Bio front padding owns the work item until
the native completion body releases the bio. The only allocator installing
f2fs_write_end_io uses this bioset; direct-I/O completion uses its separate
iomap callback and does not interpret the padding. Statistics are unbound
exactly once before dispatch. The complete native write-completion body,
including node-footer validation, checkpoint errors, encryption/compression
cleanup and page-count-before-end-writeback ordering, is preserved verbatim
after extracting the wrapper.

Adapt ACK's shared read/write workqueue into separate read and write queues.
The write queue has WQ_MEM_RECLAIM and its own execution context because reclaim
may wait for write completion, while post-read decompression can allocate memory.
The local Documentation/core-api/workqueue.rst requires reserved execution for
reclaim-dependent work and separate queues for dependencies. This avoids adding
a dependency on a potentially memory-blocked read worker; it is a source-derived
forward-progress precaution, not a reproduced kernel deadlock. Both allocations
and unwind paths are handled, and superblock cleanup drains/destroys the queues
before releasing their owner. No new permissions or historical ABI are added.

The extracted host check passes 103 cases / 1,082 assertions covering allocation
failure at either queue, native-body identity, atomic/process contexts, thresholds
including zero and UINT_MAX, completion errors and single cleanup. A control
routing completion onto the shared queue fails the separation/reserve checks.
It does not model actual worker scheduling, reclaim or device I/O. Optional SDK
runtime use is unconfirmed; the configured generic Android interface is retained
conservatively. Sources and documentation were local; no remote was accessed.

## F2FS free-space and device geometry

Retain the ACK free-section estimator, with an argument-order correction.
get_additional_blocks_required declares node/data/dentry output arguments, but
ACK calls it with node/dentry/data. That subtracts spare capacity from the wrong
class, including subtracting the unused dentry capacity from combined data under
active_logs=2/6. The port passes the matching classes. Native reserved-section
accounting and mount/segment geometry validation remain. Missing current segments
leave full demand outstanding; the new estimator handles exact capacity and
combines directory/data demand according to active_logs. It is a worst-case
space estimate, not a proof that arbitrary concurrent allocations will succeed.

Trim minimum granularity includes every ordinary device. ACK's loop condition
stops at the first zoned device, so the port skips each zoned device and continues
through the remaining bounded list. Native privilege, mount-write, range and
corruption checks remain. The existing primary-device granularity and minlen
conversion are retained. Zone-count rounding uses bdev_is_zone_start rather than
a power-of-two mask, preserving ordinary power-of-two results while handling the
configured generic block API's other supported geometries. No device is added.
The configured zoned filesystem interface retains max_open_zones reporting and
the one-time GC default ratio of 80; SDK use of zoned storage is unconfirmed.

GC now selects the representative type from the first segment actually migrated,
instead of an empty leading segment, and keeps SSA/SIT consistency checks for
subsequent segments. Checkpoint-disabled victim selection skips empty sections
before age calculations. Readahead phases finish/restart their plug to dispatch
pending reads. The previously adapted freezer path still drains every prefetched
summary reference, and native six-argument gc_range locking and resize free-space
corrections remain intact.

Extracted space/trim calculations pass 263,934 host cases, including differing
class capacities, active_logs modes, missing current segments and mixed device
orders. Controls restoring ACK's swapped arguments or stop-at-zoned loop fail.
The 972 GC freezer/refcount cases still pass after adapting only the helper shim
signatures; that harness does not execute the internal migration phases. These
are source/control-flow checks, with no filesystem mount, concurrency, device
discard or allocation test. All evidence is local.

## F2FS eviction record publication and bitmap storage

Retain the configured filesystem's generic ACK inode-record optimization as a
coherent adaptation of 5d30bda7072a, 3ed358df1ef6, 92d46ac21eb2 and c9b33047f288.
Its optional SDK runtime use is unconfirmed (the supplied data fstab uses ext4);
retention preserves Android configuration and filesystem runtime behavior.
The eviction split preserves native crypto/verity cleanup, corrupt-inode error
handling, freeze exclusion, quota repair marking and device-alias extent lifetime.
No historical structure layout or binary KMI is preserved.

Async work owns only the superblock, inode number and record bits, never the freed
inode. Preserve BOTH append and update bits with OR: ACK's successive assignments
lose append when both flags are set. A dedicated WQ_MEM_RECLAIM eviction queue,
separate from read processing and write completion, provides a reserved worker
for the reclaim path. The eight-item mempool and synchronous kswapd recording
are retained. Queue creation unwinds all preceding allocations; filesystem exit
unregisters before freeing the pool. Mount failure and unmount release records
before destroying queues and superblock state. This does not establish a bounded
latency or prove memory-pressure forward progress under every workload.

The native VFS find_inode_fast/iget_locked waits for I_FREEING to finish; evict
calls the filesystem eviction method before removing the old inode from the
hash. Flush already queued records before publishing EVERY newly loaded ordinary
inode, extending ACK's directory-only barrier to regular and other inodes. This
prevents a prior incarnation's queued work from arriving after a later fsync or
deletion removes its records. Internal mount inodes bypass this wait because
metadata inode creation precedes queue initialization. Deletion itself does not
flush the queue, avoiding an extra reclaim-path wait. APPEND/UPDATE queries also
flush outside the record spinlock. Checkpoint/unmount record release flushes once
BEFORE clearing bitmaps; ACK's converted loop invokes the helper only for
ORPHAN/FLUSH, where it does nothing. Concurrent later eviction retains the native
checkpoint exclusion rules; the flush is not a ban on all future record work.

Only membership-only records become radix-tree value bitmaps. Orphan and
multi-device-flush records keep their native lists and payloads. Count each newly
set bit and each actually cleared bit, and reset counts on destruction. ACK's
bitmap conversion left these counts zero, disabling the native INO_ENTRIES
pressure estimate and debug accounting. The retained estimate multiplies record
count by the old entry size, so it conservatively overestimates packed storage
rather than claiming exact allocated bytes. No on-disk format changes.

Extracted host checks execute record submission, publication, lookup, removal,
bitmap boundaries and clearing: 436 cases / 21,514 assertions pass. Controls
reintroducing lost append flags, absent publication waits and uncounted bitmaps
fail. Queue/completion checks pass 104 cases / 1,096 assertions, cover failure at
all three queue allocations and confirm native completion-body identity. These
use modeled trees/workqueues and do not execute kernel concurrency, reclaim,
fsync, storage recovery or a mounted filesystem. All evidence is local, including
Documentation/core-api/workqueue.rst; no additional remote source was accessed.

## F2FS symlink creation failure ownership

Adapt dabf5c69c7f3 so encryption/page-symlink failures remove the directory entry
before instantiating the new dentry, then retire the new inode with the orphan
reservation already established by deletion. Other failed-inode callers still
acquire their own orphan reservation. This avoids publishing an already unlinked
inode and double-accounting its orphan slot. The native failed-inode cleanup,
quota/error checks, corrupt link-count checks and filesystem-operation exclusion
remain. New inode insertion sets I_CREATING; native find_inode_fast rejects it,
so GC's f2fs_iget does not wait on this creator's I_NEW while rollback balances.

Once the target is written and the dentry instantiated, later writeback or
DIRSYNC failure is reported without undoing the created link. Unlike ACK's
separate ret variable, the port preserves native writeback error propagation;
it does not report a failed flush as success. A missing rollback directory entry
returns ENOENT rather than pretending an orphan reservation was obtained. This
also makes ordinary unlink report ENOENT for that inconsistent/missing-entry
case instead of its old success result. If rollback itself fails (for example
checkpoint error, I/O failure or exhausted orphan slots), the ACK fallback can
still publish a broken symlink while returning the original creation error.
That residual failure behavior is explicit, not a successful-rollback claim.

The conflict context's ban on unlinking device-alias files is NOT imported.
Pinned GrapheneOS system/fs/fs_mgr/userdata_alias_remove.cpp concretely unlinks
/data/<alias>; native LTS static alias teardown remains compatible. Retain the
original inode pointer for unlink tracing and the existing casefold invalidation
outside the operation lock. No encryption format or authorization change.

Extracted symlink, unlink and failed-inode control flow passes 630 modeled
failure/flag cases and 5,082 assertions. Negative controls for lost writeback
errors, duplicate orphan reservation and missing-entry false success all fail.
The shims do not execute actual encryption, quota, I/O, GC or crash recovery.
All source evidence is local, including fs/crypto/hooks.c, fs/inode.c and the
GrapheneOS consumer; no additional remote source was accessed.

The full LTS 95d5975c9d1c (symlink writeback errors) patch was also examined.
Its error return and DIRSYNC-on-success condition are preserved. Its attempt to
unlink after a flush error is deliberately replaced by ACK's creation-versus-
persistence boundary: that inode is already published, so error rollback must
not leave a hashed dentry pointing at an unlinked inode. The consequence is that
a failed persistence operation may leave a visible, potentially non-durable or
broken symlink; callers still receive the error. This is an explicit failure-
semantics tradeoff, not an assertion that durability improved. Native encryption,
block initialization and read error handling are unchanged; no new access right
is granted. The earlier payload-failure path instead unlinks before publication.

## F2FS dynamic device aliasing with static unlink compatibility

Adapt 8b4be13c97ad and the rename cleanup in 0b3c285a9221. Retain the generic
CAP_SYS_ADMIN reserve/release ioctls, status query and compat dispatch for the
configured filesystem. These operate on preformatted alias control files; they
do not enable a new device/platform or invent a format. Native LTS already has
static device aliases. GrapheneOS vold/fs_mgr and f2fs-tools provide positive
static alias evidence; searches of local system and f2fs-tools do not establish
a dynamic-ioctl consumer. Dynamic runtime use remains unconfirmed and is retained
conservatively for Android filesystem/API compatibility. The SDK fstab is ext4.

Preserve the actual fs_mgr userdata_alias_remove.cpp unlink route, which ACK's
blanket alias-unlink ban would break. Track the restored control inode number
instead of only a has_alias bit. Last-link deletion clears that device's pinning
restriction; existing allocated blocks still remain unavailable until native
inode truncation frees them. A reserve ioctl on an unlinked inode is rejected,
so an old descriptor cannot recreate an unregistered alias after deletion.
The dynamic matcher requires the registered inode and device basename, validates
nonzero 32-bit length, exact device bounds, non-zoned storage and section
alignment. Reserve/release require a regular alias inode. ACK's hardlink, rename,
replacement and flag/size-change restrictions remain for control-file identity;
rename replacement uses out_dir cleanup rather than leaking looked-up folios.
These restrictions affect alias files only. Status reads take the inode shared
lock. No binary KMI layout is retained.

Restore alias identities after recovery and before initializing in-memory pinned
segments. Lookup/read failures abort through existing mount cleanup rather than
silently permitting pinning into an unrecognized alias device. Invalid alias
inode type/link state is rejected. Source review covers the corresponding mount
failure labels, which precede starting GC and joining the shrinker.

Reserve holds native gc_lock while reading device geometry, checking capacity,
resetting victims, moving ALL NR_CURSEG_TYPE logs and performing range GC with
its native sixth lock argument false. The entire existing free_segment_range and
f2fs_gc_range functions are unchanged by this feature, including freed-section
accounting, all-curseg handling and native lock/unpin behavior. The reserve SIT
walk calls the unchanged native update_sit_entry once per block, preserving its
bitmap/accounting checks instead of importing ACK's bulk-allocation rewrite.
Zero-length invalidation is harmless, and alias truncation locks/snapshots and
clears the extent tree so repeated release cannot double-decrement blocks.

Use 64-bit capacity addition and saturating subtraction for reserved capacity;
concurrent reserved_blocks changes must not turn insufficient space into a huge
unsigned free count. The free-block predicate likewise avoids subtraction wrap.
Pending alias reservation is transferred to valid-block accounting only on
successful quota/allocation checks and returned on every earlier failure.
Reservation flags are published under the segment-map lock with explicit single
accesses for lockless readers. Both ioctls retain the mount write reference
through the final checkpoint, preventing filesystem freeze from overtaking that
persistence step. A checkpoint failure is returned with the already changed
in-memory alias state; no atomic crash-rollback guarantee is claimed. Privileged
users of the raw device must still coordinate release with their external use,
as with static alias ownership.

The segment selector skips reserving devices and keeps pinned allocations off
registered alias devices. Fix ACK's premature exhaustion after wrap: the fallback
pass stops preferring a different zone, so it cannot skip the last usable zone
and falsely return ENOSPC. Existing native zone/hint behavior remains: a hint can
override ONLY_SEQ preference, and zone separation can ask a pinned caller to
retry even with a conventional section available. Both limits were reproduced
against the pre-alias selector; they are not asserted to be new port guarantees.

Extracted reservation/accounting/geometry tests pass 16,442 cases / 17,552
assertions, with controls rejecting capacity wrap, persistent-curseg-only
relocation and dropping mount-write protection before checkpoint. Full-function
identity is checked against pre-alias 334f9060783c for SIT allocation/update and
resize/range-GC. Segment selection passes 98,306 cases / 692,645 assertions;
controls ignoring device reservations or restoring premature exhaustion fail.
The extended symlink/unlink test passes 630 cases / 5,114 assertions, including
clearing only the deleted alias identity. These use modeled maps, locks, quota,
GC and storage; actual concurrency, multi-device operation, crash recovery and
SDK runtime use have not been executed. All sources are local; no additional
remote resource was accessed.

Follow-up alias bounds review: f2fs_scan_devices returns without allocating a
device table for ordinary single-device mounts, independently of the on-disk
alias feature bit. Guard both allocation predicates against an absent/out-of-range
device index before dereferencing FDEV, including malformed or feature-only
single-device filesystems. The placement check now passes 98,310 cases / 692,653
assertions and additionally rejects a missing-table-guard control. This extends
the prior two-device model with absent-table queries; it is not a mounted-image
test. The capacity/reservation and symlink source functions are unchanged.

## F2FS resizable tail and pinned allocation bounds

Retain 65020e790253's optional resizable_tail_secno mount option, default zero,
and read-only pinned_area_max_secno report for the configured generic filesystem.
This supports its existing pinned-file/swap and resize APIs; no additional device
platform is enabled. Actual SDK use is unconfirmed and conservatively retained.
Native LTS resize validation, freed-section bookkeeping and six-argument range-GC
locking remain. The documentation conflict keeps the existing lookup-mode table
and its closing border; the tail option is a separate row.

Publish the boundary after mount setup, after successful remount and after native
resize metadata updates. Remount publication takes pin_sem: both pinned-section
allocation consumers (fallocate expansion and swap migration) hold this semaphore
through allocation/use. Failed remount retains the previously published limit.
Resize freezes the filesystem before committing its metadata; refreshing the
boundary then prevents a cached pre-resize value from permitting tail allocations.
A reader also clamps the published limit to the current MAIN_SECS, including the
native temporary reduced geometry during resize. Tail subtraction saturates at
zero if later shrink makes the old tail reservation larger than the new size.

Reject new pinned allocation with ENOSPC when the effective area is zero, before
forming a GC upper bound: zero * segments_per_section - 1 must not underflow to
a filesystem-wide range. The bounded GC retry keeps native lock=true, one retry
and subsequent sync/error propagation. Check the limit before the within-section
fast path as well as the whole-section search. Do not reuse an empty cached
pinned section now outside the boundary; allocate a permitted section instead.
Existing pinned extents are not relocated by changing the option, and a new tail
reservation is not a guarantee that a later shrink can migrate those old extents.

Pinned search starts at zero within its effective bound and must not restart at
first_seq_zone_segno on an ONLY_SEQ-policy wrap. It retains the corrected fallback
zone search. This supersedes the previous alias-checkpoint limitation where a
pinned allocation could return EAGAIN despite an available conventional section:
the same modeled case now succeeds inside the allowed range. Existing non-pinned
allocation-hint versus ONLY_SEQ behavior remains unchanged.

Extracted boundary, retry and cached-section checks pass 6,428 cases / 31,424
assertions and reject tail underflow, zero-area GC and outside-area reuse controls.
The expanded selector check passes 1,081,353 cases / 7,692,149 assertions, including
stale boundaries and partial-segment paths; five regression controls fail.
Reservation checks still pass 16,442 cases / 17,552 assertions and verify the
preexisting SIT allocation/update and native resize/range-GC bodies remain intact.
Publication call positions are also checked in source. These checks model locks,
GC and storage; actual remount/resize, concurrency and crash behavior are not
verified. All evidence is local; no additional remote source was accessed.

## F2FS vendor callback review

Retain the source interfaces in 49f93b14577c, 4e1c4268094a, 2240301db3ad,
1444d806aafb, 431b548a65bd, 45c752087e24, 4f9acbadfd8f, 80732fccb2e9 and
96292eb66bda. Every original patch, declaration/export and current call site was
examined. F2FS and Android vendor hooks are enabled in the SDK build. No callback
registration was located in the supplied common-modules/external C/H sources;
that does not establish dispensability. Keep the generic customization API
conservatively. The SLC motivation of one patch does not limit its generic file
creation/bio callbacks to a physical device. No device driver is added here.

The checkpoint boost/restore pair now uses a referenced task snapshot, acquired
under cprc.stat_lock shared with publication/removal. A remount can stop the
thread during the request wait; reloading its pointer afterward could pass NULL
or a dead task to the restore callback. Publish only a successfully created and
configured task, never an ERR_PTR. Drop the reference after restoring priority.
Do not hold the spinlock across callbacks, completion waits or kthread_stop.
Keep the native early synchronous path when no thread exists, queued request
completion and the stop-time drain. The saved priority is initialized from the
snapshot; callbacks remain responsible for matched boost/restore semantics under
concurrent requesters and for any scheduler-policy-specific state.

The down-read callback must acquire the real rwsem before setting skip. It now
receives NULL for read_waiters when CONFIG_F2FS_UNFAIR_RWSEM is disabled, rather
than referring to a nonexistent field. The SDK enables that option, so its queue
argument is unchanged. In either mode, no callback preserves native acquisition.
An incorrect callback can violate locking; these are trusted kernel extensions,
not a userspace permission bypass or a verified third-party implementation.

Buffered-write and writeback replacement callbacks start with -EIO, so marking
an operation completed without supplying a result cannot silently report
success or expose uninitialized stack data. Native calls overwrite that default.
The native NOWAIT rejection, outer inode/write/verity checks, preallocation
cleanup, write sync, block plug, writeback accounting and mutex release remain.
A writeback replacement still records skipped pages even when it returns success,
preserving checkpoint retry accounting. GC bypasses occur before reference/folio
acquisition and retain native atomic-inode references, validity checks and error
cleanup in the non-bypass path. Replacing GC must preserve valid-block accounting
and progress; returning success alone does not evacuate data. Custom ioctl
callbacks inherit checkpoint error/readiness guards and VFS ioctl security checks,
but must implement command-specific capability, user-copy and mount-write rules.
Compression callbacks may veto only after native eligibility checks, and retain
i_sem release on failure. OEM inode storage remains source functionality; native
GFP_F2FS_ZERO allocation clears it on every allocation, so the redundant ACK
explicit memset is unnecessary. Historical layout compatibility is not retained.

The printk callback keeps 45c752's NULL-sbi check; its va_format points to a live
stack va_list only during the synchronous callback. Creation/open callbacks keep
native fscrypt/fsverity, link, quota and error paths. Bio flag callbacks see the
native folio directly and must preserve operation/crypto/integrity semantics;
readahead's rac can be NULL. Iostat callbacks precede submission and context
unbind; skipping latency accounting still restores bi_private and frees its
mempool object exactly once. No instrumentation pointer may be retained without
independent lifetime protection. These APIs expand trusted module authority as
requested; they do not validate arbitrary module policy.

Extracted hook checks pass 44 cases each with fair and unfair locking (208 and
210 assertions); eight controls reject false write success, duplicate acquisition,
missing task references and lost priority restoration. Checkpoint/writeback
checks pass 38 cases / 338 assertions with the accounting regression rejected.
Direct-I/O iostat ownership checks pass 64 cases / 912 assertions with two failure
controls rejected. These model locking, thread shutdown and I/O; they do not
verify real scheduling, concurrent remount or third-party callbacks. All reviewed
sources are local and no additional remote resource was accessed.

## Native Merkle page-reader error propagation

Adapt 36f66dd065e5's error handling into the native ext4 and F2FS readers. A
cache miss (-ENOENT) can initiate readahead and demand reading; an existing stale
folio is dropped before demand reading. Other lookup errors return unchanged
without issuing more I/O, including simulated allocation failure. Keep the native
per-filesystem metadata offset and the previously restored invalidate-lock
protection around page_cache_ra_unbounded. The selected reader still returns a
referenced page or an error; no hash checking, crypto policy or visibility check
is bypassed. This transfers the fix from ACK's common helper without requiring
that helper's refactoring. Extracted tests pass 160 cases / 1,520 assertions over
both filesystems, cache hit/miss/stale states, lookup/read failures and readahead
lengths. Controls reject retrying non-ENOENT errors and leaking a stale reference.
Actual storage I/O and memory pressure are not tested. All sources are local.

## Fs-verity diagnostics and retained inode representation

Retain 5ad4c759e693's five fs-verity diagnostic trace events, including their
call sites and maintainer entry. These instrument enable/tree construction and
hash verification without changing control flow. All modified implementation
files equal the prior source after removing only trace calls/includes and
whitespace; the event header matches the full reviewed ACK patch. Parameters
are initialized before calls, and dynamic digest arrays use the validated hash
algorithm's digest size. Events copy values while their inode/verity-info owners
are live. Trace output includes inode, sizes, offsets and hashes; access remains
subject to existing tracing permissions. No new userspace read interface or
permission exception is introduced. SDK consumption is unconfirmed, so retain
this generic configured diagnostic capability conservatively. Compilation is
recorded separately; actual trace collection is not verified.

The preceding representation decisions retain native per-filesystem info pointers,
zero initialization, release/acquire publication, fsverity_active's initialized-info
check, and cleanup. They retain the used native verification-context inode and
page wrapper needed by compressed F2FS consumers. These choices do not preserve
historical binary layout: they avoid internal storage/argument refactors that add
no target integrity operation. The cross-inode rhashtable-chain use-after-free
fixed by efe665b77332 cannot arise without that hash chain; its complete fix and
introducing patch were reviewed. This is not a blanket claim about every possible
verity lifetime issue. Earlier Merkle readahead is adapted separately below;
it does not depend on these representation changes.
No additional remote resources were used.

## Early Merkle-tree prefetch with native verification retained

Partially adapt 5c0658a3a258 for configured ext4 and F2FS. Their read_folio and
readahead methods start hash prefetch before data I/O submission, with the
mapping invalidate lock already held by the native filemap/readahead callers.
The new optional filesystem operation translates the Merkle-relative page index
to the existing per-filesystem metadata offset. The shared prefetch helper
issues readahead only for a missing or stale cache folio, drops any acquired
reference exactly once, and leaves other lookup failures for the demand reader.
The mandatory verification/read path still reports those errors; speculative
prefetch cannot mark data or hashes verified or suppress an integrity failure.

Use native fsverity_get_info's acquire load rather than raw pointer access.
Reject absent info, empty/negative file sizes, absent callbacks and zero-length
ranges. Bound prefetch to the data-covered tree: clamp the page count before
addition, reject indices at or beyond EOF, and clip partial-page data blocks.
These guards prevent unsigned wrap/underflow and recursive Merkle prefetch when
the same mapping is read beyond the file's visible size. Native validated tree
parameters and metadata offsets remain; no alternate tree ownership is added.

Retain the three-argument native Merkle reader and completion-time readahead
fallback, including the preceding non-ENOENT error fix. Existing verification
code is byte-identical to 6cd9fcdf600d after removing the new helper. Retain
read_metadata's native sequential prefetch capped by bdi io_pages instead of
the ACK whole-request prefetch and reader-signature reduction. This preserves
bounded metadata-ioctl I/O and minimizes changes to the LTS integrity path while
providing early prefetch on the target data path. Btrfs is disabled for the SDK
and keeps its native reader signature; no future-platform-only adaptation is
needed. Cached first pages can cause speculative prefetch to skip a range,
as in ACK; native demand reads and fallback continue to provide correctness.

Extracted 64-bit, 4 KiB-page tests pass 9,220 cases for SHA-256/SHA-512 geometry,
1/2/4 KiB blocks, partial/empty files, EOF, maximal index/count inputs, absent
info/callback and modeled cache/error/reference states. Four negative controls
reject zero-range underflow, unbounded ranges, reference leaks and retries of
other lookup errors. The check compares range endpoints against an independent
integer-division oracle and confirms existing verification code is unchanged.
These checks do not establish real concurrent I/O behavior or performance;
compilation is recorded separately. Only supplied local sources were used.

## Historical ext4 encryption/casefold merges

Fourteen historical ext4-only candidates are reconciled against the current
native implementation, including every unique resolution in six merges. The
evidence records complete original patch hashes and 24 exact native-function
comparisons. The stable-inode encryption feature and current fscrypt policy
constraints, encrypted direct-I/O alignment and DUN limiting, casefold hash
preparation, key-presence checks, generic name comparison and encrypted_casefold
sysfs advertisement are already represented. Keep the current implementations,
including native shortname snapshots during rename and key-add race guards.

The 5.9 merge introduced an inline-directory bounds error by selecting
bh->b_size instead of the actual search buffer size. Its follow-up correction is
present in LTS, along with later hash-tail, inline-header and readdir bounds
checks. Do not replay the historical merge regression. The old lblk argument
plumbing and private ext4 casefold callbacks were superseded; their removal does
not remove directory validation or encrypted filename matching. The unlink API
has also evolved to perform key setup before starting its transaction; retain
native lock, transaction and cleanup ordering. Preserve the newer encrypted
inode/DAX/inline-data setup and direct-I/O atomic mapping checks. The separate
casefold rmdir deadlock fix remains applied.

The supplied goldfish fstab selects ext4 encrypted userdata with fsverity, and
vold contains a concrete encrypted/casefolded directory preparation path. This
review does not infer dispensability from missing consumers. It preserves native
capabilities and does not test live encrypted filesystem behavior. Only local
sources were read; a URL embedded in a vold comment was not accessed.

## Fscrypt history and asynchronous encrypted zeroing

Fourteen additional fscrypt-only candidates, including three nonempty merges,
are reviewed in fscrypt-local-review.json. Native LTS already supplies complete
inode-crypto zeroization, inline-crypto capability selection and raw-key fallback,
precise DUN widths, wrapped-key setup and current data-unit-aware interfaces.
Keep the native bounded device arrays and per-mode key list rather than older
queue-reference, allocation and argument arrangements. The native key-claim list
fix is stable commit 9d1dc507b99c: its UID ownership, quota keys, mk_sem locking,
other-user claims, synchronous list cleanup and RCU master cleanup are retained.
Twenty-two relevant functions exactly match the pinned LTS implementation.
Android legacy wrapped-key flag/KDF compatibility and dm-default-key integration
are separate retained deltas; these historical decisions do not remove them.

The integrated b52f2a4f2d07/adcf6597b9c8 zeroout sequence is retained: physical
blocks are converted to 512-byte sectors once, each bio receives its encryption
context, and page batching stops at a DUN discontinuity. IV_INO_LBLK_32 inline
crypto remains excluded for sub-page filesystem blocks, preserving the older
a79db694f392 guard. The pending counter includes a producer reference until
submission ends and one reference per bio before submit. Immediate completions
therefore cannot release the on-stack completion state during submission.
The caller waits for all submitted I/O; cmpxchg records a nonzero completion
error without allowing later success to erase it, and every bio is released.

Compared with LTS's serial submit_bio_wait loop, the retained ACK implementation
permits multiple requests in flight and can finish submitting the range before
an error is reported. It still returns failure if any request failed and never
claims that unwritten blocks were zeroed. This increases possible concurrent I/O
and allocation pressure; actual latency and memory-pressure behavior are not
measured. The non-inline encryption path is unchanged except for passing the
already computed sector value to the inline helper. Retain this generic ACK
runtime behavior conservatively; no physical storage driver is added.

Extracted zeroout checks pass 1,584 cases / 1,004,124 assertions, covering
1/2/4 KiB blocks, large sector addresses, vector boundaries, DUN wrap and
immediate, deferred reverse-order and mixed completion schedules. Four controls
reject missing producer lifetime protection, wrong sector units, lost errors
and missing DUN splits. Allocation, atomics, completion and block I/O are modeled;
this does not test real concurrency or cryptographic transforms. Only supplied
local sources and the host compiler were used.

## F2FS historical encryption and remaining merge resolutions

`evidence/f2fs-historical-and-merge-review.json` records 18 complete candidate
reviews, including every unique resolution hunk in the selected merges. Nineteen
complete function bodies match pinned LTS. The twentieth retains ACK's unsigned
summary entry count; callers supply counts or the constant one, and the helper's
body is unchanged. Compacted-summary capacity and page boundaries use the native
filesystem block size, with no duplicated geometry fields.

The native encryption interfaces preserve stable 32-bit inode policies, inline
crypto, ciphertext GC, encrypted casefold hashing and eligible direct I/O. The
old merge that dropped crypto-context setup is not reproduced: newly allocated
merged bios receive their context. Generic casefold matching retains decryption
cleanup, strict-encoding behavior and safe concurrent-rename comparison. Native
verity/compression/alignment restrictions on direct I/O remain intact.

Merge resolution is selective. Checkpoint diagnostics follow the newer native
sync sequence, with the previously reviewed bounded retry loop and error return.
Android lock timing/priority controls coexist with native size-aware sysfs writes
and the per-superblock lockdep class; task-priority writes retain capability,
range and teardown protections. Node writes validate against the independent
folio index. Failed reads never inspect an uninitialized footer, and large-folio
completion retains errors across constituent bios. Writeback completion accesses
superblock state and wakes checkpoint waiters before ending writeback, preserving
the LTS teardown fix.

F2FS cleancache initialization is retained for the enabled generic Android
configuration. An ordinary-page cache hit must pass fsverity verification when
required before being marked uptodate. The separate large-folio reader retains
its earlier ownership/error adaptations. GC retains vendor bypass and referenced
atomic-inode handling alongside the native encrypted-folio uptodate check.
These are source-level conclusions supported by the earlier targeted checks and
successful emulator build 81. No filesystem was mounted and no real encrypted
I/O, scheduler, teardown race or cache performance was tested. All evidence in
this review is local; no additional remote resource was accessed.

## FUSE mixed passthrough ownership and access

Local MediaProvider `jni/FuseDaemon.cpp` negotiates upstream passthrough together
with writeback cache, chooses passthrough per open according to redaction and
completed transformations, and checks the Android `fuse_passthrough` feature
attribute. `external/libfuse/lib/fuse_lowlevel.c` registers backing files only
for negotiated passthrough and sets FUSE_INIT_EXT/max_stack_depth. The local
mediaprovider_app policy permits FUSE descriptor operations without granting it
CAP_SYS_ADMIN; app_neverallows excludes other untrusted apps. These are concrete
compatibility reasons for the Android mixed-open behavior and narrower daemon
privilege, not a claim that SELinux makes arbitrary FUSE operations safe.

Dropping CAP_SYS_ADMIN allows a process holding the daemon connection to retain
backing files without that global capability. Connection access, possession of
the lower descriptor, regular-file checks, stack-depth bounds and backing-open
LSM checks remain relevant security controls. The adaptation restores the native
negotiation gate while retaining the Android capability relaxation. It also
handles prepare_creds allocation failure before publishing a backing object;
the file/object are released instead of retaining a NULL credential.

The ACK split between cached/DIO counts and passthrough-open counts left two
old shared-counter assumptions in place. Completing an ordinary parallel direct
write could release the inode's backing reference despite active passthrough
opens, and a cached open could skip waiting for direct writes merely because a
backing pointer existed. The adaptation waits whenever the DIO count is negative
and gives backing-reference release exclusively to the last passthrough open.
Conflicting backing objects remain rejected. Cache writeback errors now stop
passthrough reads/splices rather than being ignored before lower-file reads.

The extracted checks cover 250 mode sequences, 576 registration/error cases and
32 read/writeback outcomes (19,621 assertions). Five regression controls detect
premature backing release, skipped DIO waiting, absent negotiation, missing
credential handling and swallowed writeback errors. Kernel locks, allocation,
references and lower I/O are modeled; real concurrent I/O and Android media
redaction are untested. Mixed writable mappings and invalidation of busy/dirty
cache pages remain broader ACK coherency limitations, not established correct
behavior. The original ACK change explicitly acknowledged the mixed shared-mmap
limitation, and local MediaProvider disables splice-write negotiation after
observing redaction/cache issues. This port retains the required mixed-open
interface with the stated limits; it does not claim that these focused fixes
solve all mixed-cache coherence. No remote resource was accessed for this review.

## FUSE-BPF lookup failure and reference cleanup

Review of the retained child-inode and stacking-depth fixes found incompatible
error conventions in their callers. fuse_iget_backing returns NULL on allocation
failure. Create, mkdir, mknod, link, symlink and lookup paths now report ENOMEM;
lookup finalization cannot turn allocation failure for a positive lower dentry
into a successful negative result. The existing correct-child-inode and I_NEW
initialization rules remain. d_splice_alias consumes the inode on failure too,
so both creation and lookup continue to clear their local reference immediately.

A rejected backing file is represented by ERR_PTR(-ELOOP). Common lookup-reply
cleanup now skips error pointers and releases both backing and unused BPF file
references. fuse_get_bpf_prog consumes its file reference; the caller clears that
slot before handing it over, preventing a later double fput. Revalidation starts
with zeroed reply storage and performs cleanup even when a node-ID mismatch
short-circuits the comparison. Failed daemon replies no longer acquire backing
or program references from output data that was not copied. Stack-depth limits,
canonical-path size validation, locked create/delete lookup, atomic backing-inode
exchange and backing-getattr error propagation remain retained.

The extracted lookup/reference checks pass 49 cases / 282 assertions and reject
four regressions: fput on an error pointer, double BPF-file release, allocation
failure as negative success, and double inode release after splice failure.
The checks model VFS/reference operations and do not establish concurrent
replacement safety. These fixes adapt retained ACK error paths; no native LTS
fix or access check is removed. Source review also checked all seven callers of
fuse_iget_backing against its NULL-return contract. Only local sources were used.

## dm-bow construction, teardown and checkpoint discard

The supplied SDK configuration enables DM_BOW. Local fs_mgr builds `bow` targets
for block checkpoints and selects a 4096-byte block size below dm-default-key;
local vold changes `bow/state`, monitors `bow/free`, and implements rollback.
The supplied x86 goldfish fstab does not request a block checkpoint. This does
not establish that the generic Android capability is dispensable: retain it for
userspace/configuration compatibility, with current SDK runtime use unconfirmed.
Sources examined are `system/fs/fs_mgr/fs_mgr.cpp`,
`system/fs/fs_mgr/libdm/dm_target.cpp`, `system/vold/Checkpoint.cpp`, and
`device/generic/goldfish/board/fstab/x86` in the pinned GrapheneOS checkout.
No additional remote source was consulted.

Keep ACK's kobject-removal/wait before workqueue and bufio destruction: a sysfs
callback must not access destroyed helpers. Adapt early construction failure
paths by initializing both locks, the list, tree and completion before device
lookup can fail, and explicitly return ENOMEM when log allocation fails.
Pair dm_get_device with dm_put_device after draining helpers; destroy both locks.
The original log-allocation path could report successful construction after
freeing the context. The original early teardown could operate on an
uninitialized mutex; the omitted device put leaked a table-device reference.

Extend range locking to the entire table-status list/tree walk, including its
initial validation and all error exits. Guard an absent previous range when
coalescing the first range. Keep the native device-mapper map-result contract:
queue-allocation failure completes the bio with BLK_STS_RESOURCE and returns
DM_MAPIO_SUBMITTED. Returning -ENOMEM instead reaches the LTS dm core's default
BUG branch; the core is deliberately unchanged. In CHECKPOINT state, acknowledge
all nonempty discards before sector-zero relocation, including requests beginning
at zero. Those requests must not discard live relocated data or bypass the
checkpoint discard policy. Preserve the zero-length flush passthrough and
committed-device discard-capability handling. Read the atomic state through its
API instead of accessing its counter directly.

The initial `check_dm_bow.py` executed extracted constructor/destructor/queue/map
code with allocation and I/O-ownership shims: 272 cases, 1261 assertions and five rejected
regression controls. It does not execute real block I/O, kernel concurrency,
range splitting or crash recovery. The status-walk locking and first-range guard
were reviewed directly; they are not covered by that harness.

This review also identified separate, unresolved checkpoint-integrity questions:
`copy_data`/`add_log_entry` currently ignore dirty-buffer write/flush errors,
several internal returns mix errno and block status, and `add_log_entry` releases
an uninitialized buffer on dm_bufio_new failure. The state-change workqueue drain
waits for backup workers, which submit writes without waiting for their endio;
its subject's claim about write completion is therefore not sufficient evidence.
Bufio caches may also need invalidation before rereading blocks modified through
normal bios. These findings were provisional at the initial lifetime checkpoint and are
addressed by the subsequent dm-bow checkpoint I/O ordering and error propagation
section below. The focused checks still do not establish runtime crash consistency.

## dm-user timeout ownership and allocation progress

Retain the enabled Android `user` device-mapper target and its message protocol.
Local `system/fs/fs_mgr/libsnapshot/snapshot.cpp` constructs DmTargetUser devices
and waits for `/dev/dm-user/<name>`; `snapuserd/dm_user_block_server.cpp` reads
requests and returns snapshot data through those control nodes. Local
`system/sepolicy/private/snapuserd.te` grants that daemon access. This establishes
a concrete Android consumer, but does not establish that this SDK instance uses
dm-user for an OTA. The source capability is retained conservatively. No external
resource was used in this review.

Adapt the no-daemon timeout implementation rather than importing its lifetime
assumptions unchanged. Every message initializes its delayed work once. Taking a
message for a channel or for final target destruction removes and reinitializes
its list node while holding the target lock, then cancels/waits for work outside
that lock. A callback skips a detached message. Teardown repeatedly takes the
current first entry instead of preserving a next-entry pointer across unlocking:
another timeout callback could free that saved entry. Cancellation returning
false is not evidence that the callback freed a message; it may have observed a
live daemon and returned without freeing anything. Always release the claimed
message after cancellation completes. Finally free the allocated misc-device
name along with the target.

On the last channel close, rearm every still-queued message with zero delay using
mod_delayed_work. This also handles an earlier timer that observed a daemon and
returned, or a pending initial-connection timer. Do not reinitialize a work item
that may still be executing. A running callback checks for a pending rearm
before freeing its message: last-close may have queued another invocation while
the current callback was waiting for the target lock. That earlier invocation
must leave ownership to the pending one. Count live channels from references with the
DM-owned reference excluded only while that reference actually exists. Keep the
initial configurable 4000 ms daemon grace period and normal daemon-serviced I/O.

An exhausted message pool must not wait under the target lock: readers and
callbacks need that lock to make progress. Use GFP_NOWAIT and the existing
DM_MAPIO_REQUEUE path, checking NULL before initializing a message. Check pool
construction failure before exposing the misc device. The native LTS mempool,
workqueue and device-mapper implementations remain unchanged. These are source
adaptations to an ACK-only target, not reversals of upstream fixes.

The retained channel cleanup uses the from_user member and deletion-safe walks;
constructor references start at one plus one per channel; completion uses
bio_endio without an extra bio_put. Current operation mapping excludes removed
REQ_OP_WRITE_SAME, and the file operations do not reference removed no_llseek.
The unused misc-device conversion helper stays removed, and private helpers
retain static scope.

`check_dm_user_lifetime.py` executes the extracted message, timeout, allocation,
channel-dequeue and target-release routines against deterministic scheduling
shims: 102 cases, 4495 assertions and seven rejected regression controls. It covers
callbacks running during cancellation, callbacks removing following list entries,
last-channel rearming, pool exhaustion and references after DM teardown. This is
not a real kernel concurrency or snapshot-protocol test. Constructor pool failure
is reviewed against native mempool_init_node cleanup, not fault-injected in this
harness. The local daemon also documents that a mid-payload read error cannot be
reported after a successful reply header; this protocol limitation is unchanged
and is not claimed solved by the timeout fixes.

The subsequent dm-user integration review found two more boundary defects.
`bio_copy_from_iter` and `bio_copy_to_iter` returned only the last segment's
short-copy count, losing bytes already copied in preceding segments. Return the
accumulated count, so bio advancement and message positions match the bytes
actually transferred. A three-byte first segment followed by a one-byte partial
copy of the next now reports four bytes instead of one. The unfixed extracted
helpers failed 7,440 assertions in the boundary matrix.

Use bi_iter.bi_size for the request extent instead of walking data vectors:
discard, secure-erase and write-zeroes bios carry a nonzero extent without data
vectors. Reject data-copy attempts on these bios with bio_has_data before any
vector access, including an unexpected payload after their reply header.
Completion's sanity check applies only to successful data-bearing bios; an error
reply or a completed no-payload operation can legitimately retain an extent.
Keep all UAPI opcode numbers and the five-u64 message header unchanged. Local
snapuserd's packed header has the same 40-byte layout on the SDK target and
serves read requests; the no-payload safeguards protect the target's already
advertised operations rather than adding a new platform feature.

`check_dm_user_copy.py` executes extracted size/copy helpers for both directions,
segment lengths, offsets, capacities and fault positions: 41,856 cases and
429,488 assertions. Three regression controls reject loss of preceding bytes,
data-vector access on no-payload bios, and the old vector-based extent calculation.
The vector-access shim traps a no-vector dereference; this is a host model and
not a real uaccess fault or device test. No upstream code is replaced by these
ACK-target corrections.

## dm-bow checkpoint I/O ordering and error propagation

Follow-up local inspection resolved the previously recorded implementation
questions. Vold's cp_prepareCheckpoint performs FITRIM and writes state 1;
cp_commitChanges writes state 2 without explicitly waiting for every in-flight
block I/O. The earlier ACK state_lock plus flush_workqueue only waited for
workers to submit their bios, while sector-zero writes bypassed those workers.
That does not establish completion before relocating or committing sector zero.

Route checkpoint-sensitive I/O through an ordered WQ_MEM_RECLAIM workqueue,
including TRIM-phase mutations, zero-length flushes and sector-zero reads/writes.
Each worker uses a referenced bio clone and submit_bio_wait, preserving the DM
bio's original completion fields and completing it once after lower completion.
Keep nonzero reads and committed I/O as passthrough. State changes hold state_lock
to stop queue submissions, drain the queue without holding ranges_lock, and only
then take ranges_lock for copying and publishing the next state. Range preparation
and lower I/O remain serialized. This reduces concurrent checkpoint write
throughput; no runtime performance measurement is claimed. The stronger ordering
is justified by the need to preserve backup/commit contents, without weakening
native LTS DM, bio, workqueue or bufio implementations.

For I/O crossing sector zero, prepare the remainder before choosing the live
sector-zero destination: backing up the remainder can itself move that live
block. Submit the relocated first block and remainder in order. Use the current
LTS bio_split error-pointer contract, and GFP_NOWAIT for the second allocation
from the shared bio pool so the worker cannot wait for another reserve while
holding its first allocation. Clone allocation/splitting failure before mutation
completes the original bio with the corresponding block error. Normalize nonempty
bio sectors with dm_target_offset before range lookup. Current fs_mgr creates a
single target starting at zero; this also prevents an accepted nonzero target
start from being mistaken for an index into a zero-based range tree.

Before copying a source block, forget its clean bufio cache entry so an earlier
ordinary bio cannot leave a stale cached backup image. All target bufio access is
under ranges_lock, and successful copy/log operations flush dirty buffers before
returning. Copy read/allocation/write/flush errors propagate as block status;
sysfs converts them to negative errno. A failed dm_bufio_new has no buffer to
release. Latch backup/log I/O failures and failed state transitions, rejecting
further I/O/state changes through that target rather than pretending the partly
updated metadata is trustworthy. Queue/clone allocation failure before mutation
is retryable; pure backup-space exhaustion during normal checkpoint writes still
permits a commit. Actual backing I/O ENOSPC is distinguished by the helper's
failure latch. Invalid range lookup and failed trim splitting cannot silently
permit a write to a range still considered free.

The expanded check_dm_bow.py executes extracted construction, teardown, map and
worker code in 1,214 cases with 5,616 assertions and eight regression controls.
Its range preparation and lower completion are shims. check_dm_bow_buffers.py
executes extracted copy_data/add_log_entry/state_store in 198 cases with 3,029
assertions and four regression controls for stale cache, swallowed write error,
positive sysfs error and retrying a failed checkpoint. Storage, CRC and queue
execution are modeled; these checks do not establish real concurrency, actual
crash recovery, encryption correctness or durable-media behavior. The build is
the required integration verification. The original Android log layout remains
unchanged and its documentation is adapted to the current native documentation
location and the corrected behavior. No remote source was consulted; the URL in
the original ACK commit message was not accessed.

## dm-default-key IV bounds and source compatibility

The supplied goldfish userdata fstab requests metadata encryption. Local
vold/MetadataCrypt.cpp creates a DmTargetDefaultKey, and libdm emits IV offset
zero, 4096-byte sectors, iv_large_sectors and allow_discards. Preserve this target,
its fscrypt skip-default-key dependency, key-size/type parser, source crypto APIs,
status redaction, ioctl restrictions and explicit key wiping. Wrapped-key options
remain part of this generic Android interface: vold has concrete raw and wrapped
branches, but a wrapped-key-capable SDK device has not been demonstrated. The
block fallback advertises RAW only; retaining the parser does not permit software
fallback to interpret wrapped material as a raw key. End-to-end wrapped-key
operation remains unverified.

The original maximum-DUN calculation could wrap iv_offset + len - 1. Reject that
range during construction using checked addition; also reject IV offsets that
would make every encrypted request fail the existing alignment check. These
checks prevent a partly usable/wrapped mapping and do not relax encryption or
access checks. The DM core already rejects zero-length targets. A valid single
data unit at DUN zero needs at least one DUN byte: use a minimum of one instead
of passing zero to blk_crypto_init_key, which rejects zero. Normal vold's zero-IV,
4096-byte-sector mapping is unchanged. No LTS implementation is replaced by
these changes: dm-default-key is an ACK-added target.

check_dm_default_key.py executes the actual constructor, optional parser,
destructor and map code with host shims. It checks 3,432 cases and 18,193
assertions against a 128-bit IV-range oracle, including allocation/key-setup
failure cleanup, target-relative mapping, data-less requests, skip flags and
existing crypto-context rejection. Four negative controls reproduce zero-width,
unchecked wrapping, unaligned-IV acceptance and uninitialized-key eviction.
Crypto, devices and bio allocation are shims; actual encryption, key erasure in
kernel memory, device table validation, concurrency and hardware are not tested.

Keep the existing report_zones callback and append-emulation safety condition
with the generic target for the selected CONFIG_BLK_DEV_ZONED build. Native DM
honors emulate_zone_append before calling the mapping that derives the IV, so a
zoned append must have a known final sector before encryption. This conservatively
retains an existing generic block interface; the supplied userdata is conventional
virtio storage, and actual SDK zoned use is unresolved. No platform driver is
added for it. Do not copy the ACK BLK_FEAT_ORDERED_HWQ assertion: that flag belongs
to a different block-layer ordering optimization and is absent from the native
LTS interface. Retain native queue/zone serialization instead. These narrow
target decisions do not finalize the separate multi-file block-crypto candidates.
Only local checkouts were used.

## Android test-selection archive

The x86_64 common_kernel target includes test_mappings_zip in extra_dist. Its
pkg_files rule packages the kernel tree's TEST_MAPPING files as inert JSON
metadata. Restore 29 missing generic/x86 mapping files so that source changes in
those paths remain associated with Android's existing test groups and filters.
This adds no kernel driver, runtime permission or historical vendor-binary KMI.
The supplied KernelAbilistTest.java inspects Android CPU ABI properties and
returns immediately on non-Arm; its descriptor is not a kernel KMI layout check.

The complete before/after JSON of all 34 pending metadata-only commits was
parsed, rejecting duplicate object keys, and the per-path test/import/option
changes recorded. Retain the final ACK group and filter evolution rather than
replaying obsolete selections: it includes the Telecom class-name correction,
the later removal of broad Libcore selection in 8896bb41cd5a, and scheduler/power
presubmit reductions. Exclude only the desktop-postsubmit IGT group introduced
by 6442b9cc8f99; it specifically selects the separate desktop display validation
suite and is not part of this SDK build target. Final ACK's DRM mapping contains
only that group, so the file is omitted entirely. The other 51 examined final
mapping files match ACK semantically.

These files describe tests; they neither execute the tests nor establish that
every referenced Android package/filter is available or suitable on this SDK.
For example, ACK's fs mapping imports system/core/fs_mgr while the supplied
GrapheneOS checkout locates fs_mgr under system/fs/fs_mgr. Preserve the existing
metadata here rather than claim that the Android test harness was validated.
Only the archive generation and kernel/module workflow are in the requested
verification scope. No Android OS build, CTS/VTS run or external resource access
was performed for this review.

## DMA-buf accounting under allocation failure

The earlier OOM accounting question is resolved by `a7db20b7e4ce`, with
runtime behavior still unverified. In ACK, record preloading can fail before
looking up an existing record. Several callers deliberately continue successful
FD installation or mapping after this failure. A subsequent close can therefore
subtract a reference that was never counted, including from a record created
later for the same buffer. These are statistics counters, separate from native
file and DMA-buf lifetime references; the observed undercount is not itself a
claim of a buffer use-after-free.

The adaptation first counts existing records under the accounting lock without
allocating. It checks again after preloading, including failed preloading, so
another thread's intervening insertion is counted. If there is still no record
and no available allocation, the context becomes incomplete. Existing records
continue to count every duplicate and drain normally, but no new records can
be created in that context. This last condition matters: merely looking up
existing records before allocating would not prevent an untracked old close
from decrementing a record created later. Missing-record closes are expected
and quiet for incomplete contexts. The per-CPU pool's caller-owned lock and
bounded trimming remain; no allocation is moved under the accounting spinlock.

RSS, PSS and HWM reads, and HWM reset, return `EIO` for an incomplete context.
The numeric success format, permissions, disabled-accounting behavior and
owner references remain. A fresh independently constructed fork/exec context
can collect statistics again from its actual FDs and VMAs. Existing contexts
do not attempt a racing reconstruction. `fd_install` and successful mappings
remain nonfatal on accounting allocation failure; fork/exec setup can still
fail allocation. A failed `dup2` can conservatively invalidate statistics even
when its attempted acquisition is rolled back.

This is an intentional compatibility tradeoff under allocation failure. Pinned
GrapheneOS `system/memory/lmkd/lmkd.cpp` handles a failed procfs read by omitting
the DMA-buf term and continuing ordinary task RSS selection. Victim choice can
be less informed under OOM. Reporting unavailable data avoids presenting a
known incomplete count as valid, but does not guarantee accurate post-OOM
victim sizing. PSS still divides by tracked contexts: unsupported sharing and
untracked references can skew another context's estimate. RSS trace events
after failure describe the remaining known records, not complete allocation
telemetry. No new trace validity ABI is claimed. Native LTS file, buffer,
fence, reservation and mapping lifetimes are unchanged by this adaptation.

The complete nine candidate patches (2,294 lines including metadata) and the
current FD, VMA, fork, exec and procfs owner paths were reviewed. The release
hook is retained conservatively as a generic vendor source customization
interface; no SDK subscriber is established. It runs after exporter release,
so callbacks must not dereference exporter-private storage already released.
GrapheneOS Perfetto has the DMA-buf RSS trace descriptor and protobuf event;
libmeminfo supplies the RSS/HWM API tests. These local tests were inspected,
not executed. Historical current-task attribution, direct unpinned procfs
state and stale parent accounting copies are superseded by the final owner,
reference and reconstruction fixes, without restoring KABI padding.

`check_dmabuf_accounting.py` extracts the accounting/list and four procfs
handlers. Its host shims pass 46,662 cases and 7,331,995 assertions, including
selected preload interleavings, caller-owned preloads, unavailable statistics,
HWM reset and balanced references. Four negative controls fail. This does not
execute real kernel allocation faults, locking, FD installation, mapping,
fork/exec, procfs, tracing or LMK. Build 89 completed for this source, producing both kernel images, 100 common
modules and 45 virtual-device modules. All signatures verified, the altered
payload was rejected, and all protected-export and module table checks passed. `evidence/dmabuf-accounting-local-review.json` records each decision,
result IDs and exact unchanged-function witnesses supporting 28 earlier final
decisions. All evidence used for this review was local; no remote resource
was accessed.

## Rust DMA-buf source interfaces

The selected SDK configuration builds Rust wrapfd, and its code directly uses
`DmaBuf::get`, buffer/file references, vmap, bounded CPU access, fallible CPU
access closures and locked name reporting. Pinned GrapheneOS
`system/memory/libwrapfd/rust/lib.rs` opens `/dev/wrapfd` and wraps supplied FDs
with protection flags. This establishes a local userspace interface and source
consumer; it does not establish installed product use or runtime behavior.
The nine decisions in `evidence/rust-dmabuf-local-review.json` retain those
interfaces and the associated C/bindgen/file helpers. They do not finalize the
separate whole wrapfd implementation.

`b186055db111` adds an `ARef<Device>` to `DmaBufAttachment`. The native C
`dma_buf_dynamic_attach` stores `attach->dev` without taking a device reference,
while the original Rust wrapper returned an independently owned attachment
from a short-lived `&Device` borrow. Keeping only the buffer alive was
insufficient: mapping, unmapping or detaching could reach the stored device
after its last owner released it. The attachment now keeps both objects alive
through its destructor, including map-error cleanup. Native C behavior remains.
A device reference does not keep its driver bound or establish safe device
resource lifetime across unbind; this fix is specifically object lifetime.

CPU access retains checked offset-plus-length bounds and direction-restricted
copy methods. Begin failure skips the closure; every normally returning closure,
including an error result, is followed by end access. End-access failure takes
precedence over the closure result. Kernel panic is fatal, and no unwind cleanup
contract is promised. Mutable buffer names are exposed only during a closure
holding `name_lock`; callbacks must not sleep. Exporter-name access follows the
native explicit non-NULL immutable-name contract. Raw address access still
requires unsafe use and appropriate lifetime/synchronization obligations.
No live DMA coherency or file-loading correctness is claimed.

`file::Offset` preserves native signed `loff_t`. File mode constants expose
native bits used by wrapfd's write-permission checks; the size thunk delegates
to native `i_size_read` for ashmem memfd compatibility and wrapfd extent checks.
The two original size-thunk patches are identical and represented once.
Public `Device::as_raw` visibility is retained conservatively for vendor Rust
source customization. Its local use inside the kernel crate alone does not
require public visibility; an SDK external-module consumer is not established.
No physical reset-controller driver is added on that basis.

The extracted attach/map/drop ownership test passes 36 cases and 350 assertions;
removing the device reference fails 54 assertions. It uses host FFI/refcount
shims and the manifest's pinned Rust compiler, not actual DMA operations,
unbind, kernel concurrency or SDK runtime. Build 90 completed with both kernel images, 100 common and 45 virtual-device
modules. All 145 signatures and all module/export table checks passed; the
tampered signature control was rejected.
All reviewed sources were local. URLs embedded in original commit messages
were not accessed.

## Rust Binder container and identity dependencies

Ten source dependency decisions are recorded in
`evidence/rust-container-api-review.json`. All candidate patches were read in
full, together with final bitmap/ID-pool operations and their configured Rust
Binder callers. The ID pool uses one native word inline, bounds allocated
bitmaps by `i32::MAX`, and defers bit acquisition until a mutable-borrow token
is committed. Binder uses that interval for fallible handle preparation and
rechecks after dropping/reacquiring its lock. Allocation and copying remain
bounded; a stale resize request is revalidated before replacement. This does
not independently certify every Binder lock interleaving.

ARef equality delegates to its referenced type, while Task equality compares
live task object identity. Binder uses these together for current-process
checks, rather than equating reused numeric PIDs. KVVec shrinking preserves
initialized elements and leaves the original vector intact on allocation
failure; its selected caller treats shrinking as an optional memory reduction.
The new Error `ZeroableOption` implementation relies on transparent NonZeroI32
layout to represent `None` as zero and does not permit zero as an Error.
The bitflag macro is concretely used for Binder diagnostic masks.

These APIs remain the compiled source from successful build 90. No source edit
or new build was required for these decisions. Five complete helper files
match ACK exactly; KVVec retains two LTS documentation/safety-comment
corrections and the selected shrink implementation. Other task-priority and
Binder driver changes remain independently reviewed. No allocation-fault,
Binder runtime or concurrency test was performed for this group, and no
remote source was accessed.

## Rust ashmem and build helpers

Seven candidates are individually reviewed in
`evidence/rust-ashmem-build-helper-review.json`. The miscdevice seek callback and
rwsem lockdep-class helpers have direct consumers in the selected Rust ashmem
implementation. Duplicate ACK commits have identical patches and are represented
once. The backing-file class is assigned before publication using a static key;
its exclusion from ordinary write syscalls explains the separate diagnostic
class. The seek callback retains the VFS position-serialization contract and
propagates native errors, without changing file ownership.

The Rust page-size helpers support the SDK's explicitly configured `page_shift=14`
mode. Ashmem validates the size below `usize::MAX >> 1` before rounding it, and the
C parser bounds the shift before enabling the read-only-after-init setting. The
helper does not promise overflow-safe rounding of arbitrary addresses. This
review does not settle the separately tracked shared-mapping EOF question.

The NOMMU-only `is_vmalloc_addr` wrapper is removed by `5123d876580b`: the selected
MMU kernel already supplies the native external declaration. Its retained
KVVec caller continues using the native function; no allocator policy is changed.
The `02a4a20a57bd` merge's one unique resolution preserves both inline C-helper
linking and the LTS `-Zunstable-options` fix. The final SDK build is recorded
separately; compilation is not a runtime or lockdep test.

## Rust publication, poll and priority helpers

Five candidates are reviewed in `evidence/rust-poll-dependency-review.json`.
Rust Binder's NICE limit conversion keeps the unsigned clamp before conversion:
`RLIM_INFINITY` must not cause an overflow panic. Zero still produces the caller's
unset-limit rejection; a high limit cannot exceed the legal nice priority range.
WrapFD's namespace declaration generates the observed `wrapfd.import_ns=DMA_BUF`
metadata and preserves native namespace verification.

SetOnce uses exclusive atomic acquisition and Release/Acquire publication, with
Send/Sync bounds covering the stored value's movement, sharing and destruction.
The selected Binder poll caller allocates before its spinlock, and a losing
population can drop the unused queue while that lock is held. PollCondVarBox's
embedded RCU head allows this without the synchronous destructor sleeping: it
first removes poll waiters, then lets native LTS RCU defer freeing the pinned
allocation. Ordinary PollCondVar keeps its original synchronous destructor.
The native reclamation and wake implementations are retained. Source review and
successful compilation do not verify epoll races or kernel execution.

## Rust helper inlining and native build infrastructure

Fourteen candidates are reviewed in `evidence/rust-build-family-review.json`.
Native LTS already supplies Rust constructor-selection cleanup, LLVM version
detection and proc-macro linker flags; its stricter version-string parser and
Kleaf's actual linker-flag consumer are preserved. The initial helper-inlining
proposal and temporary revert are superseded by the replacement implementation,
which keeps distinct built-in/module helpers and correct code-generation order.
