The SDK selects inlining with both compilers reporting LLVM 22.0.1. Keep the
compatibility gate and bindgen-specific macro definition. Forced inlining
bypasses LLVM attribute heuristics; this choice is restricted by the existing
configuration gates and does not remove native helper checks or globally weaken
compiler flags. All 50 annotation patches preserve token streams after removing
only the annotation, and all four Rust lock patches add only inline attributes.
Performance and compiler correctness have not been independently measured.

The final Clang step retains unreachable traps and excludes C LTO flags so code
generation occurs where those trap options apply. The saved kernel Rust compile
command contains both trap flags. The SDK currently uses no C LTO; alternate-LTO
behavior is reviewed in source only. Native Rust target flags and objtool remain.

Review found that atomic.c and its generator disagreed about the helper macro
fallback. `f4693bcebd83` corrects the generator; executing it reproduces the
checked-in body and its SHA-1 exactly. Atomic operations are unchanged. The
ashmem Offset alias cleanup also preserves the earlier legacy ioctl payload fix.

## Rust WrapFD lifetime and IRQ helpers

Three candidates are reviewed in `evidence/rust-wrapfd-dependency-review.json`.
The new anonymous-inode wrapper called `File::from_raw_file` from final release,
after native `__fput` had consumed the last reference. The native Rust contract
requires a positive reference count: exposing a regular File there permits safe
code to attempt reference acquisition during destruction. `f744d35f5a43` removes
the File argument from this new callback, retaining owned-private-data cleanup.
The selected WrapFD implementation uses the default cleanup. Custom source
implementations of this newly introduced callback must adopt the safer signature;
this deliberate API deviation prevents the lifetime violation. Native LTS
anonymous-inode allocation, LSM initialization, module pinning and failure cleanup
remain unchanged. Other inherited Rust file interfaces are not certified by this
scoped review.

The extracted creation/release harness passes 16 cases and 213 assertions,
including ownership return on failure and successful retry. The actual ACK
implementation produces 16 invalid File references in the same host model.
These are FFI/refcount shims, not native LSM, file-syscall or race tests.

WrapFD uses the retained IRQ lock around completion results. The closure guard
cannot escape; three compiler rejection probes and an owned-return success probe
check that property with the actual extracted method and simplified lock types.
Native irqsave/irqrestore supplies interrupt semantics; no IRQ execution is
claimed. Error values still return through the unlock path. The selected kernel
has no panic unwinding or PREEMPT_RT behavior verified here. Mask-only page helpers
retain native base-page units for DMA and direct-I/O buffer geometry. The complete
WrapFD driver, including caller arithmetic, remains a separate audit item.

## Rust netlink reports and Binder initialization

The Rust generic-netlink and Binder reporting candidates are retained for a
confirmed platform consumer: local frameworks/native BinderNetlink joins the
`binder` family / `report` group, and frameworks/base CachedAppOptimizer receives
transaction failures and oneway-spam reports. The report contains transaction
metadata (context, PIDs/TIDs, flags, code and 32-bit payload size), not transaction
payloads. Its existing size field remains a 32-bit ABI; it is not a lossless
representation of arbitrary usize values. Family and attribute IDs match the C
Binder API and platform parser. Separate failure and spam reports remain possible.

`320c37fcab42` bounds netlink allocation arithmetic and attribute payload lengths,
rejects invalid groups before giving away SKB ownership, and excludes negative
listener errors. Native multicast returns early without consuming the SKB on an
invalid group; ACK's ManuallyDrop placement leaked it. Native nla_len is u16,
so a larger Rust string could otherwise generate a truncated wire attribute.
The selected Binder context strings do not establish a reachable oversized-string
exploit; this corrects the generic wrapper's API. `4ad2bc23b3b2` derives aligned
header lengths from native types after build 94 showed that bindgen did not
export the C sizeof macros. The signed arithmetic check also includes both native
headers and alignment. All selected native netlink C implementations remain LTS.

`5093b3165725` makes family registration and operations unsafe with explicit
registration-lifetime requirements. Raw family identifiers are changed by native
registration and have no per-operation Rust ownership token. A static allocation
alone does not prove that registration remains live or excludes a racing
re-registration. The selected Binder calls now document their invariant: register
once before binderfs publication, retain the registration, and keep the active
module resident. A source caller must obey this contract; this is a deliberate
source-interface correction to prevent unsupported safe usage. No historical
vendor binary ABI is preserved.

The family preserves native C Binder's multicast flags and network namespace
behavior; it introduces no new per-group capability gate. Local SELinux policy
allows system_server generic netlink sockets and forbids them for untrusted apps.
Thus the metadata boundary depends on the platform's existing socket policy and
privileged listeners. Policy execution and message delivery were not tested.
MAINTAINERS globs and the organizational net.rs rename are omitted: the new API
is exposed through the native LTS net.rs alongside its PHY submodule, as adapted
in 8dca59f73173. No networking functionality is removed by that layout choice.

Source review also found that active Rust Binder had no teardown for its binderfs
or shrinker callbacks, although the generated Rust module exit permits unloading
and SDK CONFIG_MODULE_UNLOAD=y. `3f90fca18094` takes a permanent module reference
only after successful activation. This intentionally prevents active driver
unloading until shutdown; it does not claim to implement complete Binder teardown.
The inactive path that retains C Binder takes no reference and remains unloadable.
The SDK disables forced module unloading. A loader reference protects the
publication-to-reference interval while init is running.

If binderfs initialization fails, unregister the successfully registered shrinker
and then destroy its unused LRU. Native shrinker_free waits for outstanding
callbacks before the module can be freed. Registration failure creates no page
ranges because no filesystem was published. A failed shrinker allocation now
returns ENOMEM instead of converting the earlier zero return value to an error.
The native callback machinery and module loader are unchanged.

Both transaction logs use 32 pinned, separately locked slots. Their bounded
context-name copies and byte-string formatting do not overread a name without a
NUL in its 16-byte buffer. Ring ordering is best-effort, including debug-id wrap
handling; it is not an audit log of every IPC. GrapheneOS dumpstate explicitly
reads both files, which are created with stats=global and carry local
binderfs_logs_transaction_history SELinux labels. Local policy permits dumpstate
and system_server and restricts other domains. No policy changes are made.

Log allocations now remain locally owned through fallible registration and are
published infallibly on success, releasing both on initialization failure. A
concurrent early mount/transaction in the short interval between filesystem and
log publication can observe an empty log or omit an initial diagnostic record;
transaction processing still works. This bounded diagnostic limitation avoids
leaking log allocations on failed module loads. Failure after unloading C still
does not restore C automatically, as documented in the earlier selector review.

The extracted netlink checks exercise 55 cases / 347 assertions; the original
ACK control fails 86 assertions. Extracted Binder initialization/shrinker/log
preparation checks cover nine cases / 73 assertions; ACK fails 14 assertions.
These use host allocation/FFI/lifecycle shims. They do not execute real sockets,
LSM policy, shrinker concurrency, module loading/unloading or Binder IPC.
Build 95 verifies the bounded-netlink implementation; build 96 passed with
the later lifetime changes, both images and all 145 signed modules. See rust-netlink-binder-report-review.json and the
recorded build results. No remote resources were accessed for this review.

## Rust rate limiting and static lock initialization

Retain the four lockdep/const-spinlock/rate-limit/Binder-print candidates reviewed
in rust-ratelimit-lockdep-review.json. Selected Binder error paths can otherwise
print repeatedly in response to malformed requests. The replacement limits
messages per call site using native defaults (5*HZ, burst 10), without suppressing
transaction processing, error returns, cleanup, netlink sends or the ring logs.
Repeated messages can be suppressed; these diagnostics are not a complete audit
trail. Formatting arguments are evaluated only for admitted messages. All changed
arguments were examined as diagnostic reads, and normalized token comparison
of all ten files confirms only print-macro, newline and formatting changes.

Rust state calls the unchanged LTS ___ratelimit implementation, including native
atomic budgets and contended interval behavior. Static initialization matches
RATELIMIT_STATE_INIT with zero initial flags/counters. Dynamic state is pinned;
static construction explicitly requires permanent placement. Both keep the
internal raw-lock address stable. SDK x86 SMP uses a zero-initialized qspinlock,
matching the Rust const constructor. Debug spinlock sentinels and lockdep wait
types match the C initializers by inspection; SDK does not enable those debug
configurations, so their compilation and runtime behavior are not claimed tested.

The lockdep relocation retains the old sync re-exports and dynamic key lifetime.
Its new Send bound permits transferring pinned ownership, without relocating the
key allocation; native dynamic-key register/unregister paths use their existing
synchronization. The explicit static constructor forbids destruction of a static
key. Only the organizational MAINTAINERS addition is omitted. Native lockdep and
rate-limit C files remain exactly LTS. The required shared initializer retains
its existing cfg branches, without adding drivers for future platforms. Existing
documentation examples are not treated as compiled doctests.

## Rust module parameter compatibility

Retain integer module parameters, radix parsing, module! generation and read-only
sysfs output for WrapFD's max_nr_load_reqs and min_bytes_per_req. The selected
driver declares usize defaults of 32 and 0x100000 and uses these values when
partitioning load requests. Build 96 contains both parm/parmtype metadata records.
Native kernel parameter ingress/locking and lib/kstrtox.c remain exactly LTS.
Whole WrapFD I/O arithmetic is still reviewed separately; accepting an arbitrary
usize parameter does not by itself validate all downstream calculations.

`aa0a73a857cc` fixes the setter's layout assumption: the macro passes a pointer to
ModuleParamAccess, but ACK casts it directly to SetOnce, assuming the value field
is first in memory. Rust makes no such guarantee. Access the actual container
field instead. Randomized-layout checks exercise nonzero offsets without invoking
the invalid ACK cast. Values remain immutable after the first successful
assignment; invalid inputs do not populate the container, duplicates return
EEXIST, and references previously returned to the default remain valid.

The same adaptation handles one leading sign before selecting radix and accepts
one trailing newline, restoring native interpretations of +010 and +0x10 while
rejecting extra signs such as -+1 and 0x+1. Bounds are checked for all ten integer
types before negating. Binary/explicit-octal syntax and unsigned negative zero
remain ACK extensions; invalid/overflow input still returns EINVAL, so this is not
a claim of identical native parser errno behavior. No native parser is replaced.

Sysfs permissions remain 0444, and native add_sysfs_param installs a store method
only when write bits exist. Reads emit decimal plus newline. Use bounded
Formatter and return an error before NUL termination on formatting failure.
ACK's RawFormatter counts attempted bytes beyond the buffer, so its position was
not guaranteed bounded as the old safety comment claimed. Generated operations
currently support only short primitive integer outputs; no existing long-output
userspace exploit is asserted. The generic boundary check uses an oversized
Display and extra guard storage to observe ACK's behavior without a host
out-of-allocation write.

The module macro retains static parameter records, type checking and its existing
built-in/loadable metadata conventions. Supported generated values are Copy
integers. No writable runtime parameter or vendor binary layout is introduced.
The macro's stale documentation accessor is not counted as a compiled example.
The extracted checks pass 5,602 assertions under each of the normal layout and
three randomized layouts. They copy the parser, callbacks, container, SetOnce and
formatters, with host string/atomic/native-struct shims. They do not execute kernel
parameter ingress, sysfs, module loading or concurrent callers. Build 97 passed
the integrated adaptation with both images and all 145 signed modules. No additional remote resources were used.

## Rust integration history and ashmem selection

Finalize the obsolete Rust/MODVERSIONS workaround and its revert against native
LTS support: use EXTENDED_MODVERSIONS plus GENDWARFKSYMS and real GPL exports,
without the old blanket export suppression or !MODVERSIONS gate. The selected
SDK builds Rust Binder as a module with Rust, MODVERSIONS and extended symbol
versions enabled. Conditional helper exports belong to the separately reviewed
inline-helper strategy. No old vendor binary layout is preserved.

Retain lru_status's explicit Rust CFI encoding and direct shrinker callback,
removing the old C trampoline. Build 97 enables CFI and integer normalization,
without permissive mode. Its rust_shrink_free_page tag is 0xb01d9dea, matching a
C fixture using the enum and callback typedef extracted from native list_lru.h.
An unsigned-integer return control has tag 0xa0498a8a. This verifies the compiled
type identity offline; it neither executes the indirect call nor exercises a
CFI trap. The native list-LRU implementation and CFI enforcement remain intact.

Retain reset.h's bindgen inclusion as a generic source interface for vendor
modules, without enabling or adding a reset-controller driver. SDK disables
RESET_CONTROLLER; its native header supplies optional/error stubs. The local
common-modules Rust search did not establish a consumer. Concrete SDK runtime
necessity is unconfirmed and recorded in unresolved-feature-questions.json.
Keeping these declarations is a conservative source-compatibility decision, not
a claim of demonstrated device use or justification for adding a future platform.

The two base Rust ashmem imports have identical stable patch IDs and new-file
contents; retain their effect once. The complete selected drivers/staging/android
subtree is unchanged from the full review at 3ea7a304b5dc. That review and its
per-file evidence cover range/LRU ownership, shrinker destruction, monotonic
protection, usercopy bounds, referenced backing files, native memfd sealing and
administrator controls. The earlier GET_PIN_STATUS/toggle-read and CAP_SYS_ADMIN
corrections remain. This reuses established source review rather than treating
successful compilation as proof of those semantics.

Temporary ASHMEM_RUST selector/filename cleanup and its revert are superseded by
the final single Rust implementation. Preserve final ASHMEM depending on SHMEM
and RUST, ashmem.rs/ashmem_exports.c filenames and ashmem_rust module metadata.
The removed duplicate C ashmem and memfd shim are not restored: their required
legacy device/memfd APIs remain in the selected Rust implementation. This target
has Rust enabled and does not require the historical ARM64 KASAN_SW_TAGS fallback.
No native LTS memfd seal or shmem security check is removed.

Retain 32-bit GET_FILE_ID's separate command number and u32 output, including the
compat_ptr conversion in both real-ashmem and memfd paths. Bionic encodes this
command with unsigned long; local libcutils compares its unsigned-long result
with fstat. Native-width and compat-width outputs consequently differ. Truncating
to the 32-bit ABI does not promise a globally unique 64-bit identifier. Bounded
usercopy/error handling and the memfd-only shmem gate remain; unknown memfd ioctls
return ENOTTY. The SDK builds IA32/COMPAT, but no actual 32-bit client was run.
The independent shared EOF/page-emulation question remains open and is not
certified by these ashmem decisions. No remote resource was accessed.

## Rust Binder priority and trace interfaces

Retain Android priority inheritance in the optional Rust Binder driver. Local
libbinder validates nice priorities -20..19 and RT priorities 1..99 and encodes
the priority in eight wire bits. Native C Binder decodes those bits through s8.
f7ca990e55b1 fixes Rust's unsigned interpretation of negative nice priorities and
bounds the unsigned RLIMIT_RTPRIO before converting to i32, so large/infinite
limits cannot truncate negative. CAP_SYS_NICE, zero-RT-limit fallback, bounded
RLIMIT_NICE and native scheduler validation remain. RESET_ON_FORK is retained.
These Rust adaptations do not replace native scheduler implementation or give
malformed node flags authority to bypass its priority range checks.

Keep the selected port's comparison against desired priority after suppressing
unrequested RT inheritance, matching C Binder. Save the actual selected thread's
policy/priority, preserving the effect of 80c8d66849ff even though the final ACK
boundary regressed that line. The pending/abort protocol and once-only atomic
selection remain. Restore the default priority before publishing a waiting
thread in the ready list; retain sender-side boosting before delivery/wakeup.
These two ordering changes prevent a waiter overwriting an incoming boost and
avoid relying only on receiver self-boost. RT conversion retains saturating
subtraction; in the final signed types it saturates to the signed bound, not the
zero described in the historical unsigned implementation's commit message.

check_rust_binder_priority.py extracts the real decoding, limit conversion,
priority setting and selection methods. It passes 3,731 assertions over every
wire byte/policy, signed extremes, zero/large resource limits, capability cases,
distinct target-thread/process priorities, pending restoration and once-only
selection. The pinned ACK control has 646 failed assertions. Task storage,
capability reads, scheduler effects, locks and hooks are host shims. This checks
decisions, not real Binder IPC, concurrent priority restoration or scheduling.

Retain priority and looper vendor hooks as generic source customization APIs;
exact SDK vendor callbacks are unconfirmed, which is explicitly recorded in
unresolved-feature-questions.json. Bare hooks are not exposed as tracefs events.
They invoke trusted kernel callbacks synchronously with live borrowed objects;
looper notifications run under the thread lock and callbacks must obey that
context. They report state, without promising exactly-once notification.

Hook definitions and the seven GPL exports remain built-in when Rust Binder is
a module. Build 98's Module.symvers confirms all seven resolve to vmlinux. This
avoids requiring an early-loaded vendor module to depend on rust_binder.ko.
RUST_BINDER_LAYOUT contains this build's computed offsets and is published
before the driver filesystem. Successful activation pins the module as described
above; inactive modules cannot generate these hooks. Consumers must only read
live objects supplied by the driver. Retain READ_ONCE and conservative null/RCU
contracts for task accessors; current Process/Thread task fields own non-null
ARef values. No future task-nulling implementation is imported.

4b5a05d8f96b derives task-pointer offsets using ARef::PTR_OFFSET and the node
allocation offset using DArc<Node>, avoiding assumptions about wrapper field
position or the alignment of a different container type. This describes the
current layout rather than fixing a historical binary layout; no incorrect
offset in the pinned compiler's previous output is asserted. The metadata's
ANDROID_BACKPORT_RESERVE padding and corresponding Rust zeros remain omitted
because their sole purpose is historical KMI continuity. Vendor modules must be
rebuilt. 3ca803b94e61 also declares the public looper hook's opaque thread type
and forward-declares task_struct, removing dependence on the private header's
inclusion order.

Keep Binder trace names used by local atrace and Perfetto. The complete trace
patch was reviewed, including all callsites, C field accessors, layout constants
and the shrinker path that copies scalar pid/page index before dropping locks.
3ca803b94e61 corrects node_ptr to the user-supplied node address, translated_handle
to the translated object's handle, and page-range traces from page indices to
byte units. Diagnostic conversion saturates and clamps reversed ranges; it does
not change memory allocation. Exposing the actual user node address restores
the native C tracing information class, subject to existing tracefs DAC/SELinux
policy; it is not a kernel pointer or transaction payload. Return-command string
lookups remain bounded. Event removal for the dual-driver selector is a separate
review, not certified by this trace decision. Build 99 passed the later trace/layout changes with both images and all 145
modules/signatures and matching export tables; no trace session, vendor callback
or driver load was run.
No additional remote resource was accessed. Exact per-commit evidence and result
IDs are in rust-binder-priority-hooks-review.json.

## Rust Binder lifecycle and query review

f1b305f3bd93 bounds the caller-controlled node-debug lookup at UINT64_MAX. The
ACK lower-bound optimization added one to the supplied pointer without checking
for overflow; the SDK enables Rust overflow checks, making that ioctl argument
a kernel-panic trigger. The port now returns the empty result at the maximum,
as native C Binder's strictly-greater traversal does. Other queries retain the
logarithmic lookup under the process lock, with usercopy after unlocking. Local
ProcessState::getKernelReferences consumes this interface. The extracted method
passes 2,052 cases over empty/populated maps and pointer boundaries; the pinned
ACK control panics in 125 cases. Trees, usercopy, references and locks are host
shims, so this is no claim of an executed kernel ioctl or concurrent traversal.

Three reviewed ACK fixes already have equivalent changed lines in pinned LTS:
3deed10abfb1 matches bd818dcf4783 for extended errors, a77715a18da8 matches
667599e71832 for obsolete oneway transaction cleanup, and 0487f3a4e332 matches
91b27f8172cd for abnormal freeze-listener removal. Ordered added/removed lines
match per file after whitespace normalization, independently of differing patch
context. The final source was checked for each effect. Preserve stored extended
errors and remote reply-error delivery; preserve removing the obsolete queued
transaction's oneway node before drop; preserve breaking the listener reference
cycle when its node reference disappears. None is replayed as a duplicate.

Keep the node_refs spinlock conversion together with all allocation/destructor
preparation. All lock sites in process.rs and freeze.rs were inspected. Usercopy
is outside the guards; node/message/tree allocation precedes locking; bitmap and
freeze-vector expansion releases/reacquires and revalidates. Terminal tree,
NodeRefInfo, NodeDeath and vector ownership is moved outside guards. Clearing a
death notification remains atomic under node_refs, while queueing occurs after
unlocking. Process cleanup keeps NodeDeath in NodeRefInfo until the detached
trees are dropped. Insertion preserves reservations and releases node_refs before
returning a dead-process error with newly initialized objects. Native bitmap_free
uses kfree; rejected poll initialization uses the earlier embedded-RCU destructor.
This source review does not replace runtime lockdep or a concurrency proof.

Preserve current-task subjective EUID for outgoing transactions and replies,
independently of the existing LSM checks using pinned process credentials. Keep
object-identity Task/ARef comparisons for current-process ownership and descriptor
cleanup. Allocation destruction only schedules close_on_free in the target
process context, avoiding unrelated descriptors in another process. Existing
allocation/invalid-fd failures may skip closes; this review does not claim to fix
those cases. BC_FREE_BUFFER keeps its explicit owned drop after the temporary
looper flag lock guard has ended.

Keep native compat_ptr_ioctl for pointer/ignored Binder ioctl arguments, gated by
CONFIG_COMPAT. SDK IA32/COMPAT builds it; no 32-bit client was run. Keep target
process default priority for oneway calls, minimum-node comparison after RT
suppression, and pending parent-priority state for nested replies. Their
capability/resource-limit and once-only selection guards remain as reviewed above.

Retain stable diagnostic crate/wait-symbol names, scalar allocation trace fields,
zero-skipping internal debug IDs, and the read-only transaction_report capability
file for the implemented netlink interface. Local libbinder reads extended_error
and uses netlink reports; a reader of transaction_report itself was not found,
and is not claimed. Internal IDs and their u32 UAPI projections can wrap and are
not security identities. No performance measurement is inferred from source
optimizations. The full 26-candidate record and result IDs are in
rust-binder-runtime-review.json. Build 100 passed the query adaptation with both images, 100 common and 45
virtual-device modules, 145 verified signatures with tampered control rejected,
and 659 protected exports/145 matching flag/CRC tables. No runtime Binder
session, descriptor cleanup or credential override was executed. No
additional remote resource was accessed.

Retain bitmap-backed handle allocation with manager-only zero, bit/tree
consistency checks and allocation retries outside node_refs. Context/process
registries use KVVec<Arc> under mutexes with propagated registration errors,
identity-based deregistration and live snapshots. Shrinking only below one-quarter
capacity to twice the length bounds arithmetic and leaves valid data on failure.
FD translation metadata and reservations also use KVVec; translation, rollback
and fd installation occur after taking Allocation out of its spinlock. The
smaller close_on_free u32 vector remains KVec. These selections retain Android
runtime and memory-pressure behavior, without adopting historical benchmark or
QEMU results as measurements of this port.

## Rust Binder diagnostic controls

Retain the ten reviewed debug-mask, lifecycle logging and error-location changes
with the selected Rust Binder driver. The C uint module parameter retains 0644
permissions, native parsing/store serialization and the same default 0x7 mask as
C Binder. The Rust Atomic<u32> has transparent representation and native atomic
reads; build 100's module symbol is four bytes. No concurrent parameter-write or
sysfs behavior was executed. Exact SDK use of this optional diagnostic control
is unconfirmed; retaining it preserves the current driver's configuration API.

All category callsites were reviewed. Replacing unconditional warning/error
prints with optional info output changes diagnostic visibility, including
severity and suppression. Validation, return codes, reference transitions,
cleanup, BR_ERROR delivery and extended-error updates remain outside the mask.
Later per-callsite rate limits remain in force, so repeated malformed requests
cannot produce an unlimited stream from one callsite. This is not a lossless
security audit log. The DeathNotification category is disabled by default;
enabling it exposes existing cookie/handle metadata through printk access
policy. No transaction payload or dereferenced user address is formatted.

Worker cancellation records use a captured owning PID in ThreadError,
DeliverCode and FreezeMessage instead of misidentifying the worker. Those scalar
snapshots are diagnostic and can outlive PID reuse; they are not authorization
identities. Constructor argument changes preserve the same work ownership,
skip flag, return codes and cancellation behavior.

ErrorLocation stores a static reference and formats only basename:line. Its
transparent Option permits None to be zero initialized without creating a null
reference. Active module pinning keeps the static location alive. Track-caller
constructor/closure changes retain reply/errno values. Option<Error> likewise
uses the already reviewed NonZeroI32 representation; None maps to numeric zero
in log output and Some retains the original negative errno. There is no change
to public error structure layout.

Consolidated failure printing uses the final TransactionInfo and still requires
an actual source errno, retaining the relevant LTS fix. Failed replies keep their
remote failure while returning transaction-complete to the replying caller.
TF_CLEAR_BUF and error cleanup remain unchanged. Ring histories and netlink
reports are independent of the debug mask/rate limit. Source-equivalent build
100 verifies compilation; no logging stress, debug toggle or cancellation run
was performed. rust-binder-debug-review.json records every candidate and the
built module symbol/hash evidence. No additional remote resource was accessed.

## Rust Binder selection, polling and mapping locks

The six interface candidates in `evidence/rust-binder-interface-review.json`
are retained with the earlier selector, native trace cleanup and module-lifetime
adaptations. Local `system/extras/kcmdlinemodprobe` positively consumes the
selector's parameter and its EPERM/EINVAL distinction. The produced Rust module
imports all eleven relevant selector/core/LSM symbols and the image exports them
as GPL symbols. This does not establish which implementation an SDK boot selects.
Native trace implementation and private header remain byte-for-byte at LTS;
resource teardown is guarded against failed built-in initialization, and an
activated Rust module remains pinned because it has no complete teardown path.
Failure after unloading C still does not restore C automatically.

For page insertion, optimistic VMA locking falls back to the mmap read lock.
The shrinker instead skips a contended VMA, without blocking under its LRU lock.
Both preserve native ownership/mixedmap checks, asynchronous mm release and the
range mutex that serializes insertion against unmapping. LTS's mutex lifetime
protection remains in the destructor and shrinker. The selected build enables
per-VMA locks; with that configuration disabled the changed shrinker would skip
these pages. No mapping/reclaim race or contention benchmark was run.

Local libbinder supports polling. The process-owned queue registers its waiter
before checking readiness; process work, directed transactions and replies wake
pollers, which recheck their own readiness. Lazy allocation happens outside the
node-reference spinlock. The previously reviewed SetOnce publication and embedded
RCU destructor preserve visibility and defer freeing after pollfree, including
when a redundant allocation loses publication. Removing the thread's synchronous
RCU destructor does not remove the grace period for poll waiters.

TF_DEFER_COMPLETE remains an opt-in Rust reply behavior. Deferred thread work is
flushed before process work, on nonblocking reads and when a signal or looper
return requires leaving the wait. Registration removal precedes the final
thread-queue check on interruption. The checked-out libbinder sendReply still
waits for completion, and exact SDK adoption of the new flag is unconfirmed and
recorded. Retention preserves the current driver's generic UAPI; it does not
assert future-platform necessity. A caller that opts in while retaining resources
can delay their release until further work arrives; the kernel does not enforce
the proposed userspace rule that only replies without owned objects/fds use it.
C Binder's behavior remains unchanged. No syscall reduction or runtime epoll
coverage is claimed.

The TransactionInfo consolidation is already largely present in native LTS
`e5049526a7aa`; per-file changed-line comparisons record its small ACK-specific
priority and Zeroable adaptations. The port keeps the native extended-error
fixes and actual-errno logging guard. These internal metadata changes impose no
historical layout contract. Build 100 covers this source-equivalent batch; its
compilation and artifact checks do not establish runtime behavior.

## Binder merge resolution and VFS namespace imports

Merge `01df5435d964` has two unique resolution hunks. The Binder hunk combines
native VMA ownership checking with Android unmap tracing; the final per-VMA-lock
path retains both. The exit hunk preserves native kthread early termination before
profile_task_exit and KCOV/KMSAN cleanup. The profile notifier has a concrete UID
accounting consumer, with framework readers for /proc/uid_cputime and /proc/uid_io.
Its presence is not inferred only from a hook name. The merge's first-parent
changes are not counted as additional unique resolution code.

The complete `fd2cbc1b7ab7` patch contains only 82 export replacements and 76
namespace imports. `349c8bdc72bb` restores the two missing ext4/F2FS imports after
native filesystem reconciliation. The SDK builds these filesystems in, but the
module declarations keep the selected source-module policy consistent without
adding hardware support. Existing modpost and loader namespace validation remains.
An import declaration does not establish that a module is a filesystem and is
not a security boundary against arbitrary trusted kernel code.

ACK changes kern_path, generic_file_open, kernel_read and kernel_write to GPL-only
exports. That source policy is retained. Its opposite change to vfs_splice_read
would remove the native LTS GPL-only flag, so `ca332f5bd33e` uses
EXPORT_SYMBOL_NS_GPL instead. Explicit namespace imports are not a replacement
for native license verification. No VFS implementation, permission check, splice
bounds check or userspace ABI changes with these metadata corrections. Historical
vendor binary compatibility is not preserved. Alternate filesystem module configs
and runtime loading are untested; the SDK builds check the selected integration.

## WrapFD driver bounds and ownership

The driver and its use-state follow-up are retained for the selected 64-bit MMU
SDK configuration. The local libwrapfd library opens /dev/wrapfd and exposes the
same fixed-width ioctl records. Checked-out SELinux policy permits device creation
and LOAD to system_server, while apps receive DMA-buffer ioctls and designated
HALs receive the ownership/guest subset. These source observations are not proof
of runtime policy enforcement. The driver preserves anonymous-inode LSM creation,
FD reservation, native DMA mapping/cache synchronization and VFS read verification.

`328a500f0012` repairs several input and I/O boundary failures. Zero-length loads
validate the destination and return without allocating an empty request list or
waiting for a completion that cannot occur. Internal nonempty requests check
aligned file extents against signed offset limits and destination arithmetic
against pointer bounds. Request splitting uses native MAX_RW_COUNT, derived from
INT_MAX even on 64-bit, and is clamped to at least one page. Extreme module parameter
values therefore cannot produce a zero divisor or an oversized VFS request.

A subrequest entirely in the tail bounce pages must not enter the direct-buffer
segment. The missing upper-bound predicate previously allowed subtraction past
that segment's end, and potentially an out-of-range pointer calculation. The port
checks both ends. Each request now records the minimum bytes needed through the
requested file end, so an earlier short read cannot be concealed by another
request returning alignment padding. Errors are recorded while every queued
request still completes before file, buffer or request storage is released.
An error can still leave partial destination writes; LOAD is not transactional.

The publication failure path previously dropped the new Wrap while holding the
same inner mutex that its destructor acquires. The port extracts content, unlocks,
then destroys the failed wrapper. A failed rewrap also restores the previous
write permission before returning content to the original wrapper. The underlying
file mode is immutable, so the previously valid protection remains restorable.
The old implementation could leave a failed permission upgrade in effect.

Device get/put calls now count matching successful acquisitions. Previously, two
gets by one device acquired two DMA-buffer references, but the first put cleared
the owner; the second put then failed and ownership could be reassigned early.
The port retains the owner until the final matching put and checks count growth.
This does not relax the caller's obligation to keep the file and device valid,
and a device reference does not guarantee that a driver remains bound. Task-owner
mm checks now use a single volatile read, without dereferencing a potentially
exiting mm pointer. The existing group-leader ownership model is retained.

The use-state guard keeps LOAD outside the content mutex while blocking rewrap,
empty and new ownership acquisition during active use. Mmap's deferred task work
keeps the pending counter alive until syscall return; wrapper destruction clears
its internal raw file pointer. Read-only mapping and remap_file_pages protection
remain. LOAD still does not require ownership, matching ACK; checked-out apps/HAL
policy excludes it. Concurrent LOADs, source-file mutation, task-group leader exit
and actual DMA/mmap races remain unverified. The supplied ACK header also declares
is_wrapfd_vma without a driver definition. No local common-module caller was found,
but that is not proof of dispensability and no working export is claimed.

Three extracted-function harnesses pass: 3,886 I/O geometry/completion cases,
72 publication/protection cases and 3,488 device ownership assertions. The original
ACK controls fail 911 cases, 25 cases and 1,612 assertions respectively. Geometry
checks compare every simulated destination byte and surrounding sentinels. Host
allocation/file/lock/completion shims do not reproduce actual asynchronous kernel
I/O; the mutex shim detects recursive locking rather than hanging. The four exact
ACK selftest files are retained after review, including mapped/unaligned loads,
ownership and remapping tests, but were not executed or claimed to pass. These
selftests contain pre-existing weak assertions/unchecked string termination; the
focused host checks are distinct evidence. See `evidence/wrapfd-driver-review.json`.

Build 102 passed at `328a500f0012`, including both images, 100 common modules
and 45 virtual-device modules; all signatures and module flag/CRC tables passed
validation. System.map contains both WrapFD get/put C entry points, but Module.symvers
does not export them to loadable modules. The supplied ACK has the same mechanism:
`#[export]` supplies linkage/type checking and the automatic GPL export generator
only scans the Rust core/kernel/bindings objects. Built-in linkage is verified;
loadable-module access is not claimed. No new export policy is inferred from the
absence of an SDK vendor caller. The generated VFS symbol record independently
confirms that `vfs_splice_read` retains its native GPL-only flag and ACK namespace.

## C Binder vendor hook history and destruction

Twenty candidate patches cover the historical Binder hooks, their four reverts,
and current replacements. The old separate/consolidated wakeup hooks are absent
from both final ACK and port. Current wait/receive callbacks are distinct; current
priority restoration uses a transaction-only callback plus a separate NULL idle
notification. All 31 current declarations have GPL exports and C callsites.
Runtime vendor/OEM fields are retained for actual source extension state, without
historical binary layout preservation. Exact SDK callback consumers are unconfirmed;
that is not a basis for removing these generic Android source interfaces.

These callbacks execute trusted kernel code. Queue selection/insertion callbacks
must preserve list ownership, locking, nonempty selected lists and exactly-once
enqueue/dequeue. Spawn callbacks can exceed the normal thread maximum; allocation
callbacks can alter async budgets; priority callbacks can suppress restoration.
Those policies can affect fairness, memory consumption and availability. The port
retains the requested customization and records these limits rather than treating
callbacks as security enforcement. Unregistered defaults follow the native paths.
Native transaction LSM checks still follow the pre-transaction observation hook;
observation does not imply authorization. The proc-free hook runs after native
task/credential/dbitmap release and must only access still-live vendor state.
Callbacks must coordinate registration lifetimes and cannot retain unowned pointers.

`761834a35e49` fixes missing vendor transaction destruction notifications. Normal
transaction destruction invoked the free hook, while transaction error unwinding
and outdated asynchronous replacement freed the transaction directly. State allocated
by init/OEM hooks could therefore leak on repeated invalid or superseded transactions.
The port notifies before buffer teardown on errors and replacement, and also covers
early errors before a buffer or OEM allocation exists. A local flag avoids a second
notification when buffer-error labels fall through to the common error path.
Callbacks must tolerate absent OEM state and a NULL buffer on early failures.
The existing normal destruction hook remains after the native locked target snapshot
and temporary reference acquisition. Native outdated-buffer failure release and FD
fixup cleanup are unchanged, as are LSM context sizing and transaction permissions.

The extracted C destruction function, all 15 error entry labels, and outdated
replacement block pass 40 host cases. The previous source fails 80 assertions.
Checks cover exactly one hook invocation, no simulated vendor allocation left at
transaction free, hook timing before buffer destruction, and native failure/fixup
cleanup. Locks, allocation and refcounts use host shims; no Binder syscall, actual
vendor module, kernel fault injection or concurrency stress was executed.
Per-candidate evidence is in `evidence/c-binder-vendor-review.json` and the focused
results are in `evidence/binder-vendor-cleanup-check.json`.

WrapFD's Bazel selftest rule and x86_64 package/Tradefed entry remain. The rule matches
the candidate exactly. ARM64 packaging is outside the existing SDK build subset;
`81e18ca6effc` also removes this candidate's ARM64 XML entry. This retains test
metadata for the selected driver without introducing future-platform execution.
The WrapFD selftest binary and runtime test have not been run.

## Android configuration and removed telemetry history

Seven decisions are detailed in `evidence/android-history-config-review.json`.
The temporary vendor-hook disable was exactly reversed; x86_64 hooks remain
selected and build successfully. Help-syntax repairs are retained for current
vendor-hook/DM_BOW entries; the other two declarations were removed in final ACK.
Vendor/OEM runtime storage and task zero-initialization remain configurable and
enabled for the SDK. Historical KABI reserve padding and its header are omitted.
Child extension state is cleared before the dup-task vendor notification; no
binary layout compatibility is promised by the retained runtime fields.

ACK removed the memhealth telemetry driver and all configuration/build/proc ABI
registrations. Final ACK and port both lack those files and references. The
original export of mark_victim was removed with it, but later ACK commit
`3a74b921c02b` independently restored that GPL tracepoint for vendor modules;
that generic export stays. Scoped local lmkd/framework-AM/SELinux searches found
no memhealth references, but the removal decision rests on explicit final ACK
history, not a claim that a search proves no consumer exists. No native OOM
selection or termination behavior is removed by these historical metadata choices.

Binder's exhaustive allocator KUnit case retains the slow annotation. It keeps
the same test function while affecting native speed filtering, warning behavior
and timeout multiplier. Build 103 produced the selected test module; the test
was not executed. Compilation does not establish runtime correctness.

## Debug-kinfo target selection and CFI rename

Eleven decisions in `evidence/debug-kinfo-source-review.json` account for the
bootloader debug-kinfo relocation, fixes and metadata evolution, historical
configuration disable, and mixed CFI rename. The driver requires a
`google,debug-kinfo` device, memory-region phandle and pre-mapped reserved memory.
Pinned ACK's x86_64 GKI configuration does not select it, its Kconfig has no default
enablement, and the exhaustive ACK config search enables it only for ARM64. The
existing SDK build selects that x86_64 configuration; checked-out virtual-device
fragments do not enable it. Its source, packed bootloader record and registration
remain omitted. This does not remove native kallsyms or module-loading functionality.
CONFIG_OF and KALLSYMS are enabled; no claim is made that those subsystems are unused
or that every externally customized emulator firmware lacks the interface.

Driver-only fixes for repeated-write unmapping, bounded string copying and new
native module/symbol layouts are omitted with the driver, without retaining their
unsafe historical implementations. Native LTS module_memory and relative symbol
tables remain. Historical disabled status on every architecture is not preserved.

The mixed rename retains CONFIG_CFI in x86_64 and omits unselected ARM64/Microdroid
configuration and debug-kinfo edits. virtio_dma_buf remains exactly at LTS: callback
identity must match before the object is treated as a virtio buffer. The historical
ACK CFI+MODULES exception would report every DMA buffer as virtio and skip exporter
validation, potentially allowing an invalid extended ops interpretation. Current
modules are rebuilt using the same KCFI toolchain, so historical binary CFI behavior
is not preserved. Build 103 compiled the actual module set; runtime DMA-buffer
interoperability and emulator firmware behavior remain unverified.

## Module SCM history and build merge resolutions

Four decisions in `evidence/module-history-merge-review.json` account for the
original SCMVERSION feature/removal and two merges containing nine unique hunks.
The temporary removal was superseded by the current implementation. The bounded
modpost value is optional, emitted only after the Makefile revision filter succeeds.
Native MODINFO_ATTR duplicates it, exposes a read-only sysfs value only when present,
and frees it during module teardown. It does not relax signatures or version checks.

All 145 build-103 module .modinfo sections were inspected: none contains scmversion,
even though MODULE_SCMVERSION=y. Retained Kleaf stamp.bzl emits -maybe-dirty without
stamping and omits optional external-module localversion under that setting; this
is consistent with the revision filter producing no SCM value. No stamped build
or runtime sysfs inspection was performed. The immutable source/dependency pins
and artifact hashes in this audit remain the actual build-provenance evidence.

The module Kconfig merge retains native menu relocation and current list-based
Android signed-module export protection. The Kbuild merge retains native shared
.module-common.o metadata, BTF/link/install paths and builtin range support, with
current source-module generation and independently reviewed modules.order metadata.
Native shared metadata supplies retpoline information; the removed per-module
copy is not reintroduced. Old DT include overrides and historical mixed-prefix
syntax are not required by the selected SDK build. Native DT rules and external
module documentation remain. Build 103 verifies the actual common/virtual-device
workflow, not every historical standalone make or out-of-tree DT build mode.

### OverlayFS history and native permission model

`evidence/overlay-history-review.json` records 26 candidate decisions, including
32 unique resolution hunks from four merges. The five versions of the old
`override_creds=off` credential bypass were repeatedly removed and restored,
then finally removed by ACK `8e21ce25c1f9`. Retain the native LTS permission
model. This decision has positive local userspace evidence: fs_mgr uses
`mount(2)` and adds `userxattr`; init executes `overlay_remounter`, which remounts
overlays under a domain with explicit permissions for the underlying partitions
on debuggable builds. The native `override_creds` flag has a different purpose:
it records credentials through the new mount API. No Android OS build, remount,
SELinux policy execution or emulator boot was performed.

The eight xattr-history commits add/remove an extra get-handler flags argument
and a security-check bypass. The final ACK no longer has that interface. Keep
native checked overlay xattr calls with mount idmaps, ACL handling and the LTS
escaped-xattr prefix fix. The review covers all affected callback paths; it does
not treat unrelated retained changes in those files as part of the removed
interface. Historical audit-log issues were not reproduced. Embedded bug and
report links in commit messages were not opened.

LTS overlay source is retained exactly, except for the separately audited VFS
namespace import in `super.c`. This preserves source-mounter read permission
checks for cross-superblock copies, linked-dentry tracking after copy-up, the
casefold error-path reference fix, native backing-file LSM state, and corrected
lock-order documentation. `d74c6ea9965e` restores the native verity lazy-load guard:
ACK's guard fix compensates for its hashtable implementation, but this port
retains native inode-info offsets and `fsverity_active() ==
(fsverity_get_info() != NULL)`. The original native expression is equivalent
under those retained semantics and preserves acquire publication.

Merge review also checks the mixed power/LSM/IPsec resolution: native battery
health values already represent those additions, LOCKDOWN audit data is kept,
and ESP selects SEQIV through the native central XFRM option. The struct-fd merge
preserves canonical-path inotify routing alongside the native fd accessor;
FUSE is a concrete provider. That merge decision does not certify all provider
runtime behavior. No security callback or permission check is removed by these
resolutions.

### Canonical filesystem notifications

`evidence/fsnotify-canonical-review.json` resolves seventeen candidates covering
canonical-path history, vendor notification hooks and four merge resolutions.
The pinned MediaProvider implements `pf_canonical_path`, and pinned CTS checks
FileObserver open/modify/close events on external storage. Retain the runtime
callback and inotify routing, with the final error-return contract, native fd
cleanup, original-path permission/LSM checks and held backing-path references.
The old void callback, disabled code and intermediate bad merge are superseded.
Native RTC and fanotify code are kept exactly for mixed merge resolutions.

`0d3cecaef139` fixes notification loss in the ACK integration. An open event is
post-operation and its caller ignores the return value, but ACK returned early
when canonical lookup failed, suppressing the native event as well. The port
always sends the native original-path notification, attempts the optional lower
notification only on successful lookup, and avoids sending the same path twice.
Inotify watch registration still propagates daemon/ENOMEM failures; ENOSYS keeps
its documented original-path fallback. The stale permission-priority shortcut
in fsnotify_file is removed; native pre-content/open/access/mmap/truncate
permission handling remains unchanged. Zero-size canonical replies still fail
with EBADMSG, and bounded NUL termination is preserved before daemon-context
lookup by the earlier `9e4d89fab8be` fix.

`check_fsnotify_canonical.py` extracts the actual notification functions and the
inotify canonical-path tail, using instrumented path references and event
callbacks. All 1,890 cases pass; the previous source fails 202 assertions.
These host checks do not execute kernel FUSE transport, inotify queues, SELinux,
concurrent operations or CTS. The source provider's authorization policy is not
certified: MediaProvider still has a TODO for uid access to path contents beyond
its is_app_accessible_path check. Canonical routing intentionally connects
stacked views, so trusted providers must return authorized backing paths.

The generic GPL-exported `android_vh_fsnotify_open` hook remains available even
though an SDK vendor callback has not been established. Its mutable mask lets a
trusted callback change or suppress post-open observation; native access-control
checks remain separate. A callback receives a borrowed file pointer and must
respect its lifetime. No historical dentry binary layout is preserved. All
sources used for these decisions are local pinned checkouts; embedded links in
commit messages and CTS comments were not accessed.
# Fscrypt history and legacy wrapped-key compatibility

The fifteen decisions in `evidence/fscrypt-history-compat-review.json` retain
native ESSIV handling, filesystem-owned post-read decryption, nonce retrieval,
IV_INO_LBLK policies and modern direct I/O. Six complete files and six functions
are byte-identical to pinned LTS. The old page-count direct-I/O implementation
was explicitly removed because it did not handle multipage bvecs correctly;
native block-count limits and ext4/F2FS iomap callers preserve DUN wrap boundaries.
The native IV_INO_LBLK_32 warning about IV reuse remains. Neither optimized IV
policy is selected by the SDK's pinned raw AES-XTS/CTS data configuration.
The sdcardfs key-removal notifier follows its explicit historical removal;
this does not remove current FUSE canonical notifications.

Native stable commit `196a8f954ae6` already supplies ciphertext bioset splitting.
The selected memory-pressure adaptation remains `e2fc28947c38`: avoid blocking
allocations while holding unsubmitted bounce pages or in recursive submission,
submit partially filled bios with a remaining reference, and retry empty
allocation failures on the reclaim-capable worker. Residual source position and
DUN travel together. The native per-page mempool implementation is retained;
the ACK bulk API/refill optimization is not required by this implementation.
Native early encryption alignment checks remain before hardware or fallback
submission. These are source-level findings, not fault-injection results.

The retained `df5cdcc556fb` legacy wrapped-key adapter is a deliberate security
exception to native key-identifier domain separation. Pinned
`system/vold/KeyUtil.cpp` emits `__FSCRYPT_ADD_KEY_FLAG_HW_WRAPPED` for
`kHwWrappedV0`, while `kHwWrapped` uses the upstream flag. Its source explicitly
distinguishes their on-disk formats. Pinned `system/extras/libfscrypt/fscrypt.cpp`
parses both options. Preserving this current Android userspace/on-disk interface
is the reason for retaining the small adapter; it is not binary vendor KMI.
Its UAPI consumes the last reserved word without changing the structure size.

For legacy requests only, the derived hardware software-secret uses the raw-key
identifier KDF context. Consequently, someone who obtains that software-secret
can add it as a raw key and obtain the same identifier. Native existing-key
lookup is identifier-based, so this can affect user claims and key removal or
re-addition semantics. This review does not claim that it exposes the separate
hardware contents-encryption key. Modern wrapped-key requests retain native
identifier separation. Unknown/conflicting flags, non-v2 wrapped-key requests,
key-size and provisioning-payload mismatches remain rejected. Native capability
checks, secret wiping and the newer stable per-user claim implementation remain.

The selected goldfish `/data` fstab uses raw AES-XTS/CTS on virtio storage, without
`inlinecrypt` or a wrapped-key option. Native secret derivation requires an
inline-crypto mount and a block profile supporting hardware-wrapped keys and
secret derivation; the software fallback supports raw keys only. Thus the
checked configuration does not activate the legacy path. Its SDK necessity and
runtime operation are not established. The generic Android interface is retained
conservatively, with this uncertainty recorded in
`evidence/unresolved-feature-questions.json`; no future-platform driver is added.
The report pins all three local Android projects. No additional remote resource
was accessed. Build 105 compiled this source, but no encrypted filesystem,
hardware-key operation, direct I/O or memory-pressure runtime test was executed.

`evidence/wrapped-key-version-review.json` traces the initial 2020 fscrypt
implementation, its 2021 temporary removal and restoration, and the 2025
refresh through five complete candidate patches. The temporary removals are
superseded; replaying them would delete current native functionality. Current
provisioning keys retain native type/flag binding, so a payload for one key type
cannot be silently reused as another. Native key-size checks, per-mode lifetime,
data-unit sizing and modern wrapped-key documentation remain. The refreshed
patch is explicitly marked partially adapted because its identifier separation
has the legacy exception described above; the ledger does not claim unconditional
preservation of that fix. Seven additional native witnesses and unchanged
earlier source blobs support this review. No runtime crypto claim is added.
# Encryption interfaces and mempool prerequisites

`evidence/crypto-mempool-interface-review.json` records nineteen further
decisions, including all 22 unique hunks of six merges. Current block-device
interfaces, hardware-secret derivation, raw-only fallback and fscrypt key-type
selection retain native LTS behavior. Five GPL crypto exports are present in
build 105's symbol table. Obsolete queue-based APIs are replaced by current
source interfaces; old module binaries are not supported.

The original encrypted/casefold dentry integration is represented by native
mount-time `generic_set_sb_d_ops`, before root dentry creation in ext4, F2FS and
UBIFS. Native no-key-name revalidation, RCU retry and flag handling remain;
historical per-lookup dentry-operations installation is not restored.
Twenty-three complete-file, function or call-site witnesses bind these findings
to pinned LTS and the reviewed source.

Mempool keeps native reserve locking, paired barriers, reclaim retry, wakeups,
and zero-minimum-pool handling. Internal helper extractions and bulk-array APIs
are unnecessary for the selected per-page crypto rescue implementation; their
forward-progress purpose is addressed by `e2fc28947c38`, as described above.
This is not a finding that all possible vendor sources lack bulk API consumers.
The SDK disables fault injection, so the additional debugfs injection facility
is omitted. Existing native kvmalloc-pool source helpers remain, avoiding an
unnecessary source API removal. Comment-only patches do not require runtime
changes. No fault injection or actual encrypted-filesystem test was executed.

The legacy wrapped-key merge retains the previously documented KDF exception
and current-target uncertainty. No new source changes or remote sources were
needed for this group; build 105 still covers the resulting kernel source.
# Encryption capability and DUN safety history

Twelve further decisions in `evidence/crypto-safety-history-review.json` cover
key-size validation, crypto-context merging, hardware capabilities, runtime-PM
ordering, wrapped-key ioctls and all sixteen unique hunks of three historical
merges. Twenty-one native file/function/table comparisons support the decisions.

Native key objects are immutable and keyslots use their identity; the old
content-hash/size comparison implementation is not restored. Native DUN merge
checks compare every limb and reject whole-DUN wrap. Explicit key-type and DUN
width checks, raw-only software fallback, missing-algorithm errors, capability
intersection and native keyslot lifetime/error handling remain. Runtime PM
resumes before taking the crypto-profile lock, preserving the deadlock fix.

The functional DUN-width changes in `770581355179` are retained through native
fields and APIs, while its historical hash-field KMI packing is omitted.
Integrated dm-default-key uses the current APIs and the previously reviewed
`2d4754f38b2b` checked IV range and nonzero DUN width. Old queue signatures and
the rejected profile-pointer compatibility test are not reinstated.

Native import/generate/prepare wrapped-key ioctls retain input bounds, reserved
field checks, output-capacity checks, stack-secret wiping and provider gates.
Provider callbacks remain trusted kernel code with native return-size contracts;
this source review does not certify hardware implementations. UFS changes in
this group are already native LTS, and no future-platform code is added.
The SDK build verifies compilation only: encrypted I/O, PM transitions and key
ioctls were not executed. No remote sources were accessed.

# Device-mapper encryption history

Fourteen decisions in `evidence/dm-crypto-history-review.json` cover the original

OEM storage does not preserve historical offsets, and vendor code must respect

The 117-path temporary block rollback and its restoration cancel exactly: the

of upstream fixes; parent candidates still have independent dispositions.
Fourteen native file/function witnesses support this group. Build 105 covers

# Encryption merge history

Four historical merges in `evidence/crypto-merge-history-review.json` are reviewed
across all unique resolution hunks, including changes outside conflict markers.
Native block submission, merge accounting and request-clone failure cleanup
remain. Android metadata-encryption skip state follows cloned bios and raw
F2FS GC I/O, and mismatched skip states cannot merge. Native wrapped-key checks
and the separately documented fscrypt legacy identifier exception remain.

Native per-mode keys now publish initialized nodes through an RCU list, with
master-key lifetime and setup serialization; the older pointer publication
scheme is not reinstated. Native current mount-option parsing and dummy-policy
checks remain, and the final-ACK removal of key-removal notifiers is honored.
The previously assessed asynchronous zeroout implementation retains sector/DUN
geometry, completion ownership and error aggregation. Sixteen native witnesses
and unchanged earlier fscrypt blobs support this group. Build 105 covers the
unchanged source. No new runtime verification or remote source was used.

# Initial inline-encryption integration

Two decisions in `evidence/fscrypt-initial-refresh-review.json` account for the

Current UFS core/crypto headers and sources are identical to pinned LTS. The

# Original block crypto and metadata encryption

The initial block-crypto and dm-default-key patches are resolved in
`evidence/block-crypto-initial-review.json`. Native block crypto preserves
request keyslot lifetime, alignment and type validation, synchronous ciphers
and completion handling. Read fallback saves its iterator and context, removes
the submitted context and restores callbacks before completing decryption.
The existing allocation rescue and Android skip flags retain their separate
justifications. Fifteen native or previously tested source witnesses support
this group.

Pinned goldfish fstab, vold and libdm provide positive metadata-encryption
consumers. The retained target matches their modern table syntax and preserves
redaction, sensitive cleanup, checked IV bounds and crypto-sector alignment.
Its skip policy assumes file keys have at least the protection of metadata
keys; skip flags remain kernel-controlled state. The complete target is
unchanged since its recorded extracted host tests. These tests and build 105
do not establish runtime encryption, wrapped-hardware operation or bootability.
Only local sources were used.

verification is claimed. Only local sources were used.

# Inline-encryption upstream refresh

The 52-file refresh `c2b86b727a41` is resolved in
`evidence/inline-upstream-refresh-review.json`. Native request contexts and

registration rejects unsupported combinations; current queue-limit updates
return an error instead of silently removing an active crypto profile.

Current fscrypt setup, multi-device checks and F2FS crypto/physical merge checks
are retained. Upstream `9dad5feb49a5` supersedes the old local NOFS wrapper:
key setup may load crypto modules and acquire locks whose owners allocate
with GFP_KERNEL, so this port does not claim NOFS-safe key setup. The existing
Android default-key, skip-state, asynchronous zeroout and allocation-rescue
adaptations keep their separate security analyses. UFS host/core files remain
exactly LTS, with the native custom-profile interface instead of historical
crypto-vops. No new physical-platform support is introduced.

Native equality and unchanged earlier-review witnesses are recorded. Build
105 covers compilation, not the runtime tests reported in the old commit
message. No remote source was accessed for this review.

# Initial Android 6.18 padding sync

`5e17f93a5c22` is historical KMI-only. Exhaustive changed-line classification
across its 142 paths found only reserved members/capability numbers, the ABI

`evidence/initial-padding-sync-review.json` records every path and patch hash.

security check is omitted, and no remote resource was accessed.

# Task vendor storage and native x86 state

Eleven task/scheduler storage commits are resolved in
`evidence/task-vendor-storage-review.json`. Runtime vendor/OEM fields are
retained in their final source form; obsolete intermediate WALT arrays and
sizes are superseded. These fields differ from unused historical KABI
reserves. Exact SDK custom callback use remains unconfirmed after the scoped
local searches recorded in the report; compatibility-first retention does
not treat a missing consumer as proof of irrelevance.

`85c809fbcecd` corrects the dynamic task extension for native x86 allocation.
The old `p + 1` accessor points at x86 FPU state, and allocation size minus
`sizeof(task_struct)` counts native architecture state as vendor bytes.
Record the requested vendor count separately, finish native architecture
sizing and usercopy whitelist calculation, then append aligned vendor bytes
before creating the task cache. Preserve native FPU layout, cloning and
usercopy limits. Initialize only vendor bytes before the vendor callback.
Size and offset exports become read-only after init; overflow rejects the
extra allocation. The initial task uses its separate GPL-exported buffer,
rounded up for nonmultiple configured maxima. No LTS fix is reversed.

Default zero request leaves native allocation size unchanged. SDK config
enables the interface with a 512-byte maximum; this is compile evidence,
not an assertion of a measured boot option. The existing memparse syntax
and permissiveness remain; malicious boot-option parsing was not certified.
The focused host check executes extracted layout/access/init functions over
14,378 cases, including native-tail canaries, alignment, integer limits and
initial-task bounds. Three regression controls fail as expected. It does
not execute a kernel fork, FPU switch or emulator boot. Build 106 passed at
this source commit with both images and all 145 common/virtual modules; all
module signatures and flag/CRC tables passed their separate checks. No remote resource was accessed.

# IRQ and network vendor storage

Six declaration/history commits are resolved in
`evidence/irq-network-vendor-storage-review.json`. Retain final IRQ, conntrack,
socket and skb shared-info runtime fields; omit generated ABI descriptions
and unused KABI reserves. Exact SDK custom consumers remain unconfirmed, so
small generic source interfaces are retained conservatively. IRQ descriptors
and normal sockets are zero-initialized; native conntrack tail initialization
includes its OEM field without disturbing the typesafe-by-RCU protocol.

Socket cloning copies opaque state; retained allocation/clone/free callbacks
remain responsible for any pointer ownership. The skb shared-info OEM array
lies after dataref and is not guaranteed zero by the generic initializer.
Vendor code must initialize it before reading or sharing and manage shared
lifetime. This review does not invent automatic initialization or certify
unknown module implementations. Native LSM, RCU, dataref and fragment handling
remain. Build 106 covers compilation only. No remote resource was accessed.

# SELinux source interfaces and status mapping

Five commits are resolved in `evidence/selinux-interface-review.json`. Native
binder genfs labeling includes the corrected filesystem name and is used by
pinned Android per-device policy. The old lockdown class-order cleanup is superseded by upstream removal of
SELinux lockdown in `f5d0e5e9d72d`; native audit and current class definitions
remain. This does not claim SELinux still implements the removed lockdown
policy checks. Four restricted AVC lifecycle/lookup hooks retain their
const borrowed node interfaces and GPL exports. Native locks, RCU lifetime,
sequence checks and permission decisions are unchanged. Trusted callbacks
must respect those contexts and lifetimes; exact SDK registrations remain
unconfirmed, so generic compatibility is retained conservatively.

`0951b28655b0` corrects status-page physical overmapping. ACK accepts a larger
reported-page VMA and passes that full size to remap_pfn_range, but both ACK
and LTS allocate only one native status page. In 16 KiB mode that can expose
three adjacent unowned physical pages to a caller allowed to map status.
Keep the reported-size acceptance while mapping only the owned native page.
Offset rejection, write denial, removal of VM_MAYWRITE and remap error
propagation remain. Padding stays unmapped and uses native SIGBUS handling;
there is no new zero-page alias or additional physical allocation.

The pinned SDK has a page_shift=14 command-line option. Pinned libselinux
maps the sysconf page size and reads the five-u32 status structure at its
start, which remains mapped. This is source compatibility evidence, not a
live policy/reachability or emulator fault test. The extracted mmap check
passes 6,912 combinations; original ACK fails 96 mapping-size checks. Build
107 results are recorded separately. All evidence is local; no remote
resource was accessed, including links embedded in historical messages.

# Native SELinux netlink permissions

Eighteen historical changes and unique merge resolutions are assessed in

the extended-permission AVC path. Pinned Android policy enables that
capability; its ordinary netdomain message set excludes GETLINK, GETNEIGH
and GETNEIGHTBL while explicitly retaining older-app neighbor exceptions.
This is positive replacement-policy evidence, not an absence-of-consumer
inference or a promise of compatibility with obsolete non-xperm policy.

Native parsing, unsupported-message handling and unknown-extended-permission
warning paths remain. Keep later NLMSG type validation and AVTAB_ENABLED
masking; do not restore BUG on future permission types. Merge resolutions
retain native policy bounds, load-state/hash-stat interfaces and netif
capabilities plus the separately audited initialization hook. The obsolete
XATTR_NOSECURITY argument stays removed; native xattr errors and genfs fallback
remain. Upstream f5d0e5e9d72d removed SELinux lockdown due to incorrect calling
credential assumptions, so old class-order merges do not justify restoring
that implementation. No LTS control is reversed. Build 107 covers compilation,
not policy compilation or live authorization. No remote resource was used.

# SELinux policy history and memfd class

`evidence/selinux-policy-history-review.json` resolves ten historical patches.

SECURITY_LOCKDOWN_LSM; this review does not claim active lockdown enforcement.

Retain the final capability-gated memfd security initialization and memfd_file
class. With the capability enabled, creation receives a task-derived transition
label and CREATE check before publication; errors release the file. Execution
uses the corresponding class permissions and preserves native NNP/nosuid,
process transition/share/ptrace and secureexec behavior. Class permissions are
policy-dependent; this is not a blanket prohibition on executable memfd files.
The capability-disabled SELinux path leaves legacy labeling. Native dispatcher
calls to other LSMs, flag validation and sealing remain.

Pinned policy includes memfd_file rules and pinned libsepol recognizes
memfd_class, but its activation was not established in the SDK assembled policy:
the examined capability file contains no such directive. This is recorded as an
unresolved runtime-activation question with a conservative final retention
choice for the current generic Android interface. No platform or policy changes
are added, and no OS build or runtime authorization test was performed. All
sources were local; embedded commit-message links were not accessed.

# Android idle timer integration

`evidence/idletimer-history-review.json` records eighteen historical changes and
