with --send_nl_msg and consumes INTERFACE, STATE, TIME_NS and UID activity events.
Retain this Android functionality with current v0/v1 UAPI layouts, native
family-specific registrations, input bounds and timer/work/sysfs lifetime rules.
The temporary global-family rollback is superseded; retain native IPv6 MARK/NFLOG
and TRACE-owner fixes and NFT_COMPAT_ARP support.

`f3f057ef8e9a` fixes an ACK null dereference: list deletion precedes sysfs removal,
so a sysfs read can find no timer. Copy notification mode only after successful
lookup under list_mutex and use the snapshot after unlock. Kernfs lifetime
protection does not guarantee that a timer remains in this list. This is not a
claim of a demonstrated use-after-free or an unprivileged exploit.

`f9788ed2879e` initializes the timer and work before publishing the PM callback.
Native blocking-notifier registration permits calls as soon as its lock is
released. The callback also skips jiffies adjustment for native ALARM_BOOTTIME,
which already includes suspend time and has no initialized regular timer.
Native shutdown, callback unregistration and work draining remain before free.

The extracted show check passes 644 cases and the original ACK control faults.
The constructor/resume check passes 91 cases; original ACK has 21 violations.
These are host shims, not live sysfs, suspend, notifications or concurrency.
Build108 verifies the first fix; build109 verifies both corrections and all
145 signed common/virtual-device modules. No remote
resources were accessed, including links embedded in historical commit messages.

# Landlock thread synchronization and obsolete sdcardfs exports

`evidence/landlock-and-sdcardfs-review.json` resolves the ten-commit TSYNC
series and two obsolete sdcardfs export patches. The configured Landlock ABI 8
and TSYNC implementation are retained, including queue-entry cleanup,
serialization and interrupted-operation fixes. The caller retains native
no_new_privs/CAP_SYS_ADMIN prerequisites, ruleset validation and layer limits.
No-new-privs propagates to sibling threads when set and is never cleared.
Different sibling credentials are cloned individually, replacing their Landlock
blob; identical credentials can share the new reference. Native filesystem,
network, task and credential hooks and other LSM state remain intact.

This API has a material security tradeoff: its explicit contract replaces
sibling Landlock domains and logging configurations regardless of their prior
restrictions. A less restricted sibling can relax another sibling's Landlock
configuration. Retaining ACK compatibility therefore gives sibling threads
shared policy authority; it does not preserve an assumption of irreversible,
independent Landlock confinement within a process. The logging-only fd=-1 form
also propagates the caller's Landlock configuration. This is an added opt-in
interface, not removal of a native access-control check or reversal of an LTS
fix. Allocation failure, task-reference lifetime, interruption cancellation,
completion barriers and trylock/restart handling were examined at source level;
no scheduler/concurrency or application-isolation runtime result is claimed.

The pinned bionic seccomp allowlist exposes the Landlock syscalls and mentions
conditional Chrome GPU probing. Its header lacks TSYNC, and scoped searches did
not establish an SDK TSYNC caller. That uncertainty is retained explicitly;
the current generic userspace interface is conservatively kept under the
compatibility priority rather than treating search absence as proof of no need.
The matching API documentation and tests are applied onto LTS without reverting
its newer test fixes. Three affected test programs compile against exported
port headers; none was run, and upstream coverage claims are not port results.

For sdcardfs, positive ACK removal history a96c98dd7a30 supersedes the six
historical module exports and the security_path_chown export-license change.
All six exports are absent at both pinned tips and in the port; native functions
and security dispatch remain. The rest of that large removal is not finalized
by this scoped review. All evidence is local; embedded source links were not
accessed.

# Wakeup report lifetime and LTS read-side protection

`evidence/wakeup-history-review.json` resolves eight wakeup-report patches and
the seventeen-path 0f2cb7cf8033 merge, including edits outside conflict markers.
Pinned SystemSuspend opens both reporting attributes, parses two durations and
reads wakeup lines after suspend attempts. Policy supplies matching read access.
Retain this generic Android interface and module exports; actual SDK suspend
activation is unverified, and virtual-device userspace can disable its suspend
loop when control files are unavailable. ARM GIC-only additions remain omitted.

`55607d0a35a3` fixes list records that retained driver-owned IRQ-name pointers.
Native free_irq permits name storage to be retired, and virtio_pci frees its
msix_names after unregistering IRQs. Capture now owns nonpermanent names with
kstrdup_const/GFP_ATOMIC and clearing either list releases them with kfree_const.
Permanent kernel names need no allocation; allocation failure reports the
permanent unknown name. Full sysfs names are preserved when copying succeeds.
Native direct IRQ capture holds the descriptor lock; source-module callers of
the threaded helper must keep the action/name live during capture. No descriptor
lock is newly taken under the reason lock. Capture is also disabled if node-cache
initialization failed, preventing a later s2idle clear from enabling NULL-cache
allocation. The fast capture-state read is marked READ_ONCE. The host extraction
check passes 76 cases; original ACK fails 27 ownership/fallback/capture checks.

`cfa8849595ba` preserves the LTS wakeup-source lifetime protocol in the ACK-added
report helper: use wakeup_srcu, because native removal waits on synchronize_srcu,
not ordinary RCU. Initialize nonzero-sized output even for an empty list and
avoid touching zero-sized output. Pass the composed message through a constant
%s format so device names cannot become varargs directives. This removes stale
source access, uninitialized report reads and format misuse paths without
changing native event counters, suspend decisions or source removal. The
extracted report/caller check passes 244 cases; original ACK fails 1020 explicit
shim contract/output assertions. These are host checks, not live IRQ/SRCU race
tests or exploit demonstrations.

Retain the later bounded printk snapshot outside reason/raw locks and signed
nonnegative duration reporting. The clock clamp is not a coherent multi-clock
snapshot guarantee. Current native epoll wait/signal handling, folio completion,
allocator watermark calculations (apart from the explicit trusted hook), and
freezer diagnostics are preserved in the reviewed merge. Obsolete Clang,
Cuttlefish, extra_free_kbytes and old tracing code is not revived. Separate DPM
watchdog parameter lifecycle and BPF reporting changes remain for their own
reviews. All sources used here were local; no embedded URL was accessed.

# DPM watchdog parameter lifetime

`evidence/dpm-watchdog-review.json` records four patches for generic watchdog
backtraces, sysctl timeouts, the pm parameter prefix and default enablement.
The SDK compiles the watchdog with 25-second fatal and 10-second warning
timeouts, but CONFIG_DPM_WATCHDOG_ENABLED is unset. Preserve these current
Android controls and configuration; no definite SDK knob-setting consumer was
established, so that uncertainty remains explicit. Disabling the watchdog means
a hung callback may lack this panic/diagnostic recovery mechanism.

`1cea64c5f402` corrects two unsafe effects of mutable global controls. Save the
enable decision in the stack instance before returning from setup. Cleanup then
uses that decision, avoiding both an uninitialized timer operation after enabling
and a surviving stack-referencing timer after disabling. Sample the timeout
values when arming, cap warning to the sampled fatal timeout and use those
instance values in the handler. Later updates cannot change an armed timer's
remaining-time subtraction/deadline. Independent global sysctl reads/writers are
not made transactional; normalization guarantees a bounded effective pair.
The documented runtime change takes effect for subsequent device callbacks.
Native synchronous timer deletion/destruction, warning/panic sequencing and
device callback control flow remain. Privileged control modes and existing
sysctl validation stay intact; no historical structure layout is preserved.

The extracted lifecycle/deadline check passes 7,500 cases; the original ACK
control produces 16,575 failing assertions. It exercises actual functions using
host timer, panic and parameter observers. It does not run kernel timers, PM
callbacks, parallel sysctl writes or an emulator. All source evidence is local.

# PM statistics and source interfaces

`evidence/pm-interfaces-review.json` records seven individual dispositions.
The suspend_stats relocation revert removed native locking introduced by
`a6d38e991dc4`, which predates v6.18. `76fb9cfd8c64` restores the exact native
out-of-line device-name recorder and mutex while preserving the visible data
needed by Android late/noirq abort reports. Concurrent writers otherwise race
on both the ring index and name buffer. This restoration preserves the Linux
fix; it does not promise coherent snapshots of all PM statistics. The newer
stable textual-overlap scan cannot establish preservation of pre-v6.18 fixes.

Retain the generic configured wait/probe hooks and wakeup-statistics GPL exports.
The relevant complete wait functions retain native control flow and cleanup;
trusted module callbacks remain responsible for context and pointer lifetime.
The exported seq-file opener requires the corresponding release operations and
appropriate permissions in any new endpoint. Exact SDK consumers were not
established, and conservative retention does not claim runtime necessity.

The BPF wakeup-source interface is retained with its configured SYSCALL-only
registration. KF_ACQUIRE/RELEASE lets the verifier track opaque SRCU tokens and
reject leaks or invalid releases. get_head returns a scalar address, not a
directly dereferenceable verified pointer. It does not require the token, so
pairing alone does not enforce traversal under SRCU. Kernel probe reads retain
native nofault behavior and capability/token/LSM checks. Loading syscall programs
requires CAP_BPF or the corresponding token authority; kernel probe reads
require CAP_PERFMON/token and pass the existing lockdown hook. Address exposure
and privileged traversal expand the surface. No runtime verifier test or
coherent snapshot guarantee is claimed.

`14fa69019d32` removes the physical out-of-band wake flag/helpers together with
their already omitted generic-domain power-off consumer. The generated SDK
does not enable PM_GENERIC_DOMAINS; the actual caller and pinned mainline TI/i.MX
setters describe a separate always-on physical domain topology. Keep native
ACPI/virtio-target PM behavior. Missing SDK search results alone are not the
basis for omission; no claim is made about unknown future platform consumers.

# PM runtime, freezer and snapshot customization

`evidence/pm-runtime-history-review.json` records twenty dispositions, including
five nonempty merge resolutions. The explicit prerequisite revert
`768546c00842` removed the native statistics lock before the data relocation
revert; the port rejects that removal and keeps the restored native lock.
Historical OF copying and mpage tracing are superseded in their final trees.
Native freezer diagnostics, s2idle checks, syscore cleanup and CPU-disable paths
remain alongside identified Android reports/observations.

The background filesystem-sync helper is serialized by the existing transition
mutex at all three call sites. A wake event may abort the wait while queued sync
continues; success waits for the pending count to reach zero. Suspend,
hibernation and snapshot ioctl retain native permission and cleanup paths.
This does not assert storage durability after an aborted attempt. ACK runtime
PM retries failed resume callbacks, while persistent suspend failures retain
runtime_error. Native status/locking, supplier references and NOIO protection
remain. Repeated driver callbacks are a deliberate error-recovery difference.

Generic freezer, resume and QoS hooks are retained conservatively with exact
SDK registrations unconfirmed. Early-resume callbacks run with IRQs disabled;
per-task freeze-failure callbacks run under tasklist_lock. QoS v2 skip and device
maximum-frequency rewrite are policy overrides, not passive observations: a
trusted vendor can alter thermal/power constraints and availability. No-hook
behavior and native request validation remain. Disabled generic-domain hooks
and non-target ARM64 CPU-resume integration are omitted with their consumers.

Snapshot/swap exports and generic transformation hooks are retained as optional
source interfaces in the SDK-configured hibernation subsystem. They do not
implement secure hibernation. The encryption-named hook operates only on
uncompressed data pages, excludes the header and compressed path, and has no
error return; the post-save hook also runs on failure. Skipping map writes may
invalidate native restoration. A trusted implementation would need a complete
format, integrity/confidentiality and failure protocol; none is added or claimed
here. Existing d7f2fd2bd53e keeps swap rotation accounting balanced across callback
changes. No bootloader integration, suspend, swap, hibernation or emulator was run.

# Printk hook record ownership and formatting

`evidence/printk-history-review.json` records nine dispositions, including the
unique printk merge resolution. Keep current generic caller, hotplug and logbuf
hooks and the native _printk_deferred GPL export. The obsolete exported function
named printk_deferred is superseded by its native macro/replacement symbol.
Hotplug skip can delay console output and caller hooks can change attribution;
these remain explicit freedoms of trusted vendor modules. Exact SDK users were
not established, so retention is conservative and does not claim runtime use.

`4e85d32c517a` repairs record ownership: native commit makes the record available
for later reuse, so passing raw writer pointers to subsequent callbacks is not
safe. The enabled-hook noinline helper copies metadata and bounded text while
the reservation owns it, commits with native finalization semantics, then invokes
the hooks in their original order with the snapshot. Disabled hooks use native
commit directly. Signatures, timing, valid record contents and printk return
values remain. Changes through the snapshot cannot change the committed log;
this deliberately rejects post-publication raw-ring mutation. Other records must
be read through the native reader protocol, and snapshot pointers cannot outlive
the synchronous callback. Native ringbuffer, nbcon and printk_safe are unchanged.

The helper adds 1,024 bytes of text plus metadata to the stack only when enabled.
Native recursion limits remain, but nested IRQ/NMI stack usage is not runtime
verified. Formatting callbacks receive initialized 12/20-byte buffers whose last
byte is forced to NUL, bounding subsequent string reads while retaining valid
callback output. This is not protection against malicious kernel module writes.

The extracted-code check passes 1,024 record-reuse and 3,900 formatting cases;
the original ACK controls fail both checks. It overwrites host source storage at
commit and checks bounded strings before calling host printf. It does not execute
kernel ringbuffer concurrency, real tracepoints, IRQ/NMI paths or an emulator.

Keep current GPL exports for descriptor lookup, interrupt counts and locked

source compatibility, and does not pin a descriptor lifetime or validate an
arbitrary callback's locking/CPU arguments. No exact SDK external caller was
established, so retention is conservative. No IRQ or affinity operation was run.

# Per-CPU IRQ affinity migration target boundary

`evidence/irq-affinity-history-review.json` records 17 dispositions after full
patch review. Keep the coherent LTS per-CPU IRQ setup, dispatch, teardown and
partition APIs. The ACK series moves ARM PMU/SPE/TRBE and GIC/AIC hardware
partition handling into generic affinity-based sharing. Their architecture and
controller dependencies exclude the x86_64 SDK target. Its virtio PCI transport
uses ordinary IRQ requests. The pinned Trusty source is a positive consumer of
the ordinary per-CPU request wrapper, whose source signature remains supported;
Trusty is not enabled in this SDK build.

The native setup/free/handler functions remain byte-for-byte LTS, preserving
its locking, enabled-CPU and NMI checks. The follow-up NULL-affinity bug does not
exist without the omitted affinity field/state machine. The union-storage
cleanup is optional optimization, not historical KMI preservation. Header fix
`be986f481f69` restores native ARM PMU declarations to match the retained driver;
all affected ARM PMU files now equal LTS. No selected SDK runtime path changes.

This evidence does not prove that unspecified external x86 modules cannot use
the newer low-level affinity API. That uncertainty is recorded as a target-boundary
limitation, not presented as a complete vendor source compatibility result.
No interrupts, NMI delivery or performance-monitor operation were runtime-tested.

# Softirq RT policy and source exports

`evidence/softirq-history-review.json` records 26 dispositions, including the
old revert/reintroduction sequence. Native tasklet tracing supersedes the old
one-argument events; native removal of ksoftirqd_running supersedes the old
synchronous-mask workarounds. Their complete current invocation/trace functions
remain LTS. The whole softirq source equals LTS after removing only the recorded
exports and Android active-mask/deferral logic, ignoring five blank lines.

Retain the SDK-enabled RT_SOFTIRQ_AWARE_SCHED policy and queued-RT repair
`0dfff3baea14`. SurfaceFlinger and AudioFlinger sources provide positive RT-use
evidence. Long NET_TX/NET_RX/BLOCK/IRQ_POLL/TASKLET work can be deferred while RT
work is current or queued; shorter classes remain eligible. Deferred work stays
pending and ksoftirqd is awakened. This favors RT latency at a possible cost to
network/storage/tasklet service. Native accounting, restart bounds, RCU and
PF_MEMALLOC restoration remain. No workload latency, throughput or starvation
bound has been measured. Approximate CPU sampling grants no ownership or lock.

Generic GPL scheduler, IRQ and softirq exports support trusted source modules;
raw locks, lists, task groups and CPU-local data still require native locking,
lifetime and hotplug discipline. The renamed frequency-scale variable is already
exported upstream. Exact SDK module consumers of these interfaces are unresolved,
so source retention is conservative. This scoped review does not certify the
separate proxy-scheduler/hook changes. No kernel scheduler runtime was executed.

# Proxy rwsem waiter initialization

`2c9eca023027` initializes `donor_wakeup` to false for both queued waiter types.
ACK reapplication `8f585b1490b3` adds the field and uses it to bypass a first
waiter's handoff priority, but leaves it uninitialized until a donor is selected.
The verified SDK configuration zeroes stack variables; explicit initialization
removes that dependency and prevents unintended handoff bypass in other build
configurations. Deliberate donor selection still sets the field to true.

`check_rwsem_waiter_init.py` compares the complete extracted writer try-lock
function against native LTS for ordinary waiters, using the actual initializer
blocks after valid nonzero poisoning. All 773 checks pass; ACK's original
initializer causes 138 failures. Explicit donor success/busy cases also pass.
Atomic and list operations are single-threaded stubs; this does not test real
lock concurrency, proxy scheduling, starvation, or IRQ behavior. The wider proxy
handoff series remains provisional pending its lifetime/state-machine review.

# Locking observation and policy hooks

`evidence/locking-hooks-history-review.json` records 28 dispositions and both
unique resolutions of merge b1065ab8198b. Preserve native mutex error waiter
removal and workqueue watchdog sampled-time calculation alongside observations.
Current rtmutex.c is LTS plus wait observations; rtmutex_api.c adds acquisition/
release observations; the OSQ implementation is native plus GPL exports. Other
scoped complete-function comparisons separate hook policy from proxy changes.

Vendor locking interfaces are retained conservatively despite unconfirmed SDK
registrations. Several are policy replacements: list-placement callbacks must
link exactly one live waiter with native bookkeeping; successful read overrides
must establish reader ownership/count/order; a per-CPU writer completion override
must actually drain readers and provide the native ordering. Incorrect callbacks
can break mutual exclusion, memory safety or fairness. Defaults retain native
behavior. These trusted module interfaces are required source customization
surfaces, not unprivileged userspace controls or passive diagnostics.

Callbacks must respect exact call-site context. RT-mutex release notification
precedes unlock; other release observations may occur after ownership is lost.
Wait finish can report failure. Pointer/list arguments do not pin lifetime or
permit sleeping under native spinlocks/IRQ-disabled/preempt-disabled contexts.
Static aggregate fields are zeroed; regular rwsem dynamic vendor/OEM data is
explicitly cleared, while mutex/per-CPU OEM initialization is callback-owned.
No guarantee of universal zeroed vendor storage is made. Separate proxy handoff,
blocked-state and scheduler reviews remain open; these decisions do not certify
them. No kernel lock contention or callback runtime test was performed.

# Proxy lock state and donor lifetime

`evidence/proxy-locking-state-review.json` finalizes 16 state/helper/history
candidates. Keep per-task blocked-lock serialization, typed mutex/rwsem state,
PROXY_WAKING transitions and corrected per-sleep rwsem state. Preserve native
mutex acquisition/release ownership operations and waiter cleanup, rwsem count
accounting, acquire/release publication and task references before waking readers.
The mutex post-spin sleeping state and rwsem metadata-before-publication fixes
are retained; deterministic extracted checks fail with their actual old code.
No claim of kernel contention, affinity, fairness or race validation is made.

The donor pointer alone is not a task reference. A remote wakeup can detach a
scheduling donor; the wider scheduler link lifetime is still under review.
Commit bb6c4caa1ee2 validates donor membership in the wait-lock-protected queue
before dereferencing its task in mutex/rwsem selection. This local defense keeps
intentional handoff to live donors and native fallback for missing donors. A
reused address could still bias priority toward a live unrelated waiter; it does
not become a memory access to an unqueued freed task in these selection blocks.
The extra mutex list walk can lengthen IRQ-disabled lock hold time. No runtime
cost, starvation bound or actual kernel stale-pointer exploit is established.

The original donor-selection candidates da0eae57da46 and 8f585b1490b3 remain
provisional pending scheduler chain analysis, including proxy_migrate_task and
remote wakeup/reset paths. The 256 simulated membership cases and 773 prior
initializer/handoff cases establish local properties only. The selected default
remains enabled as in final ACK and native LTS, superseding the temporary false
default. Scoped local userspace searches did not establish a boot override;
compilation does not establish runtime enablement. No remote source was used.

# Proxy donor chain and task layout

`evidence/proxy-donor-chain-review.json` follows up the prior provisional donor
question and finalizes seven candidates. The complete local donor-reference scan
separates freshly built migration chains from saved unlock hints. The scheduler
clears the selected root, builds reverse links only for verified same-rq owners,
and traverses them under the same rq lock. Migration marks tasks MIGRATING before
dequeue, converts the chain to a list before unlocking, and uses target-rq-locked
attachment. Native task-rq locking waits for migration completion. Saved unlock
hints instead use the protected waiter-membership defense in bb6c4caa1ee2.
This source argument resolves the scoped lifetime question; it is not an actual
race test or a claim that the remaining scheduler audit is complete.

Retain donor-first mutex/rwsem behavior for the configured Android proxy scheduler.
The intentional rwsem donor handoff bypass changes native FIFO/fairness policy
and can persist across retries. Initialization and native ownership/count/barrier
operations remain, but no latency or starvation bound is claimed. Preserve LTS
removal of proxy_tag_curr and native class-scheduler on-CPU protections.

Commit 275786041b29 removes migration_node and its two initializers after the
positive replacement with se.group_node. This is obsolete internal storage;
its retention need not be attributed to a proven historical author intention.
Keep is_blocked despite the introducing commit's KMI-freeze title because current
scheduling/wakeup code actively uses it. Undo the KMI-only relocation of
user_dumpable and restore the native bitfield placement. Its exit-time saved
value and native ptrace credential/capability checks are retained. Existing vendor
module binaries are intentionally not preserved; source modules rebuild against
this layout. No remote resources or runtime tests were added.

# Wake queues and notification hooks

`evidence/wake-queue-history-review.json` covers eleven candidates including all
eight unique paths of merge c05e84a239bf. Wake-queue add/wake operations retain
native duplicate suppression, barriers and task-reference ownership. Keep integer
wake-batch metadata rather than the historical pointer to a transient queue head.
Commit f6617750f7d3 resets the copied scalar during fork and uses WRITE_ONCE for
publication/clear. Concurrent wakers can overwrite it; it is an advisory hint,
not a lifetime reference or exact serialized count. No queue pointer escapes.

Retain generic flags/mode and socket wake-replacement hooks. Defaults preserve
native wakeups. A callback setting done must supply the replaced notification;
incorrect state masks or suppression can starve waiters. Socket async notification
still runs, and callbacks must obey the call-site atomic/RCU restrictions. Native
pollfree logic is GPL-exported for the actual Rust PollCondVar destructors, which
retain synchronous/deferred RCU lifetime handling. Exact SDK vendor hook consumers
remain unconfirmed; no absence argument justifies removing these interfaces.
No runtime socket, poll, lock-contention or scheduling test was executed.

# CPU frequency accounting and source interfaces

`evidence/cpufreq-history-review.json` reviews twelve historical candidates.
Keep per-task time-in-state accounting: pinned Framework readers consume the
process/thread proc files and parse CPU headers and 10ms time units. Keep the
synthetic dummy driver explicitly selected and distributed by the existing
virtual-device build. Its frequencies 1/2 do not measure real hardware power.
Array access/free uses the IRQ-saving task statistics lock; allocation failure
can lose accounting samples. Added memory use, lock contention, concurrent
hotplug/driver replacement and task/proc runtime races have not been measured or
executed. Persistent policy tables preserve offsets; this is not a proof of
arbitrary dynamic-driver compatibility.

Keep generic max-frequency reporting customization and GPL per-CPU governor
source export, with native RCU/lifetime obligations. Incorrect callbacks can
mislead frequency policy or violate synchronization. Exact SDK registrations
remain unconfirmed; retain these interfaces conservatively. Do not revive the
explicitly reverted max-frequency capping engine or removed frequency-table/GIC
hooks. Current EAS readiness still checks schedutil in both ACK and LTS, while
separate Android override hooks remain. Preserve native current pressure logic.
Tegra/SCMI-specific additions are outside this x86 target; the native SCMI driver
has no vendor done branch and therefore no instance of the fixed uninitialized
variable. No physical driver fix is weakened by omitting that absent branch.

# Scheduler source exports

`evidence/scheduler-exports-review.json` records complete export-history reviews,
scoped comparisons to native function bodies, and build-121 symbol presence.
Exports allow rebuilt trusted vendor modules to use current scheduler interfaces;
they do not freeze task/runqueue layout or provide old-signature shims. Preserve
rq/pi lock order, task references, CPU-hotplug coordination, RCU and static-key
lifecycle obligations. Checked scheduling APIs still enter permission/LSM checks;
explicit nocheck APIs remain for trusted kernel callers and must not be exposed
to unchecked userspace requests. Tracepoint probes run in scheduler contexts and
must not sleep or retain unreferenced pointers.

Keep native migrate_swap CPU/affinity rechecks and stop_two_cpus machinery while
allowing its source API under SMP without NUMA. Preserve current pick_task rq_flags
and RETRY_TASK contract and set_next_entity bool-first behavior. No LTS scheduling
fix is replaced to reproduce an obsolete exported prototype. Current PELT/proxy/
SCX policy changes are audited separately; export-only review does not certify
all behavior of those implementations.

Exact SDK vendor consumers of several exports remain unconfirmed. In particular,
ACK's exported sysctl_sched_latency scalar has no internal EEVDF use in the
examined source; retain the vendor source data interface conservatively without
claiming it controls native scheduling latency or proving it is KMI-only. No
runtime scheduling, contention, hotplug or external-probe tests were executed.

# Fair scheduler histories and SDK scope

`evidence/fair-scheduler-history-review.json` records historical capacity,
balancing, latency and utilization-clamp decisions. Preserve complete native
scale_rt_capacity, need_active_balance, calculate_imbalance, update_sd_lb_stats
and sched_balance_rq implementations (the latter additionally publishes rq flags
for proxy detach). Historical heuristics were reverted or
superseded by native balancing redesigns; old conflict-marker blocks and obsolete
count/scale helpers are not restored.

Commit b2b392919cf9 restores the complete native find_energy_efficient_cpu and
caller signature. The existing SDK disables CONFIG_ENERGY_MODEL, making the
native sched_energy_enabled/is_rd_overutilized gate exclude that path. Therefore
the ACK-only synchronous EAS shortcut and duplicate early PELT sync are omitted
for this target. This does not remove a generic selection hook, a userspace API,
or an LTS fix. Reassess EAS policy for a future target that enables it. Generic
pre-hook task-load synchronization remains, skipping new forks as final ACK does.

Keep cpu.uclamp.latency_sensitive metadata: pinned Android CpuPolicySpread/Pack
profiles and NNAPI init configuration write it. Both cgroup versions retain the
non-root 0/1 interface, native permissions and zero-initialized task-group storage.
Its historical built-in EAS consumer was explicitly reverted by ACK; exact vendor
placement use is unconfirmed, and a write is not claimed to change native latency.
Keep Android CAP_SYS_NICE restriction for per-task utilization-clamp changes;
this tightens native access rather than weakening checks. SurfaceFlinger's
property-gated clamp caller is positively paired with SYS_NICE in its service
configuration. Runtime activation, fairness and power effects remain unverified.

# Scheduler diagnostics and hotplug history

`evidence/scheduler-trace-hotplug-review.json` covers blocked-reason trace history,
one-way proxy enable configuration and obsolete hotplug helper history. Keep the
blocked-reason fields consumed by pinned Perfetto. Current wake site holds pi_lock
and calls architecture __get_wchan directly, avoiding recursive get_wchan locking.
Native x86 helper pins the stack and uses bounded ORC reads; a caller sampled during
switch-out can still be zero/inexact. Caller addresses and process activity remain
sensitive trace data subject to normal trace/perf permissions. No actual trace run.

Preserve the 0664 one-way proxy-enable sysfs interface: any write enables, including
text 0, and it cannot disable. Default SDK key is already enabled. Native static-key
synchronization remains, but a boot-disabled runtime transition is untested. The
original setup drops its kobject on group-create error yet returns 0; init success
alone therefore does not prove attribute availability. Keep boot status logging.

Native online-CPU balance interval and hotplug update locations remain. Historical
active-mask removal is not replayed over current native concrete-task filtering;
proxy NULL-context CPU-priority semantics retain their separate audit. Removed
historical async drain and preempt-count sampling helpers are not resurrected.

# Proxy scheduler trace records

`evidence/proxy-trace-review.json` reviews all ten events and final call sites
from 5562881341cf. Preserve Android diagnostics despite an unconfirmed exact SDK
consumer. Commit 697505edff4d zero-pads sixteen task-name arrays; ordinary strscpy
left bytes after a short name unwritten in raw event records. Native strscpy_pad
initializes those tails while retaining fields and printed strings. This does not
claim that every structure-padding byte or arbitrary probe is sanitized.

Sleeping-owner activation holds an owner reference. The detached waiter remains
live because both activation callers keep preemption or interrupts disabled, and
native task release defers the rcu_users reference drop through call_rcu. Other
sites retain rq, task pi_lock, or the actual blocked mutex/rwsem wait_lock as
appropriate. No new locks or scheduling decisions are introduced. Task metadata
may still be sampled during concurrent legitimate updates; no atomic snapshot,
runtime tracing, or scheduler-performance validation is claimed. Native trace
access controls and all LTS scheduling fixes remain.

# Proxy wakeup and migration history

`evidence/proxy-wake-history-review.json` resolves 27 wake/selection/migration
and yield candidates. Retain sleeping-owner list/reference management and later
race fixes: locked owner recheck before enqueue, pi_lock/blocked_lock revalidation
before delayed activation, references spanning unlock, and IO accounting before
changing task CPU. Finite list snapshots do not establish a bound on adversarial
chain work. Task lifetime details are in the separate proxy trace review.

Keep final strict native wake-state matching and the native on_cpu acquire wait,
CPU-selection path, and block release-store. Reverted speculative preemption-time
TASK_RUNNING mutation and removed manual force-return helper stay absent. Donor
placement can differ from its affinity while it cannot execute; real wakeup uses
native allowed-CPU selection. TASK_WAKING counts as special so return blocking
cannot become a delayed CFS dequeue. is_blocked prevents a running task whose lock
hint was published from being selected as a sleeping proxy donor.

Callbacks are reset when picks become invalid, preserving the hotplug callback.
Native hrtick is adapted to pass the donor to its donor-class tick callback, and
NULL yield callbacks are guarded. Native donor-based yield implementations are
already upstream. Core-picker RETRY_TASK resets clocks and cookie-selection state;
SDK SCHED_CORE is disabled, so that shared API companion has no runtime evidence.
No scheduler latency, fairness, exhaustive race or deadlock guarantee follows from
source review or compilation. No source change beyond the recorded trace padding
was needed for this review, and no LTS fix was weakened.

# Scheduler dispatch and deadline servers

`evidence/dispatch-server-review.json` reviews 16 class-picker, SCX context and
DL server candidates. Keep the rq-flags/retry protocol for lock-dropping pickers,
class-change mask and SCX balancing at pick time. Preserve the native balance
helper's lock pinning/deferred callbacks, CFS donor reload after newidle balancing,
and core-pick in-flight protection. Exact SDK BPF policy use is unconfirmed;
SCHED_CLASS_EXT is enabled and generic Android/vendor source behavior is retained.

Commit 6ca282d8ee2c addresses cached donors across DSQ lock drops. If the donor
changes, stop using the old pointer and retry selection, including forced SCX
server picks. DL must return that marker before invoking task hooks or assigning
a server. The retained sibling helper uses the scheduling donor. The host checker
runs four actual extracted functions with simulated replacement points and marks
old donor storage inaccessible: 323 cases pass, while 191 pre-fix controls fail.
These are control-flow checks, not live locking, BPF or scheduler validation;
same-pointer ABA and arbitrary interleavings are not exhaustively modeled.

Retain the ext DL server and common accounting fixes. A second default reservation
of 50 ms per 1000 ms adds nominally 5% to admission accounting, including when the
server is initialized but not actively serving tasks. Both servers participate in
root-domain/hotplug bandwidth handling. Native admission checks and the newer
running-server revised wakeup rule remain. Idle stopping requires no runnable or
pending work. This does not establish any measured starvation or latency bound.

Fair/ext debug parameters retain 0644 files, runtime/period bounds, native offline
CPU rejection under rq lock, and bandwidth checks under the DL lock. Failed writes
retain old parameters and do not advance ppos. Zero runtime deliberately disables
the server and is logged; no safety against starvation is claimed for that setting.
All work uses supplied local histories; no additional remote resources were used.

# Fair scheduler arithmetic and deferral

`evidence/fair-eevdf-review.json` resolves 16 candidates, including four unique
merge resolutions and the temporary ABI-driven removal of scheduler fixes. Keep
the native LTS accumulator naming, wrapped comparisons, slice-protection `first`
argument, average-invariant reweighting, separate `vlag`/`vprot` fields, and newer
three-field augmented-tree initialization/copy/rotate corrections. ACK's union
workaround is unnecessary with the actual LTS field separation; the underlying
correctness requirement is preserved. All reviewed native helper identities and
Android integration deltas are recorded individually.

Keep ACK's wider eligibility multiplication. The configured x86_64 uses int128;
both 64-bit variants pass 4,772 host arithmetic cases against a Python integer
oracle, while the native LTS control fails 732. Accumulator/current sums are
constrained to fit s64; synthetic boundary cases are not all established reachable
scheduler states. This is not proof that every scheduler arithmetic operation is
bounded, or any runtime fairness/latency result.

Keep ACK's leftmost fallback and NULL guard around the newer native picker. A
fallback can select an ineligible task; it cannot repair arbitrary queue state
or establish fairness. If the tree stays empty despite nonempty accounting,
the caller's retry can still spin. Commit 4f317b026e17 uses the existing
`printk_deferred_once` helper to suppress sustained diagnostic flooding. Concurrent
first callers can duplicate the message; this is not an atomic once guarantee.
The exact SDK failure reproducer is unconfirmed, so no absence-of-need claim is
made from the newer fixes alone.

Commit 4c34ac46ceef adapts the deadline hook so an expired deadline still advances
`zero_vruntime` when a callback requests deferred preemption. The original ACK
early return bypassed the newer LTS 87573883c30f bookkeeping. Preserve the callback's
false return and unchanged deadline while retaining that fix. Extracted helper
tests cover 360 twelve-step sequences, including wraparound, a queued competitor,
custom/default slices and ending deferral: all pass; 208 pre-fix controls fail.
They model arithmetic/control flow, not kernel scheduling, locks or PELT.

The reweight hook retains its writable weight before native rescaling. Callbacks
must supply valid positive weights; zero or oversized values can break arithmetic
and accounting. The restricted deadline hook can still defer indefinitely. These
are kernel-trusted customization APIs with unconfirmed exact SDK consumers, not
validated userspace inputs. No latency guarantee or arbitrary-callback safety is
claimed. All evidence is local; no additional remote resource was used.

# Removed scheduler implementations and surviving source exports

`evidence/scheduler-removed-history-review.json` covers ten historical candidates.
The batch pause/resume API, two-stage runqueue drain and restored `pick_migrate_task`
were explicitly reverted in ACK. Full original/revert patches and final ACK/LTS/port
definitions establish removal; this decision does not rely on an unsuccessful
consumer search. Keep the native hotplug code and synchronization. The old
ARM64/preempt-count softirq heuristic is also gone, while the newer configured
RT_SOFTIRQ_AWARE_SCHED policy remains as documented in the softirq review.

Retain surviving GPL exports for `__migrate_task`, `select_fallback_rq` and
`stop_one_cpu` for vendor source customization. Their exact SDK module consumers
remain unconfirmed. Native migration/stop implementations and fallback CPU search
are preserved; the existing fallback hook is a trusted override. Callers must
obey task/runqueue locking, CPU validity and stopping-context requirements. No
userspace permission interface, runtime hotplug result or latency guarantee is
introduced or claimed. All resources examined were local.

# PSI userspace compatibility and permission tradeoff

`evidence/psi-compatibility-review.json` resolves five candidates. Native
`cgroup_disable=pressure` and later pressure-file grouping replace the old
compile-time per-cgroup switch. Keep the current LTS accounting/configuration;
the actual emulator boot command line is not verified by a kernel build.

There is positive supplied GrapheneOS evidence for one-second PSI windows:
ActivityManagerService constructs LowMemDetector, whose JNI registers three
memory-pressure triggers through libpsi. The Zygote capability list omits
CAP_SYS_RESOURCE, system_server policy expressly forbids that capability, and
the same policy permits memory-pressure writes. Native unprivileged polling
accepts only multiples of two seconds, so it would reject these registrations.
lmkd is another PSI consumer, but already has SYS_RESOURCE and is not evidence
that the bypass is required. Source repositories/files and pins are in the report.

Retain ACK's DEFAULT_SECURITY_SELINUX alternative to CAP_SYS_RESOURCE. This is
an intentional permission relaxation for every otherwise permitted proc/cgroup
PSI writer, not a runtime SELinux-enforcement check. It permits short-window
polling using the low-priority SCHED_FIFO psimon worker, with added CPU/worker
resource exposure. Proc mode 0 becomes 0444 in proc_create_reg; Android init
changes the memory node to system:system 0666, so LSM policy remains material.
Permissive or disabled enforcement changes the exposure. No exhaustive app-domain
policy proof, measured resource bound or runtime notification result is claimed.

The complete psi.c is LTS apart from the precisely enumerated permission/mode
changes and two vendor hook calls/include. Preserve native release/write
serialization, timer shutdown and kthread creation outside cgroup_mutex. Hook
arguments are borrowed under the relevant averages/rtpoll mutex; callbacks must
not retain them or destroy/reenter their list. The event hook precedes cmpxchg
and does not certify a new wakeup; the group hook runs on each update pass. Exact
SDK hook consumers remain unconfirmed, so generic source compatibility is kept.
All examined resources are supplied local sources; no new remote access occurred.

# PELT configuration and source customization

`evidence/pelt-customization-review.json` resolves six candidates. Keep the 0644
multiplier sysctl, default 1 with allowed values 1/2/4, and its vendor veto hook.
Preserve ordinary task-time accounting and native IRQ/steal subtraction. Scale
PELT deltas and idle synchronization through the additional live clock field;
omit both historical ABI layout/CRC artifacts. Native idle synchronization stays
in set_next_task_idle after previous-class accounting (79a074be9b57).

Each runqueue samples the shift independently; accumulated clocks are not
rescaled. Native migration idle-lag extrapolation still uses unscaled scheduler
clock time, and hardware-pressure averaging retains its native clock. Do not
interpret the advertised half-life shorthand as a measured uniform wall-time
decay rate across all signals, migration or changing settings. No runtime
convergence, frequency, power, fairness or latency result is claimed.

Retain initialized replacement hooks for PELT clocks/averages, the forced-average
refresh hook, fair load notifications and task-aware RT utilization notifications.
Recorded native-body comparisons show default code remains intact. Replacements
must maintain accounting and caller locking contracts; borrowed pointers are
not references. The multiplier hook runs under its handler mutex before value
validation, so it can see subsequently rejected input. Rejection restores the
sysctl variable and does not publish a shift; it cannot undo callback side effects
or the underlying proc handler's file-position changes. Recursive writes deadlock.

Exact SDK multiplier writers and callbacks are unconfirmed after scoped local
searches; this does not establish absence of need. Preserve the generic Android
configuration and vendor source interfaces conservatively. Trusted callbacks and
nondefault timing can materially alter scheduler decisions; no arbitrary-callback
safety or arbitrary-clock-jump bound is established. No remote resources were used.

# RT and deadline chain balancing

`evidence/rt-dl-chain-review.json` resolves twelve candidates, including the
unique d202b97297c3 merge hunk. Retain execution-context versus scheduling-context
selection, chain-aware push/pull and revalidation, and live DL current-entity
tracking. They support enabled Android proxy execution, not historical binary
layout. Keep native current/migrate-disabled exclusions, CPU-priority and deadline
heap maintenance, and DL parameter inheritance with ENQUEUE_REPLENISH on deboost.
Native enqueue/accounting bodies remain apart from documented aliases and the
current-entity guard preventing nested pushable-tree insertion.

Two safety adaptations are recorded. In 3409f4102490, extend the native
fc09027786c9 active-CPU filter to RT searches with no local execution task. ACK's
NULL-context branch bypassed it, admitting inactive CPU candidates. The extracted
four-CPU test covers 32,768 static combinations; all pass, whereas the prior
implementation fails 2,800. This preserves the hotplug livelock fix's filtering
intent; it does not freeze CPU state after the check or constrain arbitrary
trusted vendor overrides.

In faad9f616aa9, take task blocked_lock while sampling a migration-chain owner.
Remote mutex and rwsem wakeups publish PROXY_WAKING under that lock without either
rq lock. Both rq locks remain held; release blocked_lock before dequeue/enqueue
callbacks. The 160-case extracted helper model passes; a forced source-level
interleaving exposes invalid primitive accesses in 24 prior cases and unprotected
owner reads in another 24. This is not a reproduced kernel crash or machine-code
race. It checks the local synchronization contract, not global chain correctness.

Traversal accepts only tasks queued on the locked source rq. Preemption-disabled
rq-lock regions protect task allocation through native RCU release, but remote
owners may change state and cause traversal to stop. Missing/self owner is a
transitional result, not proof of an unblocked endpoint. No global chain recovery,
cycle termination, fairness or latency bound is claimed. Ordinary chain migration
updates wake_cpu through set_task_cpu; unlike proxy_migrate_task it does not save
the earlier preference. Subsequent wake/return selection must still enforce CPU
affinity. No boot, scheduler stress or hotplug execution was performed.

Retain the two-context find_lowest_rq hook; callbacks must handle NULL exec_ctx,
borrowed task pointers and valid CPU selection. Exact SDK registrations are
unconfirmed, which is not evidence of no need. Native filtering and accounting
remain the default, with trusted hooks able to deliberately change policy. All
sources used for this review were local; embedded commit links were not fetched.

# Synchronous wakeup flag separation

`evidence/sync-wakeup-review.json` resolves six candidates. Retain Android's
SMP RT sync hint policy and its vendor veto, with ordinary WF_SYNC propagation.
C Binder and epoll provide positive compiled sync-wakeup paths; a particular SDK
RT workload or energy/latency benefit is not established. The policy checks the
execution current's RT policy, wakee priority, RT task count and same-CPU affinity;
it can bypass the usual capacity/softirq search. This is deliberate Android
runtime compatibility, not a real-time performance guarantee.

ACK reused 0x80 for ENQUEUE_WAKEUP_SYNC even though LTS uses that bit for
ENQUEUE_INITIAL. In b1cd2b2b2792 the sync flag moves to 0x1000, a free bit across
current enqueue/dequeue flags. Native ENQUEUE_INITIAL stays 0x80. Ordinary
synchronous fair wakeups therefore no longer receive initial-task half-slice
placement, and new RT enqueues are no longer mistaken for synchronous wakes.
Symbolic vendor source interfaces remain; historical binary flag values do not.
The extracted native placement routine passes 32 modeled cases; old definitions
fail 16 flag/placement cases, including two incorrect deadlines. This checks local
flag semantics, not runtime fairness or contention.

Keep the separate WF_ANDROID_VENDOR wake-domain flag and existing socket/wake
hooks. Allowing it in a WARN mask changes diagnostics, not permissions. The early
wake-flags hook runs with preemption disabled before pi_lock and may alter flags;
trusted callbacks must preserve internal wake semantics and atomic constraints.
The honor hook defaults true and can veto but does not bypass remaining predicates.
Exact SDK registrations remain unconfirmed; no-consumer searches were not used
as omission proof. All evidence for this review is local; no remote was accessed.

# RT balancing and diagnostic hooks

`evidence/rt-hook-review.json` resolves four candidates. Retain RT balance
replacement, push-IPI CPU selection, revised lowest-rq placement and throttle
diagnostics as generic Android vendor source interfaces. Whole-function delta
checks preserve native default behavior and current LTS fixes. The throttle site
is compiled out because SDK RT_GROUP_SCHED=n; keeping its declaration/export does
not establish an active SDK callback or justify enabling RT groups.

RT balance unpinning is not unlocking. Callbacks must restore any dropped rq lock
and honor the replacement contract. Lowest-rq status zero may leave no fresh mask;
overrides are trusted and must select a valid CPU. Push-IPI callbacks run under
rto_lock, possibly from hardirq; the native current-CPU skip still follows the
callback, but this does not validate arbitrary values or guarantee callback
progress. Bad trusted callbacks can cause missed balancing, corruption or hangs.
No hardware-specific support or removed common CPU Pause machinery was added.
Exact SDK registrations are unconfirmed; absence is not treated as omission proof.
No remote resources or new runtime tests were used for this batch.

# Scheduler hook lifetime and instantiation

`evidence/scheduler-hook-plumbing-review.json` resolves nine candidates. Preserve
the current scheduler trace registry and per-source macro cleanup needed when
build_policy.c includes multiple policy sources. The registry instantiates live
vendor APIs even though its original motivation mentioned ABI metadata. The report
accounts for all 44 moved exports: 39 same-name, two renamed, three explicitly
removed later. Preserve current cgroup declarations and omit the explicitly
reverted can_attach/update_cpus_allowed overrides.

Keep server start/skip customization and diagnostic hooks. Default start follows
the native empty-to-nonempty transition; trusted callbacks may suppress or force
service, affecting starvation protection. In dcef94ed034d initialize the skip
veto for each server. The previous function-wide variable leaked a veto through
goto again into later servers. All 216 extracted-loop cases pass; the old code
fails 20. The model uses three candidates to exercise repeated decisions, while
the configured rq has fair and ext servers. No concurrency or latency result is
claimed. RETRY_TASK still returns before any hook or server assignment; diagnostic
pointers remain borrowed in scheduler context.

The current fair preemption hook has initialized preempt/ignore controls and
retains the full native function when inactive. Preempt takes precedence if both
are set; active trusted callbacks can override native policy. Removed early hooks
are not recreated. Exact SDK registrations remain unconfirmed and are not used
as omission proof. All newly consulted resources were local.

# Scheduler deferral hooks and unimplemented bounds

`evidence/scheduler-deferral-hooks-review.json` resolves three candidates. Retain
ordinary GPL-exported Android deferral/lifecycle hooks with initialized false or
zero defaults. Native reschedule functions compare exactly after removing those
hook additions. No extra hardware support or vendor binary compatibility is added.

The names and commit descriptions overstate what the core implements. The hook
inside __resched_curr can suppress immediate requests, including idle wakeups;
it does not simply request lazy preemption. The hook in resched_curr_lazy also
suppresses the request. The schedule-entry hook has no CFS-only, preempt-only,
timer or deferral-count guard. It runs for voluntary blocking too, before rq lock
and IRQ disable. A trusted callback must implement eligibility, bounds and correct
blocking semantics. The supplied patch itself does not implement bounded deferral.
Wrong callbacks can cause starvation, busy loops or hangs. Native behavior remains
when callbacks leave the initialized outputs unchanged.

Scoped GrapheneOS and manifest-module searches did not establish the actual hint
transport, callback implementation or SDK policy. Those questions remain explicit;
absence is not omission proof. Retention preserves requested generic Android vendor
source customization, not a claim that the described framework policy exists or
has been verified. No remote source or runtime preemption test was used.

# Priority hooks and nice-value bounds

`evidence/priority-hooks-review.json` resolves nine priority, fork, yield and
uclamp candidates against their complete patches and current implementations.
The hooks are retained as generic Android vendor source interfaces, including
current scheduler-owned declarations/exports and syscall file organization.
Native permission/ownership/LSM checks remain, including separately reviewed
ACK 2b5819446e0b CAP_SYS_NICE clamp restriction. Positive GrapheneOS
callers exercise the scheduler/nice surface; exact SDK vendor callbacks remain
unconfirmed and are not presumed absent.

`8771c5fb4ae8` preserves the native nice range after the locked callback. ACK checked
the request before this mutable callback, allowing a bad replacement to reach
static_prio and the 40-entry scheduler weight tables. Revalidate -20 through 19
before any priority write. Valid customization and false veto are preserved, and
the native scoped rq guard releases on rejection. The old unlocked allowed flag
which bypassed range rejection is superseded, not restored. The extracted host
check covers 16,200 cases with zero current failures; its pre-fix control has
1,440 mismatches including 480 invalid modeled weight indices. It does not execute
kernel scheduling or establish an exploitable userspace path.

Priority/class/task-pointer callbacks still require trusted, nonblocking code
which preserves PI, queue/class and lifetime invariants. They can deliberately
customize values after userspace permission checks; those checks are not repeated
on arbitrary callback mutations. Keeping this vendor facility is a compatibility
choice, not a claim that unknown callbacks are safe. Native deadline PI setup is
retained before the setscheduler notification, and native sched_change guards,
cpuset serialization, deadline admission and uclamp validation remain. Uclamp
notifications report explicit updates, not every default/reset path; yield does
not promise that a different task will execute. No remote sources were accessed.

# Schedutil hook history and policy obligations

`evidence/sugov-hooks-review.json` resolves ten additions, removals and
reintroductions. Keep the current map-util and rq-aware I/O-wait interfaces,
including complete declarations/exports. The old worker sched_attr and update-veto
hooks were explicitly removed and remain absent in final ACK; native worker policy
and update logic are retained. The entire current cpufreq_schedutil.c matches LTS
after removing only the map-util addition and its include.

With callbacks absent, native frequency mapping, caching, policy-limit barriers
and driver resolution remain. The map-util callback can replace the raw frequency
and mutate need_freq_update/policy. A trusted callback that clears a mandatory
limit-change update can allow the raw-cache fast path to return the previous
frequency without re-resolution. Retaining that explicit Android policy contract
is a vendor source-compatibility choice; callbacks must preserve frequency and
thermal limits, valid integer ranges and update obligations. Native limits are
not claimed to constrain arbitrary callback behavior. The I/O-wait callback may
force or suppress boost, with p->in_iowait as its initial value, but does not alter
I/O accounting. All scheduler-context callbacks must obey locking/lifetime rules.

The generated SDK configuration selects schedutil. Exact SDK callback consumers
and their policies remain unconfirmed; the scoped negative searches are not
omission proof. No physical CPU power savings, runtime correctness or thermal
behavior is verified by this source review. No remote resources were accessed.

# Architecture topology scope and EAS configuration

`evidence/topology-scope-review.json` resolves 24 candidates, including old maximum
capacity state, architecture callbacks, exports, removals/reintroductions and the
current EAS eligibility hook. `2ed111267d9e` restores drivers/base/arch_topology.c
and include/linux/arch_topology.h exactly to pinned LTS. GENERIC_ARCH_TOPOLOGY is a
hidden option selected only by ARM, ARM64, RISC-V and PA-RISC; x86 cannot select its
object. Generated SDK configuration confirms it is absent. Native x86 topology
and APERF/MPERF remain exact LTS. This is positive architecture/build evidence,
not an assumption that unlocated consumers do not exist.

Omit the non-x86 call sites, additional per-CPU hw_pressure export and
initialization-complete state/declaration. None was a linkable/executable SDK
facility. Shared Android trace declarations and GPL registry entries remain for
source-module registration compatibility. Their actual consumers remain
unconfirmed; these call sites are not claimed to emit SDK events. Earlier final
historical-capacity decisions are annotated with this narrower implementation
scope. Native hardware-pressure calculations, export and local-variable fix are
preserved. Old ACK root-domain max-capacity machinery was explicitly reverted and
is absent from both tips; it is not recreated.

The EAS eligibility hook is architecture-independent Android configuration support,
retained without enabling ENERGY_MODEL for SDK. Its final placement returns true
before all native eligibility checks, including SMT exclusion, frequency invariance
and cpufreq readiness. A trusted vendor must supply a coherent policy; the hook
is not limited to the historical asymmetric-capacity exception. Default false
continues native checks, and the whole domain-building body is native. No EAS,
thermal, AMU or future-platform runtime correctness is claimed. Only local pinned
sources were used; commit-message URLs were not accessed.

# Scheduler domain merges and inactive compatibility controls

`evidence/scheduler-topology-merges-review.json` resolves five ordinary candidates
and five merges, examining every unique hunk. Native cpuset locking, synchronous
hotplug handling and deferred energy-domain rebuild work remain; old CPU Pause
resume shortcuts and approximate root-domain maximum state are not restored.
Generic GPL rebuild_sched_domains remains a sleeping, lock-constrained kernel
source API. Existing LTS mutex wrappers coexist with the LOCKDEP-only export.

GrapheneOS init positively writes sched_child_runs_first. Retain its proc scalar,
0644 handler and debug reporting, but it has no scheduling consumer and the whole
fork-placement function is native. Writing it does not change child/parent order.
Similarly, the sched_getaffinity hook declaration/export survives in final ACK,
but the invocation was removed with a later syscall split. Preserve that final
registry interface and native query behavior, consistent with the earlier registry
review. The intent behind non-restoration is unresolved; no consumer-absence claim
or runtime notification guarantee is made. The large later split merge is still
provisional; this batch examines only its affinity-removal region as supporting
history, not all its unique changes.

Merge resolutions retain current callbacks with native diagnostics, current wake
flags, native energy/hardware-pressure logic and correctly guarded bridge exports.
Obsolete nice-range bypass, old signatures, duplicated unlock and historical
vendor-array size are not resurrected. Current CPU-selection overrides still
require trusted callbacks to choose valid CPUs and preserve affinity/placement
obligations; this review does not prove arbitrary vendor policy safe. No remote
resource, runtime scheduler test, emulator boot or Android OS build was used.

# Cpuset historical reverts and native serialization

`evidence/cpuset-history-review.json` resolves nine historical candidates using
complete patches, current native function/file comparisons and final ACK removal
checks. Preserve current LTS deadline/cpuset serialization, referenced task lookup,
active/possible CPU handling and partition state. Do not replay the old removal
of deadline accounting safeguards based on its claim that they were irrelevant to
Android. Current locks and task-reference guards remain native; no sleeping call
is moved into the old RCU-only syscall lookup.

The requested-cpumask scheme and its empty-buffer fix were explicitly removed from
ACK. Neither tip contains its field or allocation path. Current LTS parsing and
v1/v2 behavior are retained without asserting equivalence of every old ABI corner
case. Likewise, current native cpuset_mutex does not justify replaying a 2020
semaphore-to-mutex patch: upstream lock hierarchy and serialization evolved after
both historical conversions. The native non-root active-CPU helper is retained;
top-cpuset/fallback paths deliberately permit possible offline CPUs, so no blanket
active-only guarantee is claimed. No runtime hotplug/concurrency or latency test,
remote resource, Android OS build or emulator boot was used.

# Cgroup callback semantics and migration retries

`evidence/cgroup-hooks-review.json` resolves 16 candidates. Retain final generic
Android notifications and explicit policy controls, including current v1/v2 task
lookup interfaces. Positive Android process-group configuration uses cpu/cpuset
controllers; exact vendor callback policies remain unresolved and are not presumed
unnecessary. Removed old requested-mask/admission overrides remain removed.

The kernel-thread hook deliberately permits a trusted callback to override
no_cgroup_migration/PF_NO_SETAFFINITY rejection for a selected PF_KTHREAD task.
The existing credential checks and native controller checks still run, but a bad callback can strand
kernel work or undermine scheduling availability. `f170aa56e193` resets this
permission for each lookup retry. The extracted 4,096-case model has zero current
failures versus 144 stale acceptances before the fix; forced leader changes are
not a reproduced kernel race or exploit. `c3756f712d3d` keeps native __acquires
attached to the extended prototype; the ACK semicolon detached it. Sparse was not
available and was not run. `876263e2b929` removes a duplicate trace include.

The fork hook may skip the parent-affinity setter and must preserve valid child
placement; it does not change memory-node inheritance or the CLONE_INTO_CGROUP
path. Notifications retain native return paths and borrowed lifetimes. The v1
attach-result event reports failed attach attempts too, but is not reached after
earlier credential denial. General controller attach notification precedes each
controller attach callback; CPU attach notification follows scheduler moves.
CPU-online notification executes under current function-scope uclamp mutex and RCU
guards when enabled, unlike the original historical after-unlock site. Callbacks
must not sleep or re-acquire that mutex. Cpuset-online notification is after its
full unlock and omits the root early-return path. The separate Android CAP_SYS_NICE exception enables cross-UID migration by
system_server: pinned ZygoteInit grants that capability, libprocessgroup opens or
caches cgroup task files, and the local system_server SELinux policy permits it.
Its historic current-only capability check weakens upstream `1756d7994ad8`, which
uses open-time credentials to prevent a less-privileged opener from recruiting a
privileged writer. `0b81c1a66cdf` requires both file_ns_capable and ns_capable in the
target task user namespace for this exception. Native root/owner/saved-UID opener
rules are unchanged. Existing ACK current-writer authority is also required;
privileged-opener/unprivileged-writer handoff gains no new capability exception.
A handoff relying solely on newly privileged writer credentials now fails by
design. This is a justified compatibility restriction to preserve native security
intent, not an assertion that cgroup-v1 authorization is identical to LTS. Normal
same-credential authorized Android operations remain permitted by the model.
`evidence/cgroup-open-credentials-check.json` reports 12,288 extracted-function
cases with no failures; the previous code attaches in 432 modeled denied-opener
cases. LSMs, actual fd handoff, and runtime migration were not executed.
No runtime policy, migration,
latency or emulator boot is verified. No remote source was accessed.

# Cgroup module interfaces and teardown ordering

`evidence/cgroup-module-api-review.json` resolves six candidates. Retain GPL
exports for taskset/CSS iteration and default/legacy control-file registration
with matching removal. Their implementations remain native LTS. Iterators require
paired end and proper references for retained tasks; cftype arrays and callbacks
must remain valid until successful unregister before module unload. Exports do
not automatically create files, grant userspace permissions, or pin arbitrary
module callback lifetimes. New module controls need their own access policy.
Exact vendor consumers are unconfirmed, so the generic Android source interfaces
are conservatively retained; absence in a scoped search is not omission evidence.

Retain four CPU-controller lifecycle hooks and cpu_cgrp_subsys export. Task-move
notification runs before the native rq lock/update; root allocation receives a
NULL parent; ordinary allocation occurs only after successful native allocation.
`97a12e6cd2f3` moves the CSS-free notification before sched_unregister_group. The
old order queued sched_free_group_rcu first, permitting a grace period and free
before the trace callback entered its own RCU protection. css_free_rwork_fn does
not hold an enclosing read lock. Native fair/RT unregister and final deferred
free are unchanged. The callback now observes pre-unregister scheduler state and
must complete vendor cleanup before teardown. This changed order is necessary
for object lifetime safety; the dead CSS must not be retained or resurrected.
Sixteen extracted-function scheduling models pass; the previous code has seven
modeled reads after reclamation. This is not a real RCU stress test or exploit.
Build 134 subsequently succeeded with this correction: kernel image and both module
distributions generated; all 145 module signatures and CRC/flag tables verified.
No remote was accessed.

# Affinity callbacks and native constraints

`evidence/affinity-hooks-review.json` resolves eight candidates. Keep generic
Android affinity policy hooks with matching source declarations/exports, including
the restored sched_setaffinity result event and the actual sca_migrate_same hook
name. Pinned Bionic pthread_setaffinity_np uses the public syscall. Exact vendor
registrations remain unconfirmed; source interfaces are retained conservatively.

`5ef031bd0c53` requires replacement destination CPUs to belong to both the native
valid mask and requested mask, after bounds validation. This restores the native
selector invariant; the callback cannot make an empty/invalid intersection valid
by returning an arbitrary in-range CPU. Kernel/migration-disabled tasks still use
the native online mask; others use active CPUs. Invalid output returns EINVAL
before mask update/move with native unlock. Callbacks must not reject required
migrate-enable transitions; the prior out-of-range rejection already relied on
this contract. The fix does not guarantee safe arbitrary vendor policy.

The same commit moves early affinity suppression after native LSM authorization.
Ownership and capability checks also remain before it. A policy can suppress an
authorized affinity change and return success, but cannot skip the LSM check or
receive an LSM-denied request at this site. The later result hook can report a
status differing from the operation outcome without rolling it back. The context
hook may intentionally ignore remembered userspace affinity for kernel-origin
changes and can mutate flags/pointers; trusted callbacks must preserve storage,
cpuset constraints, and locking rules. The native __sched_setaffinity body,
including cpuset/deadline checks and concurrent-cpuset-update handling, is intact.

The is_cpu_allowed hook only restricts normal placement: migration-disabled and
per-CPU kernel paths keep their native exceptions. sched_ext remote checks keep
native task-possible/migration-disabled/offline tests and existing proxy guards.
Its dispatch veto is reached after local/previous/global queued-work acceptance,
so it is not an absolute CPU-pause control. Its idle veto now clears SMT as well
as per-CPU eligibility, including idle-to-idle changes; otherwise stale SMT bits
could continue advertising a vetoed core. Native idle-mask races/advisory nature
remain. NULL task arguments are valid for idle/dispatch callbacks.

Host models cover 36,288 destination, 768 authorization, and 8,192 idle-mask cases
with zero current failures; old controls fail 14,688, 108, and 768 respectively.
These are extracted-code/shim checks, not actual migration, LSM execution, CPU
hotplug or emulator boot. The initial authorization harness name collided with
libc; the final harness uses a private name and all reported results use it.
Build 134 subsequently passed with the complete kernel/common-module/virtual-device
workflow; all 145 module signatures and CRC/flag tables verified. No additional
remote source was accessed.

# Android video frame limits

`evidence/video-frame-limit-review.json` resolves three historical candidates.
Retain VIDEO_MAX_FRAME and VB2_MAX_FRAME at 64. Pinned Bionic defines 64; the
selected common kernel includes videobuf2/UVC and the existing virtual workflow
builds Vivid and v4l2loopback. The temporary revert to 32 was explicitly undone.
Both entire headers differ from LTS only by the constant, and the entire current
videobuf2 core is native. Its dynamic array/bitmap storage, count clamps, offset
encoding cap, and file-I/O bounds remain. Vivid's explicit limits are 64 or more.
The constant is a default/minimum queue count, not a universal maximum. Increased
default capacity can increase device-user memory consumption; third-party driver
correctness is not established by core bounds alone. Exact SDK application need
for more than 32 buffers remains unconfirmed; Android header/config compatibility
is retained conservatively with positive local evidence. Build 134 covers this
unchanged source. No streaming/queue-init runtime test or remote access occurred.

# Scheduler module APIs and per-runqueue storage

`evidence/scheduler-module-api-review.json` resolves four candidates. Keep generic
GPL APIs for feature state/names, cpuset masks, stopper work, priority tables,
base slice, PELT load sum, active balancing and priority lists. Native bodies and
constants are retained, with existing separately reviewed callback plumbing.
Build 134 emits the expected symbols; sched_energy_present remains conditional
and absent because ENERGY_MODEL=n. Current debug.c is built unconditionally,
independent of stale historical SCHED_DEBUG commentary. Direct feature writes
must synchronize static keys, displayed bits and hotplug; internal APIs do not
validate arbitrary module inputs, references or caller context. The native
feature debugfs writer still holds its locks. Exact external callers are not
established by the scoped search, so interfaces are conservatively preserved.

The 16-u64 per-runqueue OEM field remains functional, zero-initialized static
per-CPU storage: 128 bytes per possible CPU. It offers source-level state for
vendor scheduler callbacks, not preservation of old structure size/offsets or
96-word historical reserves. Vendor access needs its own scheduler-context
synchronization and reference lifetime management. No actual consumer behavior,
scheduler policy or runtime boot is verified. No remote was accessed, including
the lore link embedded in one historical commit message.

# Scheduler merge resolutions across core updates

`evidence/scheduler-merge-followups-review.json` resolves ten nonempty merges;
all unique remerge hunks were read. Keep native callback types, diagnostic
reporting, current/donor distinction, acquire ordering, task-possible masks,
current PSI/uclamp ordering, EEVDF augmented-tree math and delayed dequeue.
Android notifications/source exports remain at their current sites. Old CFS
ideal-runtime/preempt helpers, duplicate schedutil arithmetic, and superseded SCX
cgroup-finish APIs are not restored. The old exported latency scalar remains an
explicitly unconfirmed external source interface, with no native EEVDF use; a
shared obsolete tick-hook declaration is not proof of an actual call site.

Whole selected functions compare to LTS after precisely enumerated Android
changes. Migration callbacks may bypass native hot-task/accounting work only
when taking over the operation, and must restore rq locking and correct state;

These are unique-resolution decisions, not wholesale acceptance of either merge
