No remote links embedded in historical merge messages were accessed.

# Fair selection hooks and native dequeue exclusions

`evidence/fair-selection-hooks-review.json` resolves eight candidates. Keep final
before-picker and replacement callbacks and current preemption controls; obsolete
CFS pick-entity/old preemption signatures stay removed. Pointer initialization is
matched to the current API: before-picker sees NULL, replacement sees an assigned
task, and entity state is derived only after a non-NULL result. A callback-supplied
task bypasses native EEVDF choice and must be a valid runnable fair task on this
runqueue with consistent hierarchy and lifetime. No arbitrary-pointer validation
or fairness/performance improvement is claimed.

`9e5ff622cd6e` retries selection if either callback returns after rq->donor changes,
before stale previous-donor state reaches native class/group switching. The
existing RETRY_TASK path handles this just as other lock-drop restarts. The same
commit restricts delayed-dequeue policy to suppressing a native-permitted delay;
non-sleep, special-state and throttled dequeue exclusions remain authoritative.
ACK otherwise permits setting delay=true after those exclusions. Allowing this
can violate immediate removal and task-state/throttle invariants; local evidence
did not justify preserving that override. Legitimate skip behavior remains, while
forcing an excluded delay is intentionally incompatible. Native task-based
throttle accounting (including ancestor eb962f251fbb) and delayed-finish paths stay.

The place_entity pointer conversion has a narrower effect than its historical
message claims: the final callback runs after vruntime/deadline assignment, so
changing only the pointed-to local does not change stored placement. We preserve
that final behavior instead of inventing a new application point. Callbacks can
separately mutate se, subject to their trusted scheduling obligations.

Extracted host models pass 1,536 selector and 768 dequeue cases; previous controls
fail 960 and 28. These are injected state changes and shim checks, not kernel
concurrency, actual LSM execution, streaming, or a runtime fairness test. Build
135 subsequently passed: all 145 module signatures and CRC/flag tables verified.
No remote source, including upstream commit-message links, was accessed. Exact callback policies remain unconfirmed.

# Scheduler event ordering and writable results

`evidence/scheduler-event-history-review.json` resolves six candidates. Preserve
current int flags on pre/post class events, ordinary final tick notification, and
restricted tick-entry after the rq clock update. Tick entry holds the rq lock;
final tick notification follows unlock but still cannot sleep in tick context.
Native current/donor accounting, PSI/uclamp ordering and class callbacks remain.

The after-dequeue hook is not merely observational despite its commit wording:
it receives a writable bool result and that value is returned. A module changing
it must maintain matching actual delayed-dequeue/class/task state; false success
can break scheduler bookkeeping and lifetime assumptions. The native result is
the default. We retain this explicit final Android source policy interface under
the compatibility priority, without claiming that arbitrary overrides preserve
native invariants. Exact SDK consumers are unconfirmed; no hardware-specific
WALT implementation is introduced by these generic events. No runtime scheduler
accounting or boot verification is claimed. No remote source was accessed.

# Scheduler callback history and capacity floor

`evidence/scheduler-hook-cycles-review.json` resolves eleven candidates across
scheduler callback additions, four temporary removals, and dummy-stub removal.
Full original/final patches and every intermediate added/deleted code difference
were examined, including evolving IRQ/placement signatures, topology event and
export relocation. Generic Android module source callbacks remain; no Qualcomm
hardware-specific WALT implementation is added. Exact SDK callbacks are
unconfirmed after scoped searches; absence is not used to justify omission.
The always-false android.h stubs remain absent as explicitly removed in final ACK.

Preserve native task/stack teardown and wake acquire ordering, fork resets,
hotplug diagnostics, IRQ accounting and clock updates. The report records each
callback context, including unlocked success/flush notifications, pre-init fork,
possibly NULL misfit task, and execution-task versus donor distinction. The IRQ
start bool reports whether native hardirq/non-ksoftirqd softirq delta branches
ran; it is not universally an irq_enter/exit indicator. Misfit suppression can
leave stale load and remains a trusted vendor policy responsibility.

ACK's capacity callback runs after the native minimum-one check and can undo it.
The value then populates rq and group capacities used as load-balancing divisors.
Commit b46e8cbd73681d714f56fd53634102c19f2c1a6f restores the minimum after callback execution while
preserving the original callback input and every positive adjustment. This
restricts only zero output, which cannot satisfy the native arithmetic contract.
It does not bound arbitrary positive policy values or make malicious callbacks
safe. The extracted whole-function check passes 162 cases; the previous control
produces nine zero-divisor states. This is a host model, not a kernel crash or
runtime scheduler test. Build 136 passed with all 145 module signatures and
version/flag tables verified. No remote source was accessed;
embedded links in historical commit messages were not opened.

# Utilization callback policy and activation

`evidence/utilization-policy-hooks-review.json` resolves six source interfaces.
Keep yield suppression, pre-attach new-task utilization adjustment, boosted-util
replacement and generic/task-aware fitness overrides. Their outputs can change
fairness, CPU selection and DVFS; they are trusted vendor policies, not passive
notifications. INT_MAX alone means use native boosted utilization; other outputs
return directly without native capacity capping. The fitness override is boolean
and cannot return the native unmet-minimum -1 state. Explicit false initialization
for the generic result removes dependence on SDK automatic stack zeroing; the
task-specific hook deliberately keeps ACK's true default.

The uclamp validation callback can accept vendor special numeric values and skip
native bounds/order checks. Earlier user authorization, independent Android
CAP_SYS_NICE clamp restriction and LSM checks remain. Native bucket selection
saturates, but the 11-bit value field can truncate extended values; cooperating
vendor code must handle that representation. We do not claim arbitrary accepted
values preserve scheduler semantics. Successful callback acceptance now invokes
native sched_uclamp_enable before return, outside rq locks, so it cannot silently
leave the native accounting static key disabled. Failure still returns directly.
The default path remains complete native validation.

Local GrapheneOS SurfaceFlinger and audio-interface source demonstrate clamp API
use, conditionally enabled; ART Thread.yield and monitor contention use sched_yield.
They do not establish an installed SDK vendor callback or active nonzero property.
No Xiaomi/Samsung/MediaTek hardware implementation is introduced by these hooks.
The host checks pass 2592 validation and 3456 fitness cases; the previous control
misses enable on 432 accepted cases. GCC diagnoses its uninitialized generic
result even with zero-init enabled; only that control's diagnostic is downgraded
for comparison. No SDK exploit or runtime scheduling result is claimed.
Source change: 277f9e226c2176d4d1f7581400f26af58c6190ba. Build 137 passed, including all 145 module signatures and
version/flag tables. No remote source was accessed.

# Scheduler lifecycle followups and merge ordering

`evidence/scheduler-lifecycle-followups-review.json` resolves two hook candidates
and four merges (all unique remerge hunks examined). The low-level CPU-change
event executes before native rq assignment/write barrier/CPU publication. It
covers proxy and initial placement, and may accompany the high-level event;
modules must not double-count or assume uniformly runnable/fully initialized
tasks. The tick-stop entry event precedes eligibility checks, including paths
that retain the tick. It is a chance to manage vendor timers, not a guarantee
that the hardware tick stopped or evidence of the message's power-saving claim.

These are trusted policy interfaces. A detached migration callback must perform
required dequeue/CPU/accounting work and return holding the original rq lock.
The detach path can bypass native hot-task accounting, which its implementation
must maintain. Newidle replacement precedes native active-CPU/misfit checks and

continues through the common tail, which may still issue a next-update kick.

the callback receives a mutable mask, not just a read-only hint. Native defaults
and separately hardened donor retry handling remain. EAS group out_balance=0
only bypasses the energy gate, with remaining native balancing still in control.

# SCX customization contracts

`evidence/scx-lifecycle-contract-review.json` finalizes five original/merge
candidates while retaining generic SCX source hooks on the configured SDK kernel.
Thirteen whole-function comparisons distinguish the hook behavior from native
LTS code. The native class selectors, scheduler change guards, permission/error
checks in the syscall body and DL PI fix remain. The old task_should_scx hook is
superseded by the task-aware consolidated callback. Class/flags callbacks run
before the guard; switch-finish notifications run after assignment and before
reenqueue, correcting the historical merge's callback phase.

These APIs deliberately trust kernel module callbacks. They can route RT tasks
to SCX, replace affinity updates, request migration notifications and change
slices. Callbacks must preserve valid classes, task roles, affinity accounting,
lock context and lifetime. The port does not validate arbitrary callback pointers
or enforce real-time latency guarantees. Default paths retain native behavior.

The priority-queue hook intentionally permits vtime ordering of built-in queues,
bypassing the policy otherwise enforced for BPF flags. Native list/tree updates
and mixed-mode diagnostics remain, but consistent queue mode and vtime values
are callback obligations. Starvation and SCX error/disable remain possible.
The author's assertion of eliminated risks is not adopted as a safety finding.

The skip/repeat hook allows critical tasks to transition first over multiple
passes. A callback must eventually clear repeat and process all required tasks.
In particular, disabling must leave no task attached to the outgoing scheduler.
The port retains this contract: it does not guarantee termination or completeness
for malformed callbacks. Leaving repeat positive can stall teardown/fork and
eventually overflow; skipping remaining tasks can violate the native teardown
lifetime invariant. Enable's READY gate also means already-enabled tasks may no
longer reach the repeat hook to clear it. These risks are explicitly accepted for
the requested trusted vendor customization interface, not attributed to ordinary
userspace or claimed to be ruled out by compilation. No exact installed SDK
callback was verified. This uncertainty is retained, not used to omit support.

Build 143 and generated module checks passed. Host tests verify the recorded
callback phase and veto scenarios only; they do not run BPF, real locks, callback
termination or scheduler teardown. No remote source was accessed.

file's historical KMI declaration-only annotation is excluded. Native cmdline
copy/bounds logic, signal bookkeeping, exit cleanup and block queue/completion
operations remain after removing the documented callback decisions.

The cmdline hooks only request vendor inheritance and restoration; the pair must
save/restore the reader's priority, including errors, and coordinate registration
and lifetime. There is no automatic priority boost without callbacks. Diagnostic
hooks must avoid inappropriate disclosure and honor task/stack lifetime.

Fatal-signal and cgroup freeze/thaw callbacks can suppress wakeups. Suppressing
signal_wake_up also skips setting TIF_SIGPENDING in that call; the callback must
arrange eventual signal notification and wake. No maximum delay is imposed.
Malformed policy can prevent timely killing/freezing/thawing and resource release.
Wake defaults are reset for each thread/task, and native signal/jobctl state
changes remain. The exit-check callback is a notification after PF_EXITING,
before seccomp-filter release, with no veto return value.

IO hooks expose borrowed request/bio state and wait events; a synchronous request
can complete before its notification, so callbacks must not assume it is still
in flight. The six callsites retain native queue/token/wait behavior. WBT is
compiled but WBT_MQ is disabled by default in the SDK config. Runtime IO effects,
callback priority behavior and freeze/signal liveness were not tested. No remote
source was accessed.

starts observing later submissions. References prevent normal allocation release
and request-tag reuse; they do not freeze mutable fields or extend referenced
payload, callback-private or caller-managed stack lifetimes beyond their own
ownership contracts. Callback code still needs synchronization, must not retain
unowned pointers, and must not wait recursively for resources held by its own
submission. Native early failure/merge paths gain no artificial post event.

# Perf metadata under emulated page sizes

`evidence/perf-metadata-review.json` resolves two commits using the local SDK
page_shift=14 setting and simpleperf's reported-page-size mapping calculations.
Retain the enlarged zeroed metadata allocation, matching free order, data offset,
data buffer geometry and initial free-mlock allowance. GrapheneOS init can later
overwrite that allowance, so the kernel default is not a verified runtime value.

`cef6ed5fe6a4` corrects ACK's typed-pointer arithmetic: a 4 KiB offset must be
added as bytes, not multiplied by the 1,088-byte perf metadata structure. The old
calculation can select pages outside the allocation, potentially exposing other
memory or crashing. No exploit or live mapping was attempted. The full allocated
metadata area may be writable; data and AUX remain read-only. This intentionally
extends the native one-physical-page write boundary to one reported metadata
page, within its zeroed allocation. AUX mappings must begin beyond the complete
normal buffer. Unaccounting releases every charged metadata page, preventing the
old residual charge after unmap. The size check rejects an undersized metadata
mapping before unsigned subtraction.

The native PFN mapping implementation, event revocation, LSM/resource checks,
references, locking and failed-mapping cleanup remain. Obsolete ACK clearing of
page->mapping is removed: native LTS explicitly avoids using that field for
these mappings. Host tests exercise both allocation-layout translations, with
15,076 checks and old-source controls. They model addresses/accounting rather
than allocating kernel pages or testing real VM/PMU behavior. This review does
not close the separately recorded shared-EOF page-size compatibility question.
No remote resource was accessed, including links embedded in the commits.

# Kernel header archive history

`evidence/kheaders-history-review.json` resolves eighteen commits and one merge
within that count. Preserve the native generator, dependency handling and
reproducible metadata; the sole source delta follows header symlinks during the
two copy steps. This materializes build inputs before stripping comments, rather
than shipping references to build-host paths. Historical cpio and final-tar
implementations and temporary reverts are superseded. No manifest dependency
change is needed. The native read-only sysfs export remains unchanged.

The actual generator fixture materializes five headers (three supplied through
links), preserves SPDX lines, strips ordinary comments and produces an identical
archive after input timestamp/mode changes. The native control retains three
links. Build 148's actual embedded kheaders archive also has no links and uses
normalized ownership/file modes. This does not verify incremental make rebuilds,
whole-kernel reproducibility, module loading or runtime tracing. Build inputs
are trusted: dereferencing their symlinks is not a malicious-checkout sandbox.

# Android USB notification lifecycle

`evidence/usb-notification-review.json` resolves four candidates and documents
positive UsbDeviceManager state/event consumers. Preserve the Android USB class,
state file, events, UDC `none` alias and SDK configuration. Native configuration
validation and request results remain. Later explicit ACK removals of internal
exports and old singleton function-device APIs remain represented.

`75d587b500e8` fixes an ACK allocation-error double free and teardown hazards.
Each gadget unregisters its exact device outside the spinlock; all instances use
devt zero, so lookup by devt could remove a different device. Producers check the
live device under the teardown lock before queuing. Draining therefore completes
before unregister and gadget release, and non-configfs callers with no Android
instance cannot queue uninitialized work. State reads are locked; configuration
notifications follow actual native cdev configuration under its lock, and
disconnect clears configured state. `00951cd18f1e` releases the ID only after its
old sysfs name disappears. Callers must still keep their cdev alive during calls.

The extracted host lifecycle model passes 16 cases with an old-code control
demonstrating each defect category. It does not test real USB, sysfs locking,
kernel scheduling or exploitation. Build 150 validation is recorded separately.
No additional remote source was used.

# Accessory singleton lifetime

`68368a960aad` adapts the three historical singleton-lock/publication patches.
The final ACK destructor cancelled sleeping work while retaining the IRQ-safe
instance lock, with no unlock on the last-reference path. It could also cancel
its own HID worker. Puts used the global instead of their argument; gets returned
the global again after unlocking. Native kref operations now return and release
the retained object, prevent resurrection and defer final cancellation to a
separate worker. The singleton remains reserved until old work has drained.
Creation reserves the slot, allocates and registers outside the lock, and only
then publishes. Failed registration cannot leak a referenced object to readers.
An open during registration may briefly receive ENODEV before publication.

Eight extracted host cases cover registration/allocation failure, competing
creation, lookup identity, passed-object release, zero-reference lookup and final
puts from ordinary/worker contexts. Original code fails in six defect categories;
the adapted code passes. This does not prove kernel concurrency or USB behavior.
The whole accessory addition remains provisional: EP0/HID ownership, bulk request
teardown and worker targets after misc deregistration still require review.
Local framework code positively consumes the accessory misc device and events.
No native LTS code is replaced and no remote resource was accessed. Build 151 is
recorded independently when artifact verification finishes.

# Accessory HID input and bounds

`5c0491d3dd72` routes accessory reports through native hid_safe_input_report.
The old direct raw call bypassed driver_input_lock and therefore the LTS input
quiescing during failed probe/remove. Both extent arguments remain the actual
transfer length, so short reports cannot gain permission to access extra bytes.
Native empty/missing-driver checks and HID/BPF callbacks apply; the accessory's
own HID driver defines no raw_event callback. All eleven non-accessory files in
the ACK buffer-size change remain whole-file identical to 6.18.57, including
later zero-length numbered-report, formatting and input-quiescing fixes.

The 4096-byte descriptor/low-level report limits and ID range 1..32 are retained.
The ID range does not prove a bound on all dead/in-flight allocations during
replacement; cleanup and EP0 references remain under review. Generic accessory
SuperSpeed burst descriptors stay at six. No throughput or physical controller
runtime behavior was tested. The extracted input-entry model passes 24 cases
with 12 old-code failures, but stubs report processing and cannot prove object
lifetime. No additional remote source was used.

# Accessory control and HID ownership

`25d9d4d59ed4` gives each queued control request an owned device reference,
request metadata and, where relevant, a HID reference acquired under the list
lock. Successful queue completion and failed queuing release ownership once.
Initialization retains list visibility and its own reference, so disconnect or
replacement can retire it before it publishes. Failed initialization unlinks the
object; parse errors propagate. Sleeping destruction is deferred until all list,
initialization and control references have gone, retaining the owner meanwhile.

The descriptor/ID limits are supplemented by a 64-object total allocation cap,
including retired objects awaiting completion or deferred destruction. Repeated
replacement can receive EBUSY until cleanup runs; this deliberate backpressure
preserves 32 active IDs while bounding replacement growth. Allocation failure
keeps the old ID. Workers use an owned device-model reference, and cleanup gates
control producers and drains notifications before misc deregistration. Final
release drains work before dropping that device reference. Configfs instance
storage is now freed. Native USB/HID implementations remain unchanged.

31 extracted ASan/UBSan scenarios pass without live allocations; four injected
regressions fail. Eight singleton and 24 HID input model cases still pass. These
are host models, not hardware or kernel concurrency tests. The native UDC contract
states that dequeue is asynchronous: bulk read/write cancellation and teardown
remain under review and the whole accessory addition is not finalized. Build
153 is recorded separately. No remote source was accessed.

# Accessory bulk I/O and historical replacement

`98b3d1d421be` serializes readers and writers separately and makes unbind wait
for user-copy/request owners after disabling endpoints. Endpoint queue/enable/
disable operations share an IRQ-safe lock distinct from the completion list
lock. Completion uses the request owner. Async dequeue retains pending read
state, preventing request overwrite/requeue before completion; subsequent reads
wait for the result. Shutdown wakes both directions, zero-length calls finish,
failed OUT setup disables IN, and ioctl strings are copied from a locked snapshot.
A second allocation of the singleton's embedded function is rejected with EBUSY
instead of linking its list node twice. The existing singleton design is retained.

18 host cases pass, including two pthread unbind/user-copy interleavings and two
asynchronous cancel retries. Injected missing-unbind-lock and lost-pending-state
variants fail. Native UDC contracts still apply: successful queueing completes
exactly once and disable drains requests. EP0 teardown remains native; the models
do not verify every UDC's contract implementation or actual kernel concurrency.

Five original/replacement/removal/dependency commits now have final dispositions.
Whole-file deletion identities account for the old accessory/audio-source drivers;
this does not claim a security review of their absent bodies. Modern accessory
protocol/ioctl values match the legacy public macro values. The audio streaming
driver is explicitly retired in ACK and the current mode command is deprecated.
Native MIDI's gadget-parented ALSA path and UDC gadget symlink positively match the
framework's preferred lookup; old android0/f_midi/alsa is a backward fallback.
All native core fixes remain. Build 154 verifies the bulk source separately.
No additional remote resource was used; embedded links were not opened.

# Workqueue allocation and idle hook output validation

The workqueue allocation callback receives writable flags after the original
code sized the allocation and optionally allocated unbound attributes. A callback
changing a bound queue to WQ_UNBOUND could therefore leave the native per-node
array outside allocated storage and omit required attributes. The adaptation
reserves per-node pointer slots whenever Android hooks are compiled, keeping the
callback's queue pointer stable. It then reconciles unbound attributes and applies
native BH flag/active-count validation, power-efficient mode and activity limits
after callback output. Failed allocation uses the native cleanup path. Existing
native initialization, publication, rescuer and failed-pwq cleanup ordering remain.
The cost is (nr_node_ids + 1) pointers for each bound queue when hooks are compiled;
no historical layout or vendor binary compatibility is preserved.

The CPU-idle hook already runs before target-state selection and may return a
negative cancellation result. The adaptation also rejects indices at or beyond
drv->state_count before accessing the state array, balancing instrumentation on
the error return. Existing native idle entry/exit behavior remains for valid
indices. Callback authority remains privileged; these checks do not sandbox
arbitrary kernel code or establish correctness of a selected idle state.

`check_allocation_hooks.py` executes extracted allocator code and idle-entry
prefixes with mocks under ASan/UBSan. It covers 74,088 callback/failure cases,
3,528 comparisons to native behavior without callbacks, and 104 idle selections.
The prior code fails 21,528 modeled allocation/limit invariants and accepts 52
out-of-range idle selections. Allocation shape violations in the control are
intercepted before an actual invalid host write. These are meaningful regression
controls, not kernel concurrency, scheduler or device tests. Evidence is in
`evidence/allocation-hooks-check.json`. Build validation follows separately;
related candidate finalization remains pending. No new remote resource used.

The associated nineteen source/merge candidates are individually reconciled in
`evidence/idle-workqueue-hooks-review.json`. Final generic idle, worker creation,
watchdog and timer customization interfaces remain. Exact native function
comparisons confirm that timer bucket arithmetic, worker lifecycle and watchdog
stall/report logic differ only by the identified callbacks. The timer callback
receives raw expiry before native granularity conversion and can change deadlines;
that authority is intentional and must obey the callback context.

Both credential-hook add/revert cycles are accounted for through final explicit
retirement. Native credential bodies remain intact, with only a separately
scoped export namespace difference. The old CFI-specific suppression of delayed
work callback validation remains reverted. Its complete textual witness was
rechecked after the new allocation changes, using temporary indexes. Scoped
module registration searches did not locate consumers of these six retained
hooks; that uncertainty is recorded and is not used to remove them.

# Android page-size-compatible trace subbuffers

The two ACK changes 992cefeb8225 and 4d75f609dbe8 have a positive SDK
consumer: the pinned Goldfish 16 KiB command line enables page_shift=14, and
Perfetto's CpuReader::ReadAndProcessBatch reads one reported userspace page and
rejects a nonzero shorter read as a partial-page error. Local file and repository
pins are recorded in `evidence/trace-subbuffers-consumers.json`. No remote source
was consulted, and Perfetto itself was not built or executed.

Default ordinary and persistent trace buffers now use get_order(__PAGE_SIZE).
The tracefs subbuffer-size writer rounds requests to the emulated page granularity
before deriving physical allocation order. Native 4 KiB mode retains its sizing.
Keep the native maximum of 128 physical pages: ACK's expression
7 + get_order(nr_subpages) also yields 7 for the supported x86 ratios 1 and 4,
because get_order expects bytes, not a count of pages. Do not reinterpret that
expression to enlarge the maximum. The native write-counter bound, allocation
failures, mmap restrictions, locking, snapshot update and rollback remain intact.

Reject zero or above-limit KiB values before multiplication and narrowing to int;
otherwise crafted large inputs can wrap into small accepted allocations. This
also avoids old signed page-count overflow. The change rejects out-of-range
aliases while matching ACK for valid requests. Larger default subbuffers can
increase minimum per-CPU allocation and reduce capacity of a fixed reserved
range; native failure handling is retained. No native LTS fix is removed or
weakened. The old writer/polling API for the excluded ARM protected-hypervisor
trace implementation is a separate concern and is not introduced here.

`check_trace_subbuffers.py` passes 12,048 extracted size cases, 10,860 native
valid-input comparisons, 11,402 ACK valid-input comparisons and 68 allocation
wrapper cases under ASan/UBSan with the kernel's -fno-strict-overflow setting.
Controls expose 18 small-buffer mismatches and 114 oversized values accepted by
the old calculation. Whole ring_buffer.c equality after removing only the new
allocation default/header/comment verifies preservation of native algorithms;
the writer's complete locking/snapshot/rollback suffix and range-allocation body
are checked against LTS. These host checks do not test kernel tracing, mmap,
Perfetto parsing or boot behavior. Full emulator build evidence is recorded
separately after the source commit is built.

# Tracing and signal hook contracts

Eighteen candidates are reconciled individually in
`evidence/tracing-signal-review.json`, including the page-size adaptation above.
The three historical diagnostic hook add/revert/re-add sequences retain their
final source interfaces, while obsolete ftrace-size hooks and LTO recordmcount
workarounds stay retired. Native export-type/event support is already present.
Exact function/file comparisons preserve native tracing iteration, user-object
suppression, lockdep/context tracking and signal permission/send algorithms.

The callbacks are privileged policy surfaces. Ftrace enter and dump callbacks
can suppress diagnostics; buffer access can reveal trace data, and callbacks
must preserve buffer bounds/termination when allowing printing. Enter/exit hooks
are not balanced on veto/disabled/non-oops returns. The hung-task hook retains
ACK pass-scoped need_check state, so a false value persists until a callback
resets it; completion is skipped on traversal budget/RCU breaks. This can suppress
subsequent hang diagnostics and their panic action. Preserve that ACK policy
contract for vendor customization, while documenting it explicitly rather than
claiming every task gets an independently initialized selector. Native task-state
filtering inside check_hung_task is untouched.

Preemption/IRQ callbacks are NMI-excluded and must avoid recursion or sleeping.
Signal observation occurs before the sighand lookup and can report a failed send;
exit observation runs in the fatal group-exit locking context. Task pointers are
borrowed. The independently scoped wake-veto and process-reaping hooks are not
certified by these observation-hook decisions. Local exact registration searches
did not identify module consumers, so these generic interfaces are retained with
that uncertainty recorded. No callback, signal or diagnostic path was executed.

# Futex wake callback output initialization

The OEM futex wake hooks introduced by d3d80333c42a can be registered
independently. ACK left target_nr uninitialized and passed it by value to the
per-waiter and completion hooks even when no traversal callback supplied a value.
Initialize it to nr_wake, the native requested wake budget; a traversal callback
can still replace it. This changes no native waiter matching, PI-state rejection,
bitset, wake-count, strict-zero, hash-lock or wake-queue behavior. The value is
hook metadata, not a replacement for the native nr_wake termination condition.
It avoids an indeterminate/stack-derived value in partially registered callback
configurations; it does not sandbox arbitrary kernel callbacks.

`check_futex_hook_state.py` exercises the entire extracted futex_wake body under
ASan/UBSan with mocked hash buckets/locks and all eight combinations of the three
hooks. All 43,008 cases match native return values and woken-task masks, with
6,852 observed hook arguments. A defined-poison model of the old unset output
fails 2,457 cases. The control explicitly initializes to INT_MIN to demonstrate
unset-output propagation without claiming to execute undefined C reliably.
This verifies host control flow only, not futex concurrency, module loading or
scheduler behavior. No LTS fix is removed and no new remote source is used.

backend, leaving native core/sysfs code exactly intact. This does not assert
that an unknown external module could never register a remote processor; the
current SDK target scope, not failure to find a registration, is the reason.
Both historical add/revert/re-add sequences are distinguished. Final ACK
calls recovery_set under the remoteproc mutex in both enabled/disabled sysfs
branches, after changing the flag; enabled triggers recovery after unlocking.
The recovery-work hook is on the common exit path and is not success-only
despite its comment. The earlier broad disposition for 4824c0ad359b is replaced
with this explicit whole-feature omission, without increasing the final count. No remote processor, callback or alternate-config kernel was run.
No new remote resource was consulted.

# UFS platform scope and upstream interrupt fix

Fifteen candidates are examined individually in `evidence/ufs-review.json`.
The pinned Goldfish x86 fstab uses `/dev/block/vdc` for data and the ramdisk
loads virtio block/PCI modules in both ordinary and 16K configurations. UFS
core, PCI and platform drivers are nevertheless compiled; they are not called
disabled. The target-specific omissions cover UFS controller hooks, UniPro
monitoring, physical power-mode/rail handling and TX equalization, rather than
generic Android block storage or encryption. Native PCI matching also supports QEMU UFS (Red Hat 0x0013), so these paths
are not universally physical-only. The pinned SDK uses virtio; adding a QEMU
UFS device is a different configuration. Unknown vendor modules, arbitrary
passthrough configurations and physical-device behavior are not certified.

Native 6.18.57 UFS drivers and headers are retained in full. In particular,
`ff333def3147` already removes threaded IRQ delegation to fix the regression
addressed by ACK's opt-out hook, retaining the upstream MCQ and lock-inversion
fixes. The port does not undo that fix to recreate the old callback context.
Native keyslot restoration remains intact; hardware-specific bulk-restoration
and initialization/self-test callbacks are omitted for virtio SDK storage.
This does not claim equivalent FIPS testing on future physical controllers.
The universal VCC delay and TX tuning changes can matter for physical-device
reliability and data integrity; their omission is limited to this target.
Generic fscrypt, inline crypto and software fallback remain selected. ABI
snapshots, enumerator-ignore wrappers and DECLONLY type-view workarounds are
separately identified as historical KMI-only material. The add/remove/re-add
update_sdev sequence and parallel key-restoration variants are accounted for.
No storage runtime test or new remote resource was used.

# IOMMU callback allocation and cookie contracts

The generic IOVA/DMA hooks are retained for Android vendor source compatibility.
Three adaptations preserve native allocation invariants: reject alignment shifts
at or above BITS_PER_LONG before shifting or taking the tree lock; clear
__GFP_COMP before allocating pages which will be split/freed individually; and
preserve caller-requested __GFP_ZERO after the allocation-flags callback. The
latter prevents a callback from exposing uninitialized allocation contents where
native iommu_dma_alloc requests zeroed memory. Valid alignment values and other
allocation policy flags remain customizable. Invalid shift output now fails the
allocation instead of invoking undefined behavior. These are callback contract
checks, not protection against a malicious kernel module.

The free-tracking callback also needs a real iova_domain. Native cookie pointers
share a union: an MSI-only cookie is a smaller iommu_dma_msi_cookie, not a null
DMA cookie or a valid iova_domain. The port preserves native MSI cursor rollback
and returns before deriving the IOVA pointer or calling its tracking hook. The
matching MSI allocation path already bypasses the allocation-tracking hook.
Normal and queued IOVA freeing keep their native operations and notification.

`check_iommu_hook_bounds.py` exercises the extracted whole page allocator,
whole free routine and alignment prefix with modeled dependencies under
ASan/UBSan: 22,880 allocation cases, 624 alignment cases and 24 cookie cases.
Old controls expose 6,120 zeroing/compound contract violations, 12 invalid MSI
callback contexts and an invalid shift diagnosed by UBSan. Tests model allocator
outcomes and cookies; they do not execute DMA hardware, the kernel page
allocator, the RB tree, actual callbacks or concurrency.

The initial integration had retained deferred-map-sg header fields while keeping
native iommu_map_sg without the implementation. Those misleading declarations
are removed together. Final ACK supplies the operations in the ARM64
arm-smmu-v3-kvm-pv driver; its Makefile selects ARM_SMMU_V3_PKVM_PV and Kconfig
requires ARM64/KVM. The selected x86 SDK cannot use that backend. The commit's
virtio-IOMMU proposal describes future work, not an implemented SDK provider.
This target omission does not assert that arbitrary out-of-tree drivers could
never implement the interface. Native core mapping, synchronization, cleanup
and all 6.18.57 fixes remain unchanged. Generic vendor-data fields and other
independently reviewed interfaces remain. No new remote source was consulted.

# Intel IOMMU native fixes and protected-host gates

Nineteen candidates are individually resolved in
`evidence/intel-iommu-gates-review.json`. Native PASID publication cache flushing,
SVA/nested PIOTLB invalidation and inaccessible-device ATS guards are preserved.
The ACK build-conflict reverts are not replayed. The whole current Intel IOMMU
subtree is byte-identical to 6.18.57, including later context teardown, alias,
replacement and deferred-free fixes.

The SDK command-line files and ACK early-option gate are pinned in the evidence.
Protected-host-specific feature restrictions, host-EPT diagnostic exclusions,
cache-management handoff, forced IOMMU policy and S3 rejection are omitted for
this ordinary SDK mode. Native KVM/IOMMU support remains. QI array zeroing and
return-value follow-ups are assessed against native allocation/fallback: native
desc_status is separately kcalloc-initialized, and native init_qi returns void.
The pKVM leaf_ptes_only accumulator does not exist in native void-returning
unmap recursion. These are code-specific findings, not subject-name filtering.
Generic overlap-walker and partial-superpage defenses remain provisional pending
additional analysis of native caller contracts; not all pKVM-labeled changes
are presumed unnecessary. No new remote source or hardware runtime test was used.

# Intel shared page-table walk defenses

The native host portions of ACK e55de9a5a0a8 and 46cbf93c3172 are retained.
`pfn_to_dma_pte` rejects descent through a superpage with -EEXIST, distinguishing
allocation failure (-ENOMEM) and unsupported addresses (-EINVAL). The check is
also performed after a failed table-install compare/exchange, unlike ACK's
else-if placement, so an injected winning leaf cannot bypass it. All native
callers use error pointers consistently. Dirty tracking returns a lookup error
before computing a page size from an unset level. The native unmap recursion
warns and skips a partially covered superpage instead of interpreting its DMA
payload as a table. The pKVM-only second unpin walk is not introduced.

This does not add support for arbitrary partial unmaps. The native iommu_unmap
and iommu_unmap_fast contracts prohibit subdividing a prior map; IOMMUFD and
VFIO enforce whole-area boundaries for userspace. An invalid kernel caller can
still receive an overstated unmapped byte count after the recursive guard skips
a leaf. Similarly, Intel mapping can leave partial mappings on a later error
without reporting partial progress. These existing API limitations are not
claimed fixed, and these tests do not prove every kernel caller obeys its
contract. The added guards prevent the specific payload-as-table traversal.

The precomputed-PTE part of d74e32c4ba53 is adapted to remove a redundant,
previously unchecked walk in superpage conversion. The caller's native rounded
page count and PTE-page bound (dce043c07ca1) remain intact. Native per-entry
invalidation is retained; the optional flush batching is omitted to avoid
changing its timing without a target compatibility requirement. The native
cf8a9672fc25 gather-before-freelist splice remains unchanged, as do context,
PASID, cache-tag and alias teardown fixes.

ACK fcb3f4455248's PASID error distinction is adapted to the current native
setup helpers. OOM now reports -ENOMEM rather than -ENODEV, matching ACK's
source API behavior. The complete native pasid.c is unchanged after normalizing
only error-pointer spelling/propagation; especially, 36244dfd3853's flush before
publishing a PASID table remains. Native replacement helpers differ from ACK's
historical duplicate routines and are not reverted.

`check_intel_iommu_walk.py` executes extracted walkers and superpage conversion
with modeled pages, allocation, compare/exchange and flush operations: 100 walk,
18 unmap and 2,108 conversion cases pass ASan/UBSan. The pre-fix controls observe
22 invalid leaf descents, including one injected compare/exchange loss. Valid
superpage conversions retain the exact observed sequence of native flush
ranges. These are host control-flow checks, not hardware, concurrency or boot
verification. Whole-file PASID normalization is a source check, not an OOM
runtime test. Only local pinned source/history was used; commit-message URLs
were not fetched.

The shared `iommu_context_addr` NULL-root check from 975cf29244d2 is also
retained. Native initialization allocates the root before device registration,
so this is defensive handling of an invalid call order, not evidence that SDK
userspace can trigger the pKVM hypercall attack. The extracted helper passes
1,500 checks covering missing roots, allocation failure, copied contexts and
legacy/scalable bus/device indexing. A pre-fix NULL-root control is rejected by
UBSan. No register access, physical IOMMU or concurrent initialization is tested.

ACK a9ad95721fc4's removal of top-level PGD freeing from the private
`dma_pte_free_pagetable` conversion helper is retained. Its sole caller is
`switch_to_super_page`, which replaces mappings while retaining the domain and
its root. Full root teardown remains in native `domain_unmap` and domain-free
paths. The six removed lines have no replacement from another ACK file; native
child-table clearing/freeing and flush order are unchanged. This avoids giving a
mapping-conversion helper authority to destroy its caller's retained root.
The audit does not assert that the full-address-range condition is unreachable
for every possible hardware address-width/superpage combination. This small
source ownership change is checked against the local call graph and the full
emulator compilation; no hardware reproduction or runtime teardown test is
claimed.

## ARM IOMMU and SMCCC declarations

The SDK selects x86_64 and disables COMPILE_TEST. ARM LPAE page tables require
ARM, ARM64 or COMPILE_TEST; SMMUv3 and ACK's PKVM_PVIOMMU guest driver require
ARM64. The latter binds a `pkvm,pviommu` device-tree node and issues ARM HVCs.
These are positive architecture and protocol boundaries, not a failed consumer
search. Native ARM IOMMU drivers and their existing modularity remain untouched.

Remove the initial import's orphan IDMAP quirk and put_pages callback from
io-pgtable.h, keeping the complete native header. The ACK implementation belongs
to ARM LPAE page tables and protected SMMUv3 DMA reference accounting; adding
only the declaration provided no functioning callback contract on the SDK.
Native Intel/AMD DMA ownership is unchanged. Preserve native ARM walkers rather
than introduce the alternate reference-counted implementation or its historical
wrong-pointer, double-accounting and split-invalidation defects.

Restore the complete native arm-smccc.h. Its removed additions encode ARM
protected-guest MMIO guards, relinquish/range operations, device power/MMIO/DMA
requests, pvIOMMU operations, CCA function bounds and a C1-Pro SME erratum call.
They provide no x86 hypercall ABI. The native ARM MMIO_GUARD spelling and reserved
function numbers remain consistent with the unchanged native ARM implementation.
This does not claim those protected-guest interfaces or the erratum mitigation
are unnecessary on ARM; those platforms are outside this delivery. The complete
header deltas and local architecture gates are recorded in the review evidence.

## Input event numbering

Retain ACK 637cf1d86b54's three contextual-action key definitions and their HID
consumer-usage/debug mappings. These add generic input UAPI values below the
existing KEY_MAX and do not renumber native events or enable another platform.
They can use the existing SDK input interfaces; no specific GrapheneOS action
consumer was established, and no action behavior is claimed. Compatibility
retention does not depend on proving such a consumer absent. The initial import
already carries these additions; the current whole files match native LTS after
removing only these additions, preserving all later HID safety checks.

Historical headphone-overcurrent/unsupported-jack switch codes from 3cf5c7142213
were explicitly removed by ACK e5f5e3334480, after merge 73a11b09610f renumbered
them around SW_MACHINE_COVER. Final ACK and native switch definitions agree,
including SW_USB_INSERT. Preserve the final definitions and native jack code;
do not resurrect historical switch IDs that now conflict. This resolves the
recorded merge question by positive revert/final-tree evidence, not by failure
to find userspace references. Hardware input and emulator runtime were not tested.

## DMA synchronization batching and the SDK architecture

ACK f880f0340afa and 5bef17dd8878 split cache maintenance from completion
and batch completion across scatterlists. The only architecture enabling their
new ARCH_HAS_BATCHED_DMA_SYNC capability is ARM64; its implementation changes
cache operations to `_nosync` and supplies a `dsb(sy)` completion. ACK x86 has
no such selection and uses the empty completion stub. Native x86 cache-coherent
DMA behavior does not gain an Android interface from this optimization.

Remove the partial generic batching import and keep native synchronous DMA
helpers, their internal signatures, and scatterlist loops. Public DMA interfaces,
Android exports, and the separate empty-DMA32-zone allocation checks remain.
In particular, retain LTS 4ee4d628c4d9's THRU_HOST_BRIDGE mapping fallthrough and
d0d08f4bd7f6's P2PDMA segment length assignment. No cache completion barrier is
removed from the target: the removed function was empty for x86. ARM64 keeps
its native synchronous cache maintenance, so the missing batching implementation
is not combined with asynchronous operations. This is an architecture-specific
optimization omission, not an assertion that all DMA code is unused on the SDK.
Evidence includes both full ACK patches, native/current file comparisons and the
build configuration. Only local source was consulted; patch-message links were
not accessed. Compilation verifies this restoration; hardware DMA operation is
outside the verified scope.

## Android quota2 accounting and counter lifetime

Retain the quota2 revision-3 IPv4/IPv6 match, named procfs counters and legacy
NETLINK_NFLOG notifications. GrapheneOS system/netd's BandwidthController installs
quota2 rules and reads/writes /proc/net/xt_quota; its NetlinkManager subscribes to
the quota notification group. The local iptables extension supplies this ABI.
The SDK configuration enables both quota2 and its logging option. These are
positive userspace and configuration consumers, not inferred hardware needs.

Keep ACK's short-write termination, whole-message zeroing, bounded string copies,
and usersize boundary before the private master pointer. These respectively
avoid parsing uninitialized stack bytes, disclosing uninitialized notification
bytes, unbounded string handling, and copying a kernel pointer to userspace.
Keep the proc_ops conversion and Android's exact quota-threshold notification
behavior. The historical GROW inversion and NO_CHANGE threshold semantics are
unchanged; no new packet-policy behavior is claimed.

ACK 550e926637c5 fixes a use-after-free when a second rule acquires a newly
published counter before its proc entry creation fails. Retain that reference
handling. Adapt final destruction to call proc_remove on the owned entry,
initialized to NULL, after dropping the list lock. A surviving counter whose
creation failed has no proc entry: after its final list removal, another thread
can create a same-name counter and proc node. The old name-based removal could
then delete that replacement's node. The NULL-aware removal now leaves the
replacement alone. For a successful creation the old node occupies the name
until removal. Native procfs rundown drains active callbacks before the counter
is freed; no sleeping proc operation is moved under a spinlock.

Release the logging socket on proc-directory/xtables-registration failure and
module exit, after unregistering matches and removing proc entries as applicable.
This closes resource leaks without changing the notification wire format.
The implementation remains globally named in init_net, as in ACK: no network
namespace isolation is claimed. The SDK disables user namespaces. A shared
counter surviving initial proc creation failure can still lack its proc node
until fully removed and recreated; this adaptation does not promise availability
under allocation failure.

check_quota2_lifetime.py executes extracted lifecycle functions with modeled
procfs/netlink and injected interleavings. Nine nonlogging and ten logging cases
pass ASan/UBSan. Negative controls detect the pre-ACK-fix use-after-free, deletion
of a replacement node, and the old initialization socket leak. These are host
control-flow checks, not kernel-thread, real proc rundown, packet, or emulator
runtime tests. Native LTS has no quota2 implementation; native netfilter/procfs
security checks are unchanged. Only local source/history was consulted.

## Virtio block and loop validation

Retain native virtio-block request setup, scatterlist handling and IRQ completion.
The old Android discard BUG_ON exemption was reverted, then upstream removed the
obsolete assertion/size member after dynamic scatterlist allocation. Native LTS
also includes the upstream reverts of used-length suppression and IRQ completion
batching. These decisions retain current descriptor checks and native zoned-write
granularity; they do not claim generic used-length validation. The only port
differences in this driver remain the previously reviewed queue-pointer lifetime
fixes. No storage runtime test was performed.

Retain loop backing-file operation validation. ACK temporarily reverted it twice,
restored it in a merge, and finally reapplied it after upstream corrected the
check against the loop device's open mode. Native checks the backing file's mode
and releases its reference on validation error. These fixes preserve read-only
APEX image use while preventing invalid callback calls and reference leaks.
The separate ACK direct-I/O-alignment revert remains provisional: local LTP
expects backing-device alignment to suffice, while native STATX_DIOALIGN handling
also enforces filesystem requirements. Android apexd/libdm request 4096-byte
blocks, but this does not establish every backing-file/offset combination. Native
alignment handling is kept pending that decision; no LTP success is claimed.

Retain ACK's VFS namespace export annotations and corresponding module imports,
including mntget's GPL restriction. These are module source dependency rules,
not historical binary-layout preservation. All 14 export conversions and five
imports from a9d144c159d9 are present, without replacing native function bodies.
A namespace is not a security boundary against kernel code. The SDK module build
checks import consistency; it does not establish runtime isolation.

## Zoned storage capability gates

The pinned Goldfish ramdisk loads virtio_blk, and its fstab uses conventional
virtio storage with ext4/EROFS. More specifically, both ACK and native virtio_blk
reject non-power-of-two zone sizes, and the ACK driver sets neither
BLK_FEAT_ORDERED_HWQ nor BLK_FEAT_ZWOR. ACK's stacking code clears ORDERED_HWQ
when a lower device lacks it. Its other producers are UFS and test drivers;
NULL_BLK, SCSI_DEBUG and NVME_TARGET are disabled by the SDK configuration.
These positive driver/configuration contracts justify omitting the non-power-of-
two and ordered-pipeline extensions, even if a different virtio device were to
negotiate native zoned support. They do not establish equivalence for future UFS
or arbitrary vendor controllers.

Keep native serialized zone submission, geometry validation, schedulers, flush
ordering and queue-depth rules. Do not claim hardware write-order restoration
or replace native serialization with a partially imported pipeline. In
particular, native prepared-request requeue still bypasses mergeable insertion;
the ACK pipeline's new ordered-insertion flag and accompanying DONTPREP nonmerge
flag are not required for that existing path. Generic zoned interfaces and
dm-default-key's sector-correct append emulation remain available.

Retain LTS 81880f84cb36's reference-before-queue-work and drop-on-already-queued
handling. Merge 7820f5b6f1ac instead preserves ACK's alternate CPU-affine
scheduling/reference scheme; its unique resolution is not adopted. The full
native blk-zoned.c also preserves d281f3ac0d00's delayed plug removal and
36a0faaa4e3d's handling of a new write immediately after zone reset. The storage
portion of merge 097816659d76 reverses the former fix and is rejected. Its
initially provisional DWC3 hook resolution is resolved in the later generic xHCI
reset customization review below.
Optional debugfs formatting, comment and preparatory locking/refactoring changes
do not require replacing these native algorithms. No storage runtime test is
claimed. Patch hashes, full native-file comparisons and feature producers are
recorded in evidence/zoned-storage-review.json.

The separate DWC3 SUSPHY revert/reapply pair is resolved by positive native
history: 9cfb31e4c89d guards register access when already runtime suspended and
moves it before core exit. Complete DWC3 core source/header remain native, with
that correction and later fixes; neither historical intermediate state is used.

## Release generation and compiler configuration history

The 32 decisions in `evidence/build-config-history-review.json` account for
historical release-script arguments, eight nonempty merge resolutions, and
compiler/configuration changes. Final ACK explicitly retired the script-side
Android/KMI arguments and BUILD_NUMBER suffix. The current script equals final
ACK and retains native release-tag ancestry checks and read-only Git inspection;
its sole difference from LTS is the three object-tree configuration lookups in
6cb31ee26a30. Existing Kleaf generates localversion metadata. Build164's
`6.18.57-maybe-dirty` is its non-stamped release format, not evidence of an
uncommitted source change or a historical vendor KMI guarantee.

Keep native linker detection, user-program compiler/linker flags, and symbol
whitelist processing. Symbol trimming remains disabled for the SDK's separately
built modules. Module export protection does not force automatic signing, but
the SDK configuration explicitly enables it and all 145 generated module
signatures were verified. WERROR remains enabled by default and in build164.
UAPI_HEADER_TEST was explicitly dropped later in ACK because of Bionic/NDK
header-test incompatibility; it is disabled in the generated SDK configuration.
Header installation remains enabled. The native CC_CAN_LINK prerequisite is
retained and evaluates true; no successful UAPI header compile test is claimed.

The Rust Kconfig stanza and CFI integer-normalization gates match pinned LTS,
including the corrected LLVM 19.1.3 floor, Rust architecture gate, RETHUNK/KASAN
minimum, and KASAN-requires-Clang check. The SDK uses final ACK's Rust 1.91.1.p3.
The software-tag KASAN relaxation enables an ARM64-only mode and is omitted for
this x86_64 target; native sanitizer flags remain intact. Removing two obsolete
Makefile comments in the imported state has no executable effect.

Retain the generic GKI hidden-dependency framework and unconditional inclusion;
individual selections remain subject to the separate SDK dependency review.
Proxy execution and sched_ext coexistence remain selectable with PREEMPT_RT
excluded. The earlier donor-chain, locking and scx lifecycle/callback reviews
cover the supporting implementation; a Kconfig relaxation alone would not
establish correctness. Build164 enables both, without runtime stress validation.

Retain command-line log wrapping with the added helper/macros identical to ACK.
Only log formatting changes: saved_command_line and native parameter parsing
remain intact. Splitting ignores quotes, can compress separating spaces, and
cannot prevent truncation of an individual unsplittable long token. It is not a
redaction mechanism. No emulator boot or log-consumer runtime test was performed.
All evidence in this review comes from the pinned local histories and completed
build; commit-message links were not opened and no remote source was added.

## Early module protection, CFI and boot history

`evidence/module-early-history-review.json` resolves 12 more historical candidates.
The namespace-removal/reapply pair is exactly inverse by stable patch ID. Keep
native namespace verification, metadata scanning, modpost/nsdeps and USB_STORAGE
imports. Missing namespace imports are rejected in the SDK configuration; the
2019 namespace encoding and export layout are not restored. These checks express
source dependencies and do not isolate kernel modules from one another.

The five early module-protection add/revert/reapply commits are represented by
the current list-based export protection. Obsolete placeholder ABI lists and
generated string-array headers are replaced by the current symbol/flag tables.
Native signature verification is unchanged, including fatal invalid-signature
errors, enable-only sig_enforce and lockdown. The original suppression of
sig_enforce is not retained. Historical KMI import allowlisting is not configured:
the SDK disables symbol trimming and does not pass the whitelist switch that
marks other imports protected. Signed-module export protection remains separate.
Build164 checks all 659 protected export names, the corrected four-byte PREL32
lookup stride, and symbol/flag/CRC tables for 145 modules. It does not test runtime
load rejection or establish a sandbox for privileged kernel code.

The original cross-DSO CFI shadow implementation was explicitly reverted and
subsequently superseded upstream. Keep native KCFI, its RCU-protected module trap
lookup and current compiler/init annotations. The old broad __init CFI bypass
and shadow allocation are absent; CONFIG_CFI=y and CFI_PERMISSIVE=n. No runtime
CFI failure was triggered. The separate native ttynull-fallback revert and shared
bootconfig checksum are already represented, preserving console failure handling
and bootconfig size/checksum checks. That additive checksum is not authentication.
The temporary KUnit boot-runner removal was explicitly reverted; keep the runner
and its native no-op stub for this SDK's KUNIT=m configuration. No tests were
executed in a running kernel. Only local sources were used in this review.

## Generic xHCI reset customization

Retain the generic xHCI portion of 9326f2e6e8a3, preserving its hook name and
three reset phases. Although the motivating defect concerns a Synopsys PHY,
this callback is in the common xHCI reset path with no DWC3-only guard. The SDK
enables PCI and platform xHCI. The pinned local emulator binary contains
`nec-usb-xhci`, `qemu-xhci`, and `qemu-xhci,p2=15,p3=15` strings. These are
supporting binary evidence, not a successful device-registry query or proof of
the controller selected by a running SDK. Retention is conservative source
customization compatibility for this generic compiled driver; a specific vendor
callback consumer has not been established.

Omit the separate DWC3 core callback, which takes the physical controller's
register mapping and USB3 PHY array for the documented clock/PLL reset-domain
workaround. This hardware-specific extension is outside the virtual SDK target;
native DWC3 remains compiled, with all LTS fixes. This decision does not claim
that DWC3 was disabled or that an unsuccessful emulator help query proves its
absence. The accompanying merge 097816659d76 keeps the native DWC3 register API
and, separately, every native zoned-plug lifetime fix instead of ACK's reversal.

Place SOFT_RESET_INITIATED after the native Intel 1 ms register-access delay.
ACK calls it before that delay, allowing a callback to touch registers during
the forbidden interval. PRE remains after accessible/halted checks; POST is
called after the reset-bit handshake, including its error path, and before the
controller-not-ready handshake. POST does not imply successful reset or readiness.
The hook has no return override. With no callback, all native register operations,
return values and bus-state clearing remain intact. Registered callbacks are
trusted kernel code and can still invalidate controller state; they must not
sleep, retain the borrowed hcd, or assume missing probe allocations exist. Stop,
shutdown and resume callers can hold the host spinlock with interrupts disabled.

`check_xhci_reset_hooks.py` extracts the actual port, native and ACK functions.
Ten cases per function cover inaccessible/running controllers, both handshake
failures, Intel delay and ASMedia flow control under ASan/UBSan. Port results,
register-operation order and bus state match native. A callback that reads a
register during INIT exposes premature access in three ACK-control cases and
none in the port. These are host shims, not real MMIO, locking or USB tests.
The complete emulator workflow succeeded in build165 at f4b1c885269e, producing
the kernel, 100 common modules and 45 virtual-device modules. All 145 signatures
verify against that build's certificate, the tampered-payload control is rejected,
and all module flag/CRC tables match. This verifies compilation and artifacts.

## Native LTO and module-protection replacements

Five decisions in `evidence/lto-module-replacement-review.json` close the older
LTO add/revert and mixed SCS merge history, plus the subsequent module-protection
replacement. Keep native LTO selections/flags and link scripts instead of the
old separate .lto objects and temporary symversions machinery. Preserve native
SCS annotations, EFI-stub instrumentation exclusions and ARM64/vDSO sources.
The mixed fork resolution retains both Android frequency-accounting cleanup
and native SCS release, including native CPU-mask cleanup between them. The
existing SDK workflow uses LTO_NONE; alternate LTO and ARM64 builds are untested.

The old generated module-protection arrays were explicitly removed in later ACK
history and replaced by current list/flag-based machinery. Keep its applicable
SDK configuration and protected exports. In particular, reject e9669eeb2f45's
sig_enforce suppression and lockdown bypass: native signing.c remains unchanged.
This intentionally permits native policy to reject unsigned modules when
enforcement or lockdown requires it. The actual SDK modules are rebuilt and
signed; historical unsigned-binary behavior and KMI import allowlists are not
preserved. Build165 verifies signatures and table structure, without executing
the loader or claiming runtime enforcement tests. No remote source was used.

## DRM color pipelines and SDK display scope

The 33 decisions in `evidence/drm-pipeline-sdk-review.json` retain native LTS
atomic display, mode-object, framebuffer and color-management code. The new
colorop subsystem is omitted for this SDK target. This decision uses positive
platform evidence: Goldfish packages the Ranchu composer; its DRM backend
explicitly selects virtio devices and requests universal-plane and atomic
capabilities. ACK's pipeline constructor is called by AMD display setup for
DCN 3.0 or newer. The complete virtio-gpu and VKMS plane initializers match in
ACK, LTS and the port, and create no color pipeline. Both virtual drivers are
built as external modules; their absence from the common configuration is not
used as evidence of exclusion. AMDGPU is disabled in the SDK build.

A real userspace consumer exists in the pinned local `external/drm_hwcomposer`
project. Its `vendor.hwc.drm.enable_color_pipeline` property defaults false.
When enabled, capability failure produces a warning, but noncursor planes
without COLOR_PIPELINE are subsequently rejected. This is not a working
fallback. ACK's SDK drivers cannot provide those pipelines either. The port
retains native EINVAL for the unsupported capability instead of advertising
it without the required target provider. Adding a provider for another platform
is outside the present scope. Native vendor source interfaces and the separately
reviewed DRM print, TTM hook and virtio changes remain intact.

Every feature patch was examined, including DATA blob sizes and references,
LUT interpolation, atomic locking/state swap, destruction and suspend state.
The blob-release, bypass-duplication and affected-state fixes address code
introduced by the omitted subsystem; their faulty prerequisite code is also
absent. No native 6.18.y fix is dropped. Native DRM UAPI headers remain intact,
including the existing AMD CTM declaration in its original header. The original
colorop patch's unrelated removal of a deprecated connector-state accessor is
also omitted to preserve the native source interface. Color-pipeline-specific
documentation is omitted with the feature.

Ranchu's guest composer has an RGBA color-matrix operation, while its host
composer explicitly excludes virtio from host display-color-transform support.
These paths do not establish complete color support in every composition mode;
no visual output or runtime behavior has been tested. Build165 already verifies
compilation and artifacts for the unchanged source used by this review.

The old SECURE framebuffer flag and extra panel-mode flags have explicit ACK
reverts whose added/deleted lines are exact inverses. Retain native rejection
of unknown framebuffer flags and native mode validation. Accepting a flag named
SECURE alone would not enforce protected-memory isolation. The review records
local project commits, file hashes and provider queries. Embedded patch/RFC
links were not accessed, and no additional remote source was used.

## DRM capability guard and render-node buffer interfaces

Four further decisions in `evidence/drm-interface-review.json` cover the final
ACK color-pipeline guard, its property-allocation error fix, and two retained
generic interfaces. Final ACK checks for a plane with COLOR_PIPELINE and returns
EOPNOTSUPP without one. Native LTS returns EINVAL for the unknown capability.
That errno difference is explicit: both reject the request on the SDK providers,
and the examined drm_hwcomposer consumer only checks for nonzero failure.
Native legacy color properties stay visible. The NULL-check correction fixes
the omitted pipeline constructor; the faulty constructor is also absent.

Retain 6e1eaaea3c36's permission change for the three dumb-buffer ioctls.
The local minigbm virtio implementation calls create/map/destroy dumb buffers
when its 3D parameter is false. Allowing render clients intentionally relaxes
the LTS permission policy and exposes allocation/mapping code to render-node
holders. Native zero-dimension and arithmetic-overflow checks, output-field
initialization, and file-private GEM handle lookup/deletion remain unchanged.
Other ioctl permissions, including DRM master checks, remain intact. This
supports the SDK's minigbm path; it is not a claim that allocation code is free
of vulnerabilities. The entire ioctl file differs from LTS only at these three
table flags. Result commit: 2fbc2da4ade330c86d97491696f9223c9df7bc8f.

Retain c2030b6f4e11's GPL mode-conversion export through
f9dd0aa8659c, using the native helper and its current string-copy behavior.
This preserves a generic source-module customization interface for configured
DRM; exact optional SDK consumer use is unconfirmed. No historical binary ABI
layout or userspace privilege is added. Build165 covers the retained sources;
no runtime ioctl or display test was executed. Only pinned local inputs were used.

## DRM atomic-state trace routing

Complete 94d485fafb51's category-printer call site in atomic commit while keeping
native checking, return propagation and driver commit ordering. Also allow the
state dump when only the trace mask is enabled: ACK's remaining debug-only guard
otherwise drops these trace messages. In the category-printer constructor,
choose the trace callback directly for trace-only output. ACK routes that case
through the debug callback, which treats debug as enabled under dynamic-debug
configuration and can unexpectedly copy trace-only content to syslog. Other
native debug-printer and dynamic-debug callsite semantics are unchanged.

The existing DRM trace instance, root-writable mask and generic print interfaces
from f30505952f41 remain available to configured virtio graphics and rebuilt
modules. Its kernel-doc reference is now present. The unrelated Nouveau
whitespace edit and historical ABI/symbol-list updates are omitted. Native DRM
initialization failure/unplug synchronization and trace-array checks remain.
This change adds atomic state diagnostics to authorized tracefs readers and
intentionally uses debug severity for category-enabled syslog output; it does
not change ioctl permissions or display state. Diagnostic dumps may contain
sensitive state, and enabling them has CPU/buffer costs.

`check_drm_category_trace.py` tests extracted port/ACK/native atomic functions
and actual category-printer callbacks with host shims under ASan/UBSan. Each
variant checks all sixteen debug/trace-mask combinations, including unrelated
categories, against successful commits, check failures and driver failures.
Both dynamic-debug macro modes are exercised. All 352 cases pass their stated
expectations; ACK controls lose twelve trace-only atomic dumps per mode and
emit four unwanted syslog entries in the dynamic-debug category-printer mode.
The port has neither result. This verifies routing and control flow with fixed
masks, not real tracefs, dynamic-debug callsite instrumentation, concurrent mask
updates or a running display. The SDK build enables TRACING. The full workflow succeeded in build166 at 13caca5ed131, producing the kernel
and all 145 modules. Their signatures and module tables verified successfully,
and the modified-payload signature control was rejected.

## Thermal PM hook decisions are local to each zone

Retain the generic thermal wakeability hook from 238d0b4a83d8, but initialize
its output for each zone in both PM traversals. ACK initializes the value once
before the loop, allowing a callback that sets it for one zone and ignores
others to skip unrelated zones. A fresh zero preserves native preparation and
completion for every zone without its own affirmative callback decision.
The single-zone registration path already initializes its output locally.

The hook intentionally lets trusted vendor code bypass native suspension,
polling cancellation and resume scheduling for a zone whose interrupts and
controller remain usable. That decision must stay consistent across prepare
and complete; this change does not validate the hardware assertion or freeze
callback policy across the PM cycle. Incorrect policy can touch unavailable
hardware or disrupt thermal management. Native zone locking, resume completion
waits, global-list locking and workqueue flushing remain. No callback restores
the native behavior, and the default is never inherited from another zone.

`check_thermal_pm_hook.py` extracts both traversals from port, ACK and LTS.
Thirty host cases under ASan/UBSan cover no selected zone, each individual zone,
and all zones in both phases. ACK skips six unselected zone operations across
the cases; the port skips none and passes zero initially to every callback.
These are traversal shims, not thermal hardware, workqueue or suspend tests.
Build167 at 85ab9dbc6af8 completed the existing emulator workflow. The kernel
image, 100 common modules and 45 virtual-device modules were generated. All
145 module signatures and module tables verified; the modified-payload
signature control was rejected.

## USB lifecycle and generic thermal interfaces

Eleven decisions in `evidence/usb-thermal-interface-review.json` retain generic
USB lifecycle diagnostics and PM hooks, native offload synchronization, and
thermal customization. USB, sideband support, thermal core and thermal netlink
are enabled; the virtual-device build also includes dummy_hcd. Exact optional
vendor callback consumers remain unconfirmed. Their retention preserves the
configured generic interfaces without adding a platform driver.

The USB state helper preserves native active-duration arithmetic, recursive
detach behavior and device-state locking. Tracepoints capture initialized
allocation state and completed state/port updates. Authorized trace readers gain
device name, speed, state, current and authorization diagnostics. The PM hooks
intentionally permit trusted code to bypass interface/device suspend/resume;
a suspend bypass occurs before offload locking and URB flushing, while a resume
bypass still reaches native offload-unlock/reset-resume cleanup. Callbacks must
maintain coherent hardware/offload and prepare/resume policy. Native detached
device gates, default behavior and error cleanup remain unchanged. Native
09f78265a93d already supplies the dedicated offload spinlock, active-runtime-PM
checks and paired references. No older offload implementation is restored.

The thermal event hook defaults to enabled and leaves native message encoding,
allocation/error cleanup and multicast intact. Trusted modules may suppress
events before allocation, potentially hiding information needed by userspace
thermal policy. Non-zone events initialize tz_id to zero; callbacks must inspect
the event type rather than interpreting that value alone as a zone identity.
In-kernel governor processing is not disabled by this event filter. The separate
PM hook uses the per-zone adaptation described above, preserving native zone
locks, completion handling and workqueue flushing.

Omit the USB DECLONLY and generated ABI-graph changes whose sole purpose is old
symbol-CRC compatibility. Current modules are rebuilt. The old public thermal
is-enabled export has an exact explicit ACK revert. The function-instance
pointer is likewise explicitly removed by d23db69132ac; modern accessory and
configfs interfaces remain under their existing reviews. These retirements
are established by source history, not by claims that no consumers exist.
No runtime USB, thermal or suspend test is claimed, and no remote source was used.


## TTM allocation interfaces and backup lock ownership

Retain the ACK flags-based TTM device/pool initialization API, DMA/DMA32
getters, optional beneficial allocation order and ENOSPC propagation. TTM is
explicitly enabled by GKI_HIDDEN_DRM_CONFIGS for source-built module support.
This preserves generic configured-subsystem customization even though the
exact SDK vendor consumer remains unconfirmed. Native in-tree driver and KUnit
call sites are converted with identical DMA decisions and no new opt-in flags;
these mechanical conversions do not enable any additional physical platform.
No historical binary layout or CRC preservation is included.

Beneficial order zero retains native reclaim policy. A nonzero value disables
direct reclaim above that order, after the vendor GFP hook, as in final ACK.
Retain ff792dc1fcca's unsigned result, so orders greater than one are not
truncated to true. This explicitly prioritizes the selected latency policy
over a hook trying to restore direct reclaim. All other allocator flags,
DMA mapping/cleanup, cache handling and the existing MAX_PAGE_ORDER hook clamp
remain. The optional ENOSPC bit changes the error exposed by resource exhaustion;
without it native ENOMEM compatibility remains. Native dmem EAGAIN-to-ENOSPC
conversion and eviction retry logic are preserved. Trusted module authors own
the latency/error-policy choice; no arbitrary userspace flag is introduced.

Adapt the initialization warning to inspect the incoming flags. ACK's getter
refactor reads pool->alloc_flags before initializing the pool, which can miss a
missing DMA device or warn based on stale contents. The port preserves the
native argument-based check. This is a diagnostic correction, not validation
that makes an invalid DMA setup safe.

From 42ab2c618ff1, retain the independent backup lock-ownership correction:
only AOP_WRITEPAGE_ACTIVATE leaves a shmem folio locked. The local shmem_writeout
and swap_writeout implementations explicitly unlock on every other return,
including arch_prepare_to_swap errors. A second unlock could disrupt another
lock owner's synchronization. Preserve the native page-by-page backup algorithm
and put_page lifetime operation. The new compound backup, partial-OOM split
retry, allocation-reserve policy and private fault injector are performance
changes for TTM-backed graphics, rather than the SDK virtio GEM-shmem path.
Their absence does not prove that an arbitrary optional TTM module could not
benefit; no fragmentation or latency parity is claimed. The configured public
allocation API and hooks are retained separately.

ACK's two GPU-accounting backports contain no GPU-stat counter updates: the
first only adds an unused reclaim argument, and the second wraps __free_pages
without accounting. Omit these inert parts. The latter also replaces put_page
with an order-zero __free_pages call in backup; keep native reference-release
semantics with the retained native backup algorithm instead of adopting that
change as a fictitious accounting fix. No omitted NR_GPU accounting code from
an external upstream tree is invented.

All native TTM fixes remain: NULL/handle-aware backup runs (22aa7fb4e7d0),
restore-state disposal (329ddc5d438f), resource UAF repair (73867f253670),
