success-only LRU updates (060f8f302051/d0ce7b07dafe), eviction charge conversion
(9a34b94832c3), missing-resource access handling (b94182b3d722), and the KUnit
PREEMPT_RT lock API (be0c2255d717). Source comparisons in the accompanying review
check the changed files against the native tree, allowing only the reviewed
API/policy transformations and prior hooks.

The extracted-function ASan/UBSan checks cover 49,163 allocation and
initialization cases per port/ACK variant and 65 backup cases per port/LTS
variant. They expose two incorrect ACK initialization warnings and four native
control double unlocks, with none in the port. These use allocator/writeout
shims; they are not real DMA, reclaim, swap, KUnit or concurrency tests. The
existing emulator workflow succeeded in build168 at 7bfbc774035b: kernel image,
100 common modules and 45 virtual-device modules generated; all signatures
and module tables verified, with the modified-payload control rejected. Only
local pinned source and prior build configuration were used; no remote source
was accessed.


## SDK virtual display formats and optional display features

Nine final decisions are recorded in `evidence/drm-sdk-tail-review.json`.
Retain the eight primary-plane 32-bit RGB/BGR formats and their matching virtio
protocol values, together with removal of the host-byte-order-only framebuffer
gate. Goldfish's DrmClient forwards gralloc's format to drmModeAddFB2; minigbm
advertises ABGR/XBGR variants. This intentionally expands accepted framebuffer
formats while keeping native handle lookup, references, allocation cleanup and
modeset initialization. The two changed virtio files were compared in full:
only the reviewed list, translation function, gate and legacy preference differ
from LTS. Big-endian mappings remain source-compatible but are not tested here.

Retain virtual dumb-buffer alignment to the emulated page size. The local
Goldfish x86_64_16k product passes page_shift=14; backing shmem is allocated at
the returned size. The native common ioctl retains zero-dimension and 32-bit
multiply checks and initializes outputs. Alignment acts on the u64 size on
x86_64, and can add up to 12 KiB compared with 4 KiB rounding. This is buffer
sizing, not resolution of the separate shared-file EOF-tail question.

Omit optional CTM signed-magnitude and LUT32 helpers and their new fixed-point
KUnit suite with the reviewed absent feature. VKMS explicitly sets has_ctm=false
and implements native u16 LUT interpolation. AMD's LUT32 calls belong to the
omitted color pipeline. Preserve the existing fixed-point functions and test
suite; the additional tests change no runtime algorithm. Consumer searches are
recorded but are not treated as proof of arbitrary module absence.

The DP Panel Replay sentinel, DSC branch/sink capability parser APIs and VRR
sync-offset matching option are scoped to additional display-link/timing
features. The SDK providers construct virtual connectors and use their existing
virtio protocol or VKMS composition/vblank implementation, not DP AUX/DSC sink
negotiation. Native mode matching/validation and the separately retained source
exports remain. Omit the ARM64 bridge-connector hidden select and its enclosing
Kconfig gate expansion. The latter also exposes DP/HDMI options and can default
the HDMI CEC notifier helper; it is not characterized as entirely ARM-only.
The existing generic hidden DRM selections for source modules remain intact.
No physical display support, optional module equivalence, KUnit execution or
rendered output is verified. Only pinned local sources were used.


## DRM custom-property capacity

Retain fd1f129aa0a0's 96-property capacity for configured DRM source-module
customization. Although the commit describes downstream QCOM demand, the change
is in the generic object property interface and adds no QCOM driver. The exact
SDK vendor consumer exceeding 64 properties remains unconfirmed; retention is
conservative under the compatibility priority, not proof that current virtual
drivers need 96. The earlier 24-to-64 candidate is superseded by this final
capacity, rather than still described as textually identical to native LTS.

Both parallel arrays use the same constant, and native attach_property checks
the bound before writing either array and incrementing count. Existing iteration
uses the initialized count. Registration is by trusted driver code before object
exposure, not an unprivileged interface for attaching arbitrary new properties.
This costs 512 additional bytes per property container on x86_64 and permits more
properties to be exposed by drivers. Native value validation, lifetime and object
access rules are unchanged. All modules are rebuilt against the enlarged source
layout; omit both generated ABI graphs and historical binary-layout promises.
No change to another manifest component is needed. Build169 at 67a2c6c604c7 completed the existing emulator workflow, generating
the kernel image and all 145 modules. All signatures and module tables verified;
the modified-payload control was rejected. No display runtime or vendor
property population is claimed.


## Console capacity and serial history

Eighteen decisions, including four merges, are recorded in
`evidence/tty-console-review.json`. Retain 64 HVC tty adapters: the SDK enables
HVC_DRIVER and builds virtio_console as a module. The count sizes the native
tty driver; native allocation, lifetime and console-index handling remain.
Exact SDK demand above eight adapters is unconfirmed, so this is conservative
retention of configured generic runtime capacity. MAX_NR_HVC_CONSOLES stays
unchanged; this does not imply 64 printk consoles or unlimited hotplug.

Keep native serial Kconfig and reject SoC dependency relaxations for Samsung
and Spreadtrum, including the two Samsung merge resolutions. The SDK selects
8250 console support and uses its x86 virtual platform. The old separate GENI
early-console driver has an exact explicit revert across all files; do not
resurrect it. The temporary Samsung dependency-add/revert pair is also exact.

The historical DCC CPU0 implementation was reverted, leaving only the
independent enable parameter and an unused include; exact source reconstruction
checks this. Later native 4419da5d5d4b reintroduced its own CPU0 support and
kfifo use, which remain intact. Do not replay the historical revert or include
cleanup over that native code. Omit the downstream DCC enable parameter and
its documentation because HVC_DCC's ARM/ARM64 dependency prevents this code
from being selected on x86; this is not a claim that probing unsupported DCC
registers would be safe on an ARM guest. The DCC merge's kfifo/moduleparam
resolution is correspondingly split in favor of the native implementation.

The PL011 pKVM example explicitly depends on ARM64, imports its nVHE module
build rules, and implements ARM MMIO to a configured physical UART. Omit the
example, prototype/module-only fix and ARM64 loader token cleanup with its
SMC/PL011 clients. This adds no support solely for future physical platforms
and makes no decision about x86 pKVM or generic module infrastructure.

The three-path 2fa0ed8af5b0 merge is checked at function level. Final ACK, LTS
and port controller_add/complete_walk are identical; the old platform-serdev
and success-walk tracing branches have no remaining final code. Native scoped
path validation and weak revalidation remain. Keep the native sync-state helper
with its later READ_ONCE driver snapshot; do not substitute ACK's repeated
loads. Final headers all omit the historical driver_init declaration. All
other serial/serdev implementation files in this review are native LTS.
No serial I/O, hotplug, DCC or EL2 example runtime is claimed. Only local sources
were used.

## Wireless ranging and proximity interfaces

Sixteen decisions are recorded in `evidence/pmsr-interface-review.json`.
Retain the generic cfg80211/mac80211 PMSR/FTM and proximity interfaces in the
configured SDK wireless stack. Goldfish explicitly creates a hwsim radio for
VirtIO Wi-Fi and starts wpa_supplicant using nl80211; ACK hwsim concretely
parses the new role/type/antenna/PD bitmap fields. These establish relevant
configured interfaces, not shipping use of every optional capability. Limited
searches of pinned wpa_supplicant, Goldfish and frameworks/opt/net/wifi did
not establish new PD callers. Exact necessity remains unconfirmed and the
interfaces are retained conservatively for Android runtime and module source
compatibility, with no new physical platform enabled.

Preserve appended netlink attribute numbers and actual wire encodings. The
single RSTA capability is superseded by per-mode capabilities while keeping
the old userspace bit. EDCA RSTA does not require LMR feedback; TB/NTB RSTA
does. Per-role peer limits supplement the total pre-allocation bound. PD
requests use distinct bandwidth/preamble capabilities, reject TB and limit
NTB FTMs per burst to four. Threshold and suppression inputs are passed to
drivers: this is not central enforcement of event filtering. Advertised
minimum intervals and concurrent-role support also lack central enforcement
in pmsr.c; driver protocol behavior is unverified. The original periodic
parameter prose does not override native policy bounds retained in the code.

The audit found an ACK null dereference reachable through infrastructure NTB
requests without NOMINAL_TIME. Result commit 18287885bb0e reads it only when
present, retains mandatory presence for PD NTB, and preserves zero/default
behavior for legacy infrastructure requests. Rejecting every old request
would break compatibility; dereferencing the missing attribute permits a
kernel crash through the capability-gated, namespace-administrative PMSR
command. Also read FTMS_PER_BURST as its policy u8 and AW_DURATION as its
policy u32, bounded at 255 before assignment. The first width mismatch also
exists in native LTS; correcting it does not remove an LTS protection. These
reads avoid using padding or the wrong byte on other byte orders; only the
x86 host is tested. Correct misleading antenna, spatial-stream and result
availability-window comments to their existing u8 wire format, without
changing serialized data.

Accepting zero FTMs with a nonzero maximum deliberately differs from native
LTS's nonzero requirement: ACK defines zero as driver/firmware auto-selection.
Keep that Android behavior, the advertised upper limit, and PD/EDCA limits;
drivers remain responsible for choosing supported counts. This is a bounded
compatibility relaxation, not a claim that arbitrary driver auto-selection
has been validated. No other native request protection is dropped: retain
cfbda103aeae preamble range checks, fbaa8c31ef94 unsupported LCI/civic errors,
0caf6416bfbb exactly-one measurement validation, and native chandef parsing.
Native wiphy_work initialization, cancellation and abort ownership preserve
133684982dd0/a1b7a843f12a deadlock and teardown fixes, without KMI work aliases.

PD start/stop are administrative and use wiphy/RTNL locking, rfkill and driver
callback checks. Registration rejects a PD provider lacking start/stop;
current mac80211/hwsim supplies neither callback, so retaining the interface
cases does not claim a working PD interface. Secure-RTT checks guard PD
management operations. Addressed authentication-only RX keeps newer native
frame-length checks. Optional ROC MAC filtering requires advertised support;
current mac80211 does not implement/advertise it. Physical-driver signature
updates merely keep the common callback source interface coherent.

Secure LTF seed installation requires both SECURE_LTF and SET_KEY_LTF_SEED,
existing cipher/key validation and a pairwise key. Its binary policy allows
at most 48 bytes, including zero length; cipher-specific requirements remain
with the driver. Key-add tracing contains metadata, not seed bytes. Retaining
these APIs does not supply PHY security or establish accurate/trustworthy
ranging. No historical KABI reserves are retained.

`check_pmsr_parser.py` exercises the actual extracted FTM parser and structures
with prevalidated-attribute shims under ASan/UBSan. The port passes 1,033
cases and a separate missing-nominal probe; the ACK control passes 981 safe
cases, records 976 getter/payload-width mismatches, and reproduces the null
dereference in its separate probe. The test covers roles/modes/feedback,
PD restrictions, timing fields, thresholds and native location capability
checks. It does not exercise netlink policy traversal, peer-list allocation,
radio I/O, concurrency or big-endian execution. Build 170 passed the existing emulator workflow and
produced the kernel image, 100 common and 45 virtual-device modules. All 145
signatures verified against the exact certificate; a tampered payload was
rejected, and generated export/CRC tables passed inspection. All
sources used here are local; commit-message URLs were not accessed.

## Wireless authentication and lifecycle interfaces

Twelve decisions are recorded in `evidence/wifi-auth-interface-review.json`.
The three UHR candidates already exist in native LTS: their complete ordered
added/deleted lines equal 335c515932a5, fe44ae31ca78 and 84263694c8d1. The full
UHR definitions header, rate helpers, capability/operation validators and AP
PHY validation match native source. Keep the corrected errno semantics and
separate full-operation attribute, not the earlier buggy validator or abridged
beacon interpretation. Native optional-layout FIXME limitations remain; no
complete UHR protocol support is claimed or added by these dispositions.

Retain EPPKE and the new authentication-frame form of IEEE8021X in the common
cfg80211 interface. AUTHENTICATE/CONNECT require the corresponding advertised
capability; authentication requests require AUTH_DATA. This does not implement
the protocols in mac80211/hwsim. START_AP does not have those same new feature
gates, matching ACK's AP-side handling; do not generalize the STA checks to all
commands. Pre-association key management intentionally relaxes native STA and
P2P-client connected-only checks for ASSOC_FRAME_ENCRYPTION providers, retaining
ordinary ENOLINK behavior without that feature and existing key validation.
Exact SDK use is unconfirmed; retain these configured Android interfaces
conservatively without enabling a physical provider.

Propagate assoc_encrypted from driver response data through queued connect
results to userspace. This allows userspace to condition key-delivery processing
on the driver's indication; it is not independent verification of encryption.
The receive-association function otherwise exactly matches LTS, preserving
78ac450ad462 bounds before status/IE access and BSS reference cleanup. Both
MLME receive/transmit callbacks exactly match native 8f4127a93cf6 length checks.
Existing zero-initialized callers default to no encrypted-association indication.

The probe_peer rename keeps PROBE_CLIENT as an identical-number userspace alias.
Pinned Android wpa_supplicant actively uses that old command and recognizes it
in capability/event dispatch. All source callbacks and reports are updated;
physical-driver edits provide API coherence, not platform enablement. STA probing
requires PROBE_AP, a connected interface and an omitted MAC, while AP/GO probing
requires a MAC. Current mac80211/hwsim does not advertise PROBE_AP; its existing
AP callback still requires a non-NULL peer. Keep that feature contract and do
not advertise a mode the callback cannot implement. Optional status peer/link
fields preserve current callers' -1 non-MLO behavior.

The new stop_link export retains existing SDK callers through an inline
stop_iface wrapper passing -1. Per-link shutdown is an AP/GO driver API; valid
link indices remain the driver's responsibility. No arbitrary-index safety or
old binary-symbol compatibility is claimed. Keep asynchronous event ownership,
whole-interface teardown for other callers, later NAN handling and native radar
cleanup. Removal of duplicate leave in owned-interface destruction relies on
actual netdev notifier teardown and non-netdev unregistration, not skipped
cleanup. Keep the final ACK suspend refactor and its follow-on locking: dependent
interface closure runs outside the wiphy lock under RTNL, while driver/event
operations hold the wiphy lock. Unregistered devices are marked suspended on
success too. This supports the retained configured lifecycle; it adds no physical
platform support. No link, suspend, encryption or radio runtime test was run.
Build 170 covers the unchanged implementation source and generated artifacts.
All source comparisons and consumer searches used only the pinned local trees.

## NAN configuration and regulatory validation

Twelve decisions are recorded in `evidence/nan-validation-review.json`. The
configured mac80211/hwsim path implements NAN and NAN_DATA, and the pinned Android
supplicant contains management, station and schedule consumers. The SDK radio
creation source establishes hwsim use, P2P and optional ranging; it does not prove
that NAN is selected. Retain these shared source/userspace interfaces conservatively
and record that uncertainty. No physical platform is enabled by these decisions.

Keep offloaded-discovery callback guards for USERSPACE_DE drivers, the non-netdev
NAN/P2P opencount guards, and clearing cluster membership on stop. The stop function
otherwise equals native LTS, including 7a22cbc6c6bd ranging cleanup and schedule
cancellation. Netdev notifier accounting and non-netdev stop accounting remain
separate; no runtime proof of vendor netdev-backed NAN teardown is claimed.

Keep the owned six-byte cluster ID, avoiding separate allocation and pointer
lifetime. Start may supply an ID; full change requests ignore it, and mac80211
retains an established cluster. The split hwsim start consumes the resulting
array. ACK's default generator was skipped when the entire optional NAN_CONFIG
attribute was omitted, passing an all-zero ID. Adaptation ca0c99fea3a0 moves default
generation after optional configuration and channel validation. Explicit prefix
and length validation remain, changes do not generate IDs, and no cryptographic
strength is attributed to the identifier's random 16-bit suffix. The extracted
parser and actual configuration structures pass 384 ASan/UBSan cases; the ACK
control reproduces 22 missing-default failures. Netlink policy traversal, nested
band iteration, RNG quality, driver I/O and runtime are outside that host test.

Keep NAN's DFS-required calculation and configured-channel validation. The shared
native beacon helper still permits its already-DFS-available exception: neither
the candidate title nor compilation proves an unconditional ban on every
DFS-marked channel or complete regulatory compliance. ACK's reg_wdev_chan_valid
returned early for every interface without a netdev, skipping the new NAN check.
SDK mac80211 explicitly allocates NAN in that form. Adaptation e36ec95a20b3 handles
NAN first and checks wdev_running, preserving the existing guard and body for
other interface types. Invalid channels feed the existing RTNL/wiphy-locked leave
path and dependent closure. The actual function passes 640 NAN cases and 57,344
non-NAN return/callback-trace comparisons under ASan/UBSan; ACK misses 128 NAN
checks. Radio behavior, regulatory database semantics and concurrency are not
simulated by these shims. This does not certify all vendor netdev-backed stop paths.

Keep separate band-agnostic HT/VHT/HE NAN capabilities, checked serialization,
mandatory HT, and rejection of unsupported multi-radio NAN_DATA. The current hwsim
registers these capabilities when requested. NAN_DATA reception accepts the
required zero ToDS/FromDS form while the conversion function otherwise matches
native LTS. Protected-dual action TX retains existing frame checks and requires
the secure-NAN or userspace-DE interface capability. Capability advertisement is
not independent proof of encryption. NAN_DATA keys are restricted to CCMP-128 or
GCMP-256, keeping native index, pairwise/address, length, sequence and supported
cipher checks; all validator callers pass the device context. Ordinary interface
cipher choices are unchanged. No historical binary layout or module ABI is kept.

Build 172 passed the full workflow at ca0c99fea3a0 and produced the kernel image,
100 common modules and 45 virtual-device modules. All signatures and export/CRC
tables verified; a tampered payload was rejected. Both adaptations are covered
by that build. All sources used here are local.
Commit-message remote URLs were read as local text and were not accessed.

## NAN data interfaces and schedule contracts

Eight decisions are recorded in `evidence/nan-interfaces-review.json`. Retain the
NAN_DATA netdev, management/data station distinction, peer scheduling and owner
notifications in the configured common wireless stack. A data netdev requires a
running NAN interface on its wiphy. Teardown closes dependent data netdevs before
management stop, under RTNL and then the wiphy lock; event-driven stop rejects NAN
where that lock order cannot be followed. Native c2ddd180bbee disconnect wiphy-work
cancellation and e8c75736cfd0 scan-abort handling remain in the notifier. The
per-wiphy dependency should not be described as a stronger one-to-one relationship.
No teardown, suspend or concurrency runtime behavior has been verified.

NAN station creation keeps MAC requirements, permits omission of AP-specific
AID/listen/rate fields, rejects legacy rates/UAPSD, and requires the management
station address for data stations. The actual initial-state test requires
AUTHENTICATED or ASSOCIATED; the old comment's AUTHORIZED wording is inaccurate.
Data stations inherit capabilities and aggregation state from an existing HT
management peer; separate station objects allow that peer to reuse its address
for data with different keys. Dependent stations are removed before the management
station, and reconfiguration restores management stations first. Driver checks
remain necessary beyond the generic cfg80211 parser.

Adaptation e95eaf3986b6 corrects the immutability guard to inspect the management
station, whose capabilities are the source of data-station copies. ACK checked
the data-station type, leaving that source mutable. Initial capability installation
is still allowed until HT is established; later HT/VHT/HE replacements are rejected.
This avoids divergence among inherited capability/aggregation values. The full
change function otherwise equals ACK; the correction was source-reviewed and
compiled, without a live station test. Historical KABI reserve layout is omitted.
Native station worker cleanup, deferred RCU link free, interface-list cleanup and
FILS/probe-template lifetime fixes retain exact native functions, with the sole
station-destroy reporting argument adapted from netdev to wdev.

Peer scheduling retains metadata requirements, all-or-none schedule fields,
32-slot index checks, at most two maps, distinct map IDs, incompatible channels
across maps, nonoverlapping slots on a single radio, compatibility with local
channels and coverage of each peer channel. The shared ULW parser validates
attribute IDs, allowed lengths and remaining bytes; driver-owned persistent
schedule constraints are a separate contract. Both local and peer request counts
are now bounded by U8_MAX before allocation or narrowing into 8-bit counts/indices.
The previous local native path and ACK peer path lacked that representability
check. The extra bound preserves valid representable requests and existing
channel/regulatory checks; it does not remove a native fix. Actual pre-allocation
function prefixes pass 4,112 local and 24,672 peer cases under ASan/UBSan. ACK
accepts 258 and 259 unrepresentable-count cases respectively at that boundary.
Later validation can reject requests too, so this is not evidence of a demonstrated
memory-corruption exploit. The test does not run full netlink parsing or drivers.

Unexpected-frame registration keeps its administrative permission, single owner,
READ_ONCE event target and unregister cleanup. Its current mac80211 producer is
AP-only; the NAN_DATA extension is retained as a shared API, not claimed exercised.
The unaligned-window notification has no located in-tree producer or pinned
supplicant handler; retain it conservatively and record uncertain usage. Its
pointer/length and trace inputs remain driver contracts. Channel evacuation does
have a concrete mac80211 producer under the wiphy lock after detaching the channel
and updating peers. Checked serialization and namespace/owner-directed unicast
remain; delivery, reception and cryptographic protection are not guaranteed.

A host C enumeration of the pinned supplicant header found an inherited protocol
mismatch: eleven shared NAN names have different values, and its old INIT_ULW name
also differs numerically from current ULW. All 101 compared ACK values equal the
port; all 77 substantive shared LTS values also equal it. The remaining LTS
comparison is only CAPABILITIES_MAX growing for appended PHY data. For example,
the supplicant local/peer schedule commands are 160/161, versus kernel 161/163.
This prevents assuming that source callers prove wire compatibility. The named
Android Soong wpa_supplicant includes CONFIG_NAN, despite the older android.config
file not enabling it. Goldfish's actual radio constructor omits the NAN enable
flag and interface-mask attribute, and hwsim's default mask excludes NAN/NAN_DATA;
that constructor therefore does not enable these interfaces. Other clients or
product invocation remain unverified. Preserve established ACK/LTS protocol
numbers rather than renumbering them for an older draft header. No Android source
or prebuilt is modified; the inherited mismatch remains an explicit integration
limitation, not a claim of full optional-feature compatibility. See
`evidence/nan-supplicant-uapi.json` for every numeric result and header hash.

Build 173 passed the existing workflow at e95eaf3986b6, producing the kernel image,
100 common modules and 45 virtual-device modules. All signatures verified, the
tampered control was rejected, and export/CRC tables matched. All review sources
were local. No remote
resource was accessed and no Android OS build, boot or flashing was performed.
# NAN station and key callback conversions

Candidates `3741ffb0100e` and `bd03458d3f2e` retain common wireless-device
station/key source APIs and their in-tree callers, including the mechanical
signature changes in physical drivers. This keeps module source interfaces
coherent; it does not enable another hardware target or preserve binary KMI.
The native station serializer/cleanup and key command validation remain, with
per-function native/ACK comparisons in `evidence/nan-api-review.json`.

`0e85d743ea86` fixes the inherited GET_STATION guard which rejected a netdev-less
NAN management interface although station dumping already accepted it. It keeps
the rejection for every other non-netdev type. Existing namespace lookup, locking
and read permissions are unchanged. Administrative station mutations and every
key command retain their admin checks; SET/NEW_KEY retain secret skb clearing.
Ordinary default data keys remain netdev-only. Key traces expose metadata, not
the secret key bytes. The actual GET_STATION command-body test covers 17,280
cases under host ASan/UBSan and checks cleanup on each simulated failure. Its
serialization and driver shims do not verify real netlink, concurrency or runtime.

`b0ccbc005e38` supplies a missing NAN key-handling fix from the already pinned
local upstream repository, commit `014eec318fc00f0b497bffa04f1a45255ba7ff5e`.
The ACK's final hwsim can advertise SECURE_NAN, but mac80211 still warned when
installing NAN keys and omitted the RX_MGMT driver flag. NAN and NAN_DATA now
use the AP-style peer MFP check. That flag lets a driver select suitable software
decryption when its hardware cannot handle management frames. No cipher,
permission, capability advertisement or native reinstall protection is relaxed.
The sole cherry-pick conflict was adjacent PD rejection; that rejection was
preserved. A host test executes the actual interface dispatch with and without
mesh support (128 cases each), verifies the corrected warning/flag behavior, and
checks all non-NAN types remain equivalent. It does not test cryptography or
driver offload. The rest of add_key is byte-identical to final ACK; its native
deltas are recorded. No remote URL was accessed for this fix.
## Hwsim NAN radio scheduling

Fourteen hwsim candidates have explicit decisions in `evidence/hwsim-nan-review.json`.
The source split keeps the existing `mac80211_hwsim.ko` build target. Final code
retains slot/channel gating, local and peer schedule copies, multicast availability,
per-radio cluster synchronization and protected TSF adjustment. This is the
already configured SDK simulator driver. The pinned Goldfish constructor does
not enable NAN by default; retaining its optional implementation preserves the
common Android and module-source contract without adding a future platform.
The inherited supplicant numeric-API mismatch remains separately documented.

Native `5c79ef616aa1` receive/stop serialization, `13edd8ea7ff2` exact TX-info
length validation, `e7fccad2c8e6` safe queue drain and `6dc76371a9a3` virtio RX
length clamp survive the source rename. Stop and virtio RX functions equal LTS;
native receive function deltas are recorded and preserve namespace/radio-group,
frame-size, rate-index, mutex and cleanup checks. The complete ACK-to-port main
source delta is also included in the evidence, so source movement cannot hide
replacement of newer native implementations.

`796fec2fadb1` completes TSF locking around the remaining receive timestamp
sample and initializes NAN cluster identity/notification state before the slot
timer can run. The scalar-read lock is released before receive/state processing.
A host test invoking the timer immediately from the actual start routine passes
60 cases; both successful ACK starts expose an uninitialized cluster to that
simulated timer. This is a publication-order demonstration, not a concurrency or
exploitation test. Existing RX/teardown and peer-lifetime contracts still require
runtime validation.

`e26a8023bbbf` aligns ordinary beacon timers with the retained absolute
boottime-based TBTT calculation. Keeping CLOCK_MONOTONIC would delay beacons by
the accumulated suspend duration. A host test of the actual callback verifies
9,000 interval/TSF/suspend combinations; 6,000 demonstrate the old delay. Native
stop serialization/cancellation remain. No kernel was suspended or booted.

The retained synchronization simulation is intentionally incomplete: it tracks
2.4-GHz synchronization, approximates timestamps for external media, scans only
initially, and does not implement old-schedule beaconing after cluster merge or
DW contention mitigation. The schedule checks model availability and do not
replace key/peer authorization. Neither compilation nor the focused tests
establish full Wi-Fi Aware conformance, multicast delivery or real-time behavior.
No external specification or remote source was accessed for this review.
## NAN mac80211 lifecycle and shared channel contexts

Twenty-four additional candidate decisions and complete ACK-to-port deltas for
the affected common paths are in `evidence/nan-mac-review.json`. Retain local and
peer scheduling, NDI lifecycle/carrier/TX/RX, management queues, capability helpers,
cluster tracking and channel evacuation for the optional NAN path of the existing
SDK hwsim driver. Default Goldfish radio creation does not enable NAN. The inherited
optional userspace draft-number mismatch remains documented; no full NAN runtime
compatibility is claimed. Public source APIs are retained without historical KMI
padding. An incidental `ieee80211_eml_params` declaration has no consumer in the
examined in-tree areas; external necessity is unresolved and the declaration is
conservatively retained. It creates no object or enabled platform.

Keep LTS `03525b632908` matching-reservation checks, `1c82d0c5d509` paired-context
lifetime and `7f52d554c4c8` explicit-link bandwidth calculation through the new
NAN-aware iterator. Native decrypt, fragment, frame-check and fast-RX functions
remain exactly LTS, as does the offchannel worker scanning guard. Normal bitrate
parser and RX-timestamp algorithms equal LTS after the documented parameter
changes. ACK receive admission does not bypass encryption, replay or authorization.
Native TX skb/ACK-ID cleanup, AQL multicast behavior, S1G bounds, MLE container
bounds, interface teardown and replacement handling remain in the recorded deltas.

`88b3864adac4` restores only running NDIs during NAN restart. Retain LTS
`6fb64029457c` warning behavior instead of ACK's broad failure cleanup, which can
clear driver-state flags before driver cleanup and leave other interfaces' driver
pointers dangling. No real restart was executed.

`fcebd27a0c59` fixes receive-chain aggregation for shared NAN/ordinary contexts:
take the maximum for every user and continue iteration. ACK overwrote accumulated
requirements and stopped at NAN. Actual-function host tests under ASan/UBSan pass
11,520 mixed-user cases and 720 ordinary-user cases; ACK loses requirements in
1,568 mixed cases. This demonstrates aggregation, not radio reception or the
real channel iterator's concurrency behavior.

`2c7e4c663821` extends the native paired-context lifetime rule to NAN channel
removal and evacuation. Reservation fallback can select a NAN-only context;
removing its final NAN user must not free it while a replacement points back.
The existing unreserve/finalization paths release it after breaking the pair.
Automatic evacuation skips paired contexts that cannot actually be released;
explicit replacement-driven evacuation may still detach their NAN user. The
actual removal/evacuation bodies pass 36 synthetic lifetime cases and four
selection cases; ACK makes two premature-free decisions. No live use-after-free,
exploit, RCU or concurrent reservation scenario was executed.

Local scheduling retains bounded arrays, deferred-update exclusion, allocation
before mutation, rollback and automatic backup cleanup. Peer scheduling retains
deep copies, driver-error rollback and pointer updates when channels are removed.
Carrier requires an authorized NDI peer with a common usable slot; it is neither
a delivery guarantee nor a replacement for key checks. Channel evacuation first
tests whether removing a context can satisfy combinations, then mutates and
rechecks. Driver notification/ULW publication and live schedule timing remain
runtime contracts, not outcomes established by compilation.

Build 176 completed at `88b3864adac4` with image, 100 common modules and 45
virtual-device modules, all signatures verified and modified-payload control
rejected. Build 177 also passed at `2c7e4c663821`, including the later chain/lifetime
fixes, all 145 signature checks and all export/CRC table checks. All sources examined
for this batch were local; commit-message URLs were not opened.
## Networking compatibility and retired workarounds

Twenty-eight additional decisions are recorded in `evidence/net-compat-review.json`.
The local netd initialization writes `accept_ra_rt_table` with a negative
interface-index offset. Retain this Android IPv6 routing interface and VRF table
precedence, while keeping the native safe default-router purge implementation,
which already iterates flagged tables. Native local-route flags and error-pointer
checks remain. The tethering BPF source explicitly needs Ethernet-header growth
on raw-IP ingress; LTS `6306c1189e77` already supersedes the historical ingress-MTU
exception, retaining size/overflow bounds in the actual native helper bodies.

The old XFRM compat-format bypass and default authentication-truncation overrides
were explicitly removed by ACK `1cf1608b4abd`. Preserve current native handlers,
including u64-alignment-specific translation and newer destination-reset/netlink
socket lifetime fixes. SDK `COMPAT_FOR_U64_ALIGNMENT=y` with
`XFRM_USER_COMPAT=n` can reject affected 32-bit XFRM calls, as ACK does; this is not
a claim that every optional compat caller works. No removed Android config gate
or obsolete unvalidated wire-format bypass is restored.

The SPI compatibility adaptation in `989123cde71e` is justified by the pinned
CTS `IpSecManagerTunnelTest.getRandomSpi` at lines 1215–1223 and netd's ALLOCSPI
construction. Preserve the native allocation loop, locking, zero rejection,
reference releases and signal exits; change only the duplicate lookup scope to
destination/mark/protocol/family. This explicitly weakens `94f39804d891` global
SPI/protocol uniqueness: SPI-only state selection can be ambiguous and drop or
misselect traffic. The original local upstream commit describes that risk; no
external RFC or link was consulted. Never apply `e6e959238f93`'s removal of
zero-SPI rejection, which would undermine `cd8ae32e4e46`'s list-lifetime fix.
Actual-function host tests pass 772 cases, with 28 intended collision-scope
differences from LTS. Synthetic table/lock callbacks do not verify real concurrent
allocation, cryptography, packet selection or CTS behavior.

Historical XDP-dispatch, compat-iovec and XFRM-inexact-list reverts are accounted
for through explicit reversal pairs and current native code. Keep the safe tagged
`sockptr_t`, current automatic compat import and evolved XDP dispatcher. Exact
native function/file comparisons distinguish these outcomes from replaying old
patches. Retain the pktgen XFRM GPL export for separately rebuilt module source;
its native implementation is unchanged. Native bridge tracepoint guards already
emit all bridge exports for this SDK's `BRIDGE=y` configuration.

The obsolete configurable virtio packet-size symbol stays removed. Retain the
bounded TX skb policy in the existing SDK virtio-vsock transport, including its
zero-copy limit. Goldfish policies permit existing vsock clients; the precise
SDK host RX allocator/version is not established, so necessity of this host
interoperability limit remains uncertain and it is conservatively retained.
The entire common-transport delta from LTS is that cap and its zero-copy minimum;
native allocation/receive fixes remain. More packetization/CPU work is possible.
This adds no future platform and does not claim the SDK uses an old Cuttlefish
host. Build 177 already compiled this code; no additional code change occurred in
this batch. All review sources were local, and no remote URL was opened.
## Networking vendor hooks and socket lifetime

Sixteen candidate decisions are in `evidence/net-hooks-review.json`. Retain
functional allocation/clone/free, socket/TCP/UDP, packet-list, classification,
skb-build and page-pool hooks, plus network/reset/MSS/retransmit exports. They
preserve the requested module-source customization of common SDK networking.
Absence of a proprietary callback is not used to exclude an interface. Omit the
separate net_device layout-preservation change and historical ABI dump/symbol-list
updates; actual configuration guards and current source types remain.

Twenty-nine function comparisons preserve native LTS code after removing the
recorded hook calls and inet-create null-local handling. The complete LTS deltas
are recorded, including the deliberate override paths. Native TCP state/timer
accounting, skb initialization, security clone checks, permission checks and
socket cleanup remain. Newer native `tcp_send_active_reset` signature is retained;
modules rebuild against it. The retired duplicate kfree_skb hook stays absent,
while later hooks legitimately require the net.h includes again.

`fedf7b36bd95` clears the local socket pointer after `sk_common_release` in both
inet creation paths. The common exit callback now receives NULL and its existing
boolean error flag, matching early allocation failures, instead of a potentially
freed socket. Success callbacks retain the live socket. The actual cleanup-label
test passes sixteen cases and demonstrates two old notifications with logically
released objects. Its release marker does not free memory; this is not an exploit,
real protocol-creation, RCU or concurrency test.

Registered callbacks remain trusted kernel code. The packet-list override runs
before native namespace/device choice and must maintain valid list ownership and
isolation. The restricted BPF copy override can return before native bounds checks
and error-buffer clearing, so callbacks must validate and initialize output; a
bad callback can expose stale data or corrupt memory. The page-pool validation hook
can approve module ops outside core rodata, weakening the native origin check.
It does not independently prove module read-only storage or keep the module alive;
callback/provider ownership must ensure both. With no override, the native checks
and cleanup still run. These are explicit compatibility/security tradeoffs, not
claims that callback registration is harmless.

Both receive-window hooks retain ACK placement. The restricted callback runs
after storing `tp->rcv_wnd`, but before clamping/scaling the advertised result;
vendor policy can therefore make the two differ. Classid callbacks can change
traffic classification, and exported reset/MSS APIs can disrupt connections.
Runtime policy consistency and module correctness are unverified. No hypothetical
consumer is asserted, and no future physical platform is added. Build 178 passed
at `fedf7b36bd95` using the original workflow, producing the kernel image, 100
common modules and 45 virtual-device modules. All 145 signatures and module
flag/CRC tables passed verification; the modified-payload control was rejected.
All evidence was local;
no remote resource or Android OS build/boot/flash was used.

## Networking query bounds, CAN teardown and APF

Eight decisions and 26 source comparisons are recorded in
`evidence/net-final-review.json`. `3a910638e101` removes the incomplete ACK CAN
RX_NO_AUTOTIMER fast-path guard. Native `cd830e0bc25e` already closes the rearming
race by cancelling both timers in a workqueue after the RCU grace period, retaining
the socket reference until that cancellation. The whole CAN BCM file now equals
6.18.57, including subsequent RX locking, device lifetime and ANYDEV fixes.
The guard alone was not a complete lifetime fix and also changed ordinary receive
throttling when userspace disabled automatic timeout monitoring.

Retain native L2CAP connection ownership and FLAG_DEL handling from
`d3b739db5dc6`, plus every later LTS fix. Only the defensive NULL-connection check
differs in the complete core file; it releases the timer's channel reference.
Examined native timer scheduling attaches or checks the connection, so necessity
of that extra predicate is unestablished. It does not protect stale non-NULL
pointers. The mlx5 arm64 SIMD refactor is excluded by its explicit architecture
guard; native source remains. The NAN capability comment correctly says
microseconds, matching its UAPI encoding without changing runtime conversion.

The native cgroup BPF query bounds fix `a7131340d0f9` is retained exactly. ACK's
additional mprog revision-size guard remains with consistent internal query
signatures. The upstream rationale explicitly distinguishes older cgroup callers
from TCX/netkit, introduced with the revision field. The latter guard is defensive;
it is not evidence of an old TCX binary requirement. The actual-function host test
passes 564 count-query cases and detects 72 old revision writes beyond declared
size without executing the invalid copies. It starts at the legacy count-field
size and does not audit every undersized request or output array.

The sockmap FIONREAD fix retains disjoint message and native TCP queue accounting,
and unlocks before fallback when psock disappeared. The other file contents are
native. The unused native inline helper remains to minimize unrelated header
changes. All 288 host cases pass, covering verdict combinations, listen sockets,
queue counts and lock/reference balance. SDK BPF_STREAM_PARSER is disabled, so
the emulator workflow alone does not compile this optional function. Synthetic
callbacks do not verify real socket races or BPF syscalls.

Retain the common ACK APF UAPI, callback interface and enabled x86 configuration.
`79035f77e9e3` includes the missing original netlink specification and YNL header
dependency. The functional header equals the original APF introduction; padding,
KABI union relocation, ABI snapshots and arm64 configuration are omitted. No
hardware APF provider or future platform is added. Necessity remains uncertain:
the pinned Goldfish HAL implements APF memory operations in-process, the examined
NetworkStack JNI methods return ENOSYS, and no provider was found in the examined
kernel/virtual drivers. Retention conservatively preserves the current common
Android configuration and module source interface; absence of a consumer is not
used as proof of dispensability.

All eight APF commands require GENL_ADMIN_PERM and serialize per netdev. Native
generic-netlink permissions and namespace handling remain. Attribute policies
bound explicit request offsets/lengths, MAC addresses and VLAN tags. Driver
callbacks must validate backing RAM bounds, initialize the entire read buffer and
synchronize cross-interface/shared state. The whole-RAM size returned by a driver
is not capped by the explicit-request S16 policy. Individual command locks do not
make a multi-command transaction atomic. These are trusted-driver responsibilities,
not established runtime guarantees. Missing callbacks return EOPNOTSUPP.

The optional YNL regeneration attempt failed before generation because host Python

not regenerate them. All sources in this review were local, and no remote URL was

modules and 45 virtual-device modules. All 145 module signatures and flag/CRC
tables verified; the modified-payload signature control was rejected.

`97d96dc1cd01` completes Rust AutoFDO support: the retained Makefile.lib selected

with the ACK debug-info, discriminator, sample-use and function-splitting options.
Both affected files now match functional ACK, while all native Clang/LTO lines

## BPF history, attribute bounds and emulated mappings

`evidence/bpf-final-review.json` finalizes eighteen candidates and leaves the
ring-buffer ABI candidate explicitly provisional. The historical JIT magic check,
dispatcher introduction/allocation/alignment and dispatcher CFI annotations are
traced through their individual reverts. Four pairs have matching inverse patch
IDs; the magic-check pair has identical changed lines with evolved context, and
the last CFI pair removes the annotations after function renames. Native LTS
dispatcher, trampoline, x86 JIT and filter header are retained exactly. The BPF
Makefile retains its full native prefix and adds only the separately ported FUSE
selection. Native RO+X dispatcher images, separate writable preparation and RCU
synchronization remain. Native `__bpfcall` and architecture CFI handling remain;
this is not a claim that every native BPF indirect call has CFI.

Both missing-nospec-header copies are already represented upstream. The obsolete
SYS_ADMIN permission workaround is superseded by native token-aware NET_ADMIN,
BPF and PERFMON checks: `bpf_prog_load` is exact LTS. The local Android loader's
capability configuration and SELinux policy explicitly permit NET_ADMIN, providing
positive consumer evidence. The emulation macro undef before the BTF page-size
enum is retained; that enum still describes the hardware page size. Array-map
data allocation, mmap bounds and accounting consistently use the emulated page
alignment, while the header keeps hardware alignment and all native overflow and
zero-initialization logic remains.

`993352b44d93` completes the twelve minimum attribute-size checks in `0e614c15af5e`.
Initial integration missed the program-query minimum while resolving the native
size-aware query API. Reject buffers smaller than `query.prog_cnt` before invoking
the helper. Preserve the native cgroup revision-size check and previously adapted
mprog query protections. Host execution of actual dispatch arms verifies 2,040
command/size cases; the previous query and sockmap tests also pass. These tests
use synthetic helper/copy operations and do not exercise the entire syscall.

The ring-buffer review found that ACK's enlarged writable VMA permits mapping
producer/pending counters and data writable, violating the native consumer-only
write rule. `8a6b444d4315` maps only the hardware consumer page within that VMA.
Padding stays unmapped. Read-only requests may include only the rounding padding
beyond the actual ring; this permits the separate producer/data mappings of the
4 KB ring explicitly defined in local Android netd. The native default BPF vmops
have no fault callback, so absent PTEs fault with SIGBUS through `do_fault`.
Read-only VMAs lose VM_MAYWRITE, preventing a later write upgrade. No native LTS
permission check is removed. Tests execute the actual mapping function with
remapping stubs over 10,944 combinations, plus negative controls demonstrating
the previous writable exposure and small-ring mapping failure. These checks do
not execute kernel page tables or boot the emulator.

Candidate `ac40f53b8686` remains provisional: Android BpfRingbuf explicitly uses
4096-byte metadata offsets on x86, but the local external/libbpf implementation
uses getpagesize and a combined producer/data mapping with a data offset of that
size. With 16 KB emulation, offset normalization alone does not supply the larger
metadata gap. General offset normalization and user-produced rings must be
examined together before resolving this compatibility question. The observed
userspace code is evidence of an interface to preserve, not an absence-based
exclusion. Build 182 compiles the current corrections. All evidence in this
review is local; embedded links in commit messages were not accessed.

## BPF ring layout adaptation

`9a7c5d1e20a6` resolves the preceding ring-buffer question for both locally observed
calling styles. Android BpfRingbuf passes hardware metadata offsets and maps the
producer and data separately. External libbpf passes the userspace page size as
the offset and maps producer plus doubled data together, expecting data after
that larger metadata page. The callback recognizes this combined request by its
original offset and exact rounded length, maps the producer hardware page first,
then maps data at the emulated page boundary. The intervening gap and trailing
rounding remain unmapped. This applies to both kernel-produced and user-produced
rings, with the existing opposing write permissions enforced before remapping.

Remove the generic MM offset division: it loses the distinction between these
interfaces and cannot insert the necessary metadata gap. The separate hardware
offsets now pass through unchanged. For the combined compatibility case, offset
P and requested length at least P+2D exceed the native available extent
2p+2D-P when emulated P is larger than hardware p. Therefore the special case
does not reinterpret an otherwise valid native hardware-offset mapping. This
supports the local Android and libbpf conventions without asserting a general ABI
for arbitrary emulated offsets. The former divider did not implement one either.

`evidence/bpf-ring-layout-review.json` contains exact deletion proofs for all
three MM paths: every unrelated byte is unchanged. It corrects the earlier
offset-only reasoning, updates 34 reviewed MM witnesses and gives explicit final
decisions for `156b58543560` and `ac40f53b8686`. The existing shared EOF-tail and
DMA accounting questions remain separately provisional. The historical pending
entry in bpf-final-review.json links this subsequent resolution.

Host checks execute the actual three mapping functions over 21,888 combinations
of page size, ring size, offset, extent, permissions and map direction. They
verify each mapped source page and every padding hole, plus both remap failure
paths and the two pre-fix negative controls. The 2,040 attribute-size cases still
pass. These are stubbed host checks, not kernel runtime tests. Build 182 passed
the original workflow with all 145 modules/signatures and flag/CRC tables verified;
build 183 checks the completed layout adaptation. All resources were local.

Build 183 subsequently passed with the layout adaptation: 100 common and 45
virtual-device modules, all signatures verified, modified payload rejected and
all module flag/CRC tables matched.

## Remaining memory interfaces and DMA diagnostics

`evidence/mm-tail-review.json` records sixteen final candidate decisions with
patch hashes, exact source comparisons and local consumers. Vendor/OEM task
storage is reset before the new-task callback, and initial task storage remains
explicitly initialized. These fields support source-module state; they do not
preserve a historical binary task layout. The generic PCP THP order knob retains
validated boot-only bounds and consistent admission/index conversion, with the
previously tested separate high-order CMA list. Nondefault performance effects
and actual SDK vendor registrations remain unmeasured.

Both memfd/ashmem dispatch patches are represented by the single Rust ASHMEM
configuration. Native and compat shmem ioctls reach the Rust implementation only
for SHMEM_FL_MEMFD files; ordinary tmpfs does not gain the ioctl. Native seals and
the previously reviewed Rust checks remain. `bbc9b0705c30` removes an obsolete
Makefile line for the absent C shim/configuration. This does not change the
userspace ioctl contract used by local libcutils.

Stack expansion retains the emulated alignment mask and every other byte of the
native function, including locking, address, guard-gap and resource checks. The
page-migration sysfs switch has a positive bionic linker consumer. Its native ACK
show/store/init handlers remain, with the separately reviewed atomic padding
updates. Map-files enumeration and lookup consistently accept displayed data
ranges; original full VMA names remain valid. Native access checks and references
remain, and empty/arbitrary subranges are rejected. The page helper introduction
is adapted to the final header split and actual page_shift=14 SDK configuration,
including the previously fixed parser publication, 16 KB maximum and documented
ASLR placement-bit tradeoff. This does not resolve shared EOF-tail semantics.

Proc page counters retain the initial integration's correction to ACK's original
double-scaling of shared pages in statm. Convert shared and anonymous counts once
each; their separate rounding can overestimate the combined count by at most one
reported page. Native 4 KB counts remain exact. Local Perfetto multiplies stat RSS
by its reported page size. Extracted-code checks cover 98,304 counter combinations
and demonstrate 65,008 underreports in the original ACK candidate. They do not
exercise concurrent proc reads. The readahead KMI overlay/revert pair cancels;
native short mmap_miss and the full native file_ra_state layout remain, avoiding
the overlay's documented overlap with prev_pos.

Keep the FUSE-BPF output-pointer write rejection from `5c7556339e29` in the built
FUSE_BPF configuration. The verifier function equals final ACK; host checks reject
964 write offset/size cases, preserve the three pointer reads and demonstrate the
pre-fix write bypass. This checks that specific fix, not the entire verifier, JIT
or context-bounds model.

The native SWIOTLB implementation gains only the GPL source export under its
current __swiotlb_find_pool name. The restricted encrypted DT pool feature is
omitted: its explicit DMA_RESTRICTED_POOL gate is disabled for this ordinary SDK
guest, which does not provision that reserved-memory/device contract. DMA partial
cache-sync diagnostics permit smaller sync nents while still rejecting larger
ones; this can also hide accidental under-sync mistakes, a documented diagnostic
tradeoff for the retained partial-sync source interface. Native direction/range
checks and DMA operations remain; DMA_API_DEBUG is disabled in the verified build.
Runtime partial-sync subscribers are unconfirmed, so the generic interface is
retained conservatively. `9d5f5cbffd00` restores native coherent-pool diagnostics:
the KiB-formatting-only change provides no required Android ABI or behavior.
coherent.c now equals LTS in full. Build 184 checks these small cleanups using the
original workflow. All resources used for these decisions were local.

## Remaining selftests and test distribution

`evidence/tools-tail-review.json` records 55 individual decisions, original patch

sources. These changes affect testing and development tools, not kernel access
controls. Test skips must remain distinguishable from successful tests.

`7eab0e190225` removes an integration stub which returned success from
pthread_cancel without cancelling anything. Bionic does not provide that API.
The replacement skips only the thread-based SIG_IGN test under __ANDROID__,
retains all 19 native planned results, and preserves real cancellation on other
libcs. The process SIG_IGN, rearm and deletion tests remain enabled; copying
ACK's global exclusion would unnecessarily suppress them. The original stub
could misrepresent validation and leave a spinning test thread; its removal is
not a kernel runtime security fix. No timer selftest was run.

the USER_NS/SYSVIPC-disabled SDK configuration, plus the zero-length sendto and
mremap overflow-expression compiler fixes. The older broader futex skip and
psock header-guard pairs are reverted in ACK history and are not resurrected.
Native waitv, timeout and wouldblock tests remain. `ce995d281cc8` guards the timer
test's LONG_MAX/LONG_MIN definitions without changing its values or native
signed-ppm fix. Native RTC tests remain in full: the SDK enables RTC_DRV_CMOS,
and the tests inspect device availability and alarm features/granularity. The
ACK blanket Android exclusion is not necessary to compile those tests; actual
SDK interrupt/alarm operation remains unverified.

The shipped x86/x86_64 XML and mappings retain the current ACK invocation policy,
including the corrected closing tag, regmap prerequisite modules, architecture
controller, leaf result parsing and obsolete futex argument removal. Native
futex_requeue_pi now supplies the scenario matrix through harness variants.
The vDSO-mremap and illustrative KUnit examples remain excluded from automated
invocation as in ACK, but their binaries/modules remain built. This test-selection
policy does not prove the excluded tests pass. `0e1904a4843c` removes three stray
ARM-only XML files which the SDK distribution never selects.

Final ACK documentation accompanies the supplied test artifacts and explains
source-module testing; its tutorial patches are inert text, not applied kernel
changes. Its example device-launch commands and external links were not executed
or fetched. The original common test-launch scripts are absent in final ACK and
are not restored. The existing build entry point remains build_virt.sh.

The remaining KVM shared-library patches have positive consumers in the new
ARCH=arm64 protected-VM selftest. That test's assembly, system-register checks,
SMCCC memory/guard contracts and hyp tracing are outside this x86 SDK guest
build. Their omission does not affect native kernel KVM fixes or settle AVF
runtime parity. The BPF, sched_ext, cgroup and proc test-suite expansions are
also outside the existing enumerated SDK test artifact closure. They test
relevant behaviors, and would provide useful future runtime coverage; their
omission is not evidence that the corresponding kernel fixes or userspace
interfaces are unnecessary. Native test files remain, and kernel implementation
decisions are recorded separately.

Build 185 completed at 0e1904a4843c, including the kernel, common modules,
virtual-device modules and packaged Bionic test programs. All 145 module
signatures verified, a tampered payload was rejected, and all module flag/CRC
tables matched. Build 186 also succeeded at ce995d281cc8 with the subsequent four-line libc

These are compilation/artifact checks only. All resources for this batch were

The prior finalized psock assertion decision a755993436b5 was revalidated
after its file changed: the full native source differs only in the sendto

## Remaining filesystem and block interfaces

`evidence/fs-block-tail-review.json` records 43 candidate decisions. The four
exec/read/write/splice revert pairs cancel exactly. Native regular-file/noexec
checks, NULL-position handling and explicit splice operations remain; the old
set_fs fallback is not restored. Statmount helper bodies are native, including
copying results to userspace after releasing the namespace lock. Pidfs remains
unconditionally built as in LTS; the historical FS_PID default workaround no
longer applies.

Current ext4 inline encryption and iomap direct I/O already supply the required
crypto contexts. Both ext4 and F2fs positively use iomap and limit mappings at
DUN boundaries; retain their current alignment/fallback checks rather than add
legacy direct-io crypto plumbing for another caller. Native generic casefolding
retains shortname-store copying for rename races. F2fs still attaches crypto
contexts to merged bios, stores keyed hashes for encrypted-casefolded recovery,
and checks recovered filename bounds. The old merge-repair and synchronization
patches are superseded by these current implementations. Native fsverity lookup
and verification remain in buffer.c and Btrfs; the alternate lookup-hoisting API
is unnecessary for the retained interface. No encryption/verity runtime test is
claimed.

The sole bio.c delta initializes bi_skip_dm_default_key to false. This is required
for the configured metadata-encryption path: stale skip state must not select
an unintended bypass. Runtime ELF_MIN_ALIGN/min_coredump initialization is
retained for the reported 16 KB page size; load_elf_binary itself, including the
native .bss-to-brk gap, remains exactly LTS.

OEM fields in block cgroups, request queues, requests, disks and tag sets retain
source-module customization state. All native fields remain, with no layout
union or historical ABI padding. Module owners must initialize and synchronize
that private state; this is not a promise of globally reset request storage.
Actual SDK subscribers and performance effects are unverified, so these shared
interfaces are retained conservatively. Configfs imports the retained VFS
namespace, without changing its operations or treating namespaces as kernel
isolation boundaries.

Native tag-sharing fairness remains. The ACK performance relaxation targets
single-doorbell UFS logical-unit contention, while the selected SDK uses virtio.
UFS configuration alone does not establish that controller topology. The
ORDERED_HWQ/ZWOR pipeline exclusions use the previously recorded positive virtio
capability gates. The native dispatch, insertion, CPU-dead and tag-allocation
functions still match LTS byte for byte. Failed requests retain hctx->dispatch,
its locking and restart handshake. Neither the ACK-only dispatch mutex nor its
subsequent sleep-under-RCU and requeue-stall fixes is transplanted alone.

The kernfs deletion-notification implementation is retained with its race fix
and lock-argument documentation. Applying exactly those three patches to the
pinned LTS reproduces both delivered files, proving that no other native lines
were replaced by ACK. Directory refresh cannot restore link counts after removal
starts; removal clears existing inode link counts for VFS notification. Supers
and node locks are acquired in order and dropped/reacquired while draining.
Write-triggered removals and unattached fanotify nodes remain documented gaps.
Exact SDK watchers are unconfirmed; retain the shared notification contract
without claiming concurrency correctness from compilation alone.

FUSE supports the DONTCACHE read request and forwards its bit through both
backing paths; it explicitly rejects unsupported writes before DAX/BPF/passthrough
dispatch. Native backing-file code differs only in that mask bit. Bionic exposes
the userspace flag, but actual SDK invocation is unverified. Cache eviction is
best effort and is not an access-control property. This batch changes decisions
and evidence only; the source remains the kernel successfully built in attempt
186. Every consulted resource was local.

## ARM virtualization and shared header contracts

The 22 decisions in `evidence/arm-kvm-shared-review.json` distinguish ARM EL2
implementations from shared-header effects. The selected workflow compiles
X86_64, with native Intel/AMD KVM and IOMMU support. ARM PSCI, SMCCC, FF-A,
TrustZone and physical-device assignment implementations are outside that image.
The SMC template explicitly depends on ARM64/KVM/module mode; the hyp trace test
explicitly requires ARM64 and its protected-hypervisor testing option. These
positive architecture contracts support exclusion without inferring absence of
need from an unsuccessful consumer search. AVF runtime parity remains unverified.

`9b04584c27f3` restores the native IOMMU gathering function, removing the macro
extraction whose extra caller is ARM hyp. Disjoint-range and page-granularity
flushes and range accounting remain exactly native. The independent two-u64
Android iommu_domain vendor-data field is retained for source-module fault
handlers; its particular SDK subscriber is unresolved. The owner must initialize
and synchronize that private state. Historical layout reservation is not retained.

The same commit restores the complete native FF-A header paired with the native
firmware driver. This removes orphan ARM protected-guest constants/helpers and
the duplicated errno-table relocation from the initial integration. The later
complete descriptor-offset fix, native b39b08e6bee8 / ACK 84048f33e713, remains in
both files. The earlier incomplete patch and revert have inverse changed lines.
The separate ARM hyp descriptor-bounds fix 85870f0d74cd is not represented in
native hyp code: it is excluded solely by the current architecture target and
must be revisited before enabling an ARM target. No claim is made that an unused
header size macro alone implements that bounds check.

The omitted ARM device ownership, reset and power-lock changes are functional
security safeguards for the excluded assignment backend. They are not dismissed
as ABI maintenance. Likewise, direct-message endpoint authentication accompanies
its omitted forwarding operation, and trace reset repair accompanies its omitted
hyp tracing implementation. The host-stage2 module-ownership extension permits
a specific host-visible protected-guest sharing contract; that contract is not
advertised by this x86 SDK port. Associated ABI dumps and reserved fields are
independently omitted as historical KMI machinery. These decisions do not alter
the retained Linux vendor hooks, module signing or native x86 virtualization.

Validation here is static source/patch comparison and the separately recorded
SDK compilation. No ARM build, runtime selftest, device assignment or protected-VM
operation was executed. No additional remote resource was accessed.

## Shared build and profiling interfaces

The 15 decisions in `evidence/build-shared-review.json` preserve current LTS CFI

The task-exit and munmap profile notifiers remain. UID_SYS_STATS positively
registers its exit callback, and do_exit dispatches it. The munmap path calls its

unmap. No specific SDK munmap subscriber was established; the generic exported
registration interface is retained conservatively. Callbacks must obey the
blocking-notifier context and preserve caller state. Runtime accounting and

layouts. Final ACK's x86 list excludes KFENCE and the clock-test pair, but retains

image-only targets and their reduced configurations are outside the SDK workflow.
Their omission does not remove independently retained generic guest interfaces.

The temporary RockPi DT-rule rollback was exactly undone; current native split

while native x86 builtin/bootloader combination and generic OF support remain.
`339284930a26` removes an orphan comment that described the omitted ARM extension;
