## Shared headers and bounded firmware parameters

The 35 candidate decisions and pinned local consumer evidence are in
`evidence/shared-header-review.json`. No additional remote resource was used.
URLs embedded in historical commit messages were not opened.

Retain the exact ACK dm-user header: GrapheneOS snapuserd's local
`dm_user_header` has the same five u64 fields, and `DmUserBlockServer` reads
that protocol from its control fd. The Bionic ICMP member spelling prevents
collision with the actual `__unused` macro in Bionic's `sys/cdefs.h`, without
changing packet layout. The quota2 types include remains; the old android_fs
trace header is absent from both final ACK and LTS. Native firmware caching
configuration/implementation is already present, and the SDK disables caching.

Generic task/TCP/writeback/signal/QoS/lock OEM storage remains for rebuilt vendor
modules. These are functional data slots, not historical KMI padding. Their
presence does not implement RTT statistics, alter signal delivery or change
lock ordering. No particular SDK subscriber is established; module owners must
initialize and synchronize their state and manage lifetimes. Current task OEM
storage has six slots; dmabuf_info precedes the variable-size architecture
thread member. Five 64-bit OEM page flags and trace names remain with native
flag-capacity checks and preparation masks. Modules must release page metadata
before freeing pages. The sched_blocked_reason trace uses __get_wchan while
try_to_wake_up holds pi_lock; calling the locking wrapper would recurse on that
lock. Existing task/DMA accounting lifetime reviews remain applicable.

`14e6be19625d` keeps the ten-directory firmware parameter but fixes observed
ACK parser/getter defects. Shorter replacements used to leave stale directories;
overlong inputs were silently truncated; maximum-length entries lost their
separator or last character on readback. The new setter validates the entire
replacement before publishing it, clears unused entries and returns -ENOSPC on
excess length/count. Each directory permits 255 characters, preserving native
single-directory capacity. A mutex serializes setter/getter and copies each
mutable directory before path formatting. Filesystem I/O runs without this
mutex; an entire multi-directory search is not a snapshot and can observe an
update between attempts. Native init-namespace reads, LSM checks, size limits,
decompression and cleanup remain. Newline stripping applies consistently to
every custom directory; the four default search paths retain their order.
The parameter still has mode 0644. Accepting more configured directories expands
the administrator-controlled search order; no unprivileged write access is
added. Atomic updates prevent unintended stale or partially copied paths.

The parser uses bounded static scratch storage because boot parameter parsing
precedes mm_core_init. Each loader call adds a 256-byte stack snapshot; getter
size is checked against PAGE_SIZE. The host test reproduces the baseline stale
path failure and passes 815 replacement/boundary cases plus 20,000 writes and
40,000 concurrent reads under ASan/UBSan with pthread/string shims. This is not
a kernel firmware-load, locking or LSM runtime test. GrapheneOS ueventd documents
the parameter; an SDK multi-directory setting is unestablished, so this generic
Android configuration interface is retained conservatively.

`45b0ec108d3d` implements the already declared generic
power_supply_get_by_reference_array API. The original OF-only helper and its
inline-stub fix were superseded by ACK's firmware-node replacement. ACK's array
implementation increments use_cnt without retaining a device reference and
ignores iterator errors. The adaptation takes both references, bounds the signed
array size and int return count, propagates errors and releases/clears acquired
entries on failure. Surrounding native power-supply code is unchanged. Callers
must still put successful results before unregistering their supplies, as for
the native single-result helper. The host test reproduces ACK's missing retained
reference and passes 61,440 cases with 1,364,996 assertions, including overflow,
missing references and injected iteration errors. It simulates class iterator
lifetime; it does not verify concurrent kernel unregister behavior. No SDK
caller is established, which is insufficient grounds to remove a generic vendor
source API. The separately declared system-battery get/put helpers remain a
review item and are not implemented by this array change.

`82553b7cc82d` retains the generic SOC_SINGLE_BOOL_EXT_ACC macro while restoring
native headers for omitted backends. The six-line macro uses existing ALSA
callbacks and explicit control access flags; native access checks remain. A
specific SDK instantiation is unestablished. RC_CORE/LIRC are enabled, so retain
the generic one-second IR transmit ceiling; native zero-pulse and cumulative
remaining-duration checks are unchanged. This extends the bounded maximum from
0.5 to 1 second and does not establish physical emitter compatibility.
FAULT_NOWARN's enum is available in disabled fault-injection configurations,
without changing their no-op helpers.

Chrome EC calibration, lightbar segment/large-sequence and power-monitor
protocol additions are omitted together with their header fixes. Both SDK
configurations disable CHROME_PLATFORMS; sensor/lightbar dependencies identify
the required EC backend. Driver code, ABI text and command header now all equal
LTS. These fixes may be required on Chrome hardware; importing an orphan payload
would not implement that feature. SOF/SoundWire are disabled; keep native ACPI
const qualifiers, IPC4 definitions and topology tokens with the native backend.
The special FIPS140 module is explicitly ARM64-only in ACK Kconfig. Its forced
fips_enabled and alternate static-key implementation serve module integrity,
not solely KMI. Remove those orphan conditionals with the omitted module while
preserving native FIPS and jump-label behavior; no certification is claimed.

The two UFS hook-header fixes follow the separately documented UFS scope:
Goldfish SDK storage uses virtio. UFS remains configured and native UFS supports
a QEMU device, so absence is not inferred from a disabled driver or universal
physical-only assumption. Keep exact native UFS code and omit the complete
Android UFS hook family for the selected backend. Generic block, crypto and
module interfaces retain their independent decisions.

## Power-supply source interfaces and sparse sysfs enums

`evidence/power-tail-review.json` finalizes nine candidates, including both
unique power-supply merge resolutions. `610c2b5413c3` supplies previously orphaned
generic source interfaces and sysfs labels. No new remote resource was used.

System-battery enumeration now implements the retained get/put declarations.
The matching ACK implementation filters battery devices and excludes explicit
device-scope supplies; missing/failed scope properties retain ACK behavior.
A first pass counts, then a second pass collects device and use_cnt references.
An added INT_MAX check prevents count overflow. The two passes are not an atomic
snapshot: disappearing batteries can reduce the result, and an increase beyond
the allocated count returns EOVERFLOW and releases partial results. Allocation
or iterator errors and an empty second pass likewise release resources and
return a null output. Successful callers must put the returned supplies before
unregister, following the native lifetime contract. The complete core outside
these helpers and the earlier firmware-node array API equals pinned LTS.

ACK TCPM is a positive consumer of this generic API, although the selected SDK
does not use that physical PD negotiation path. A specific SDK consumer remains
unestablished; generic vendor source compatibility warrants retention. The host
test passes 139,264 scenarios and 1,580,440 assertions covering changed supply
sets across passes, scope filtering, allocation failures, injected iteration
errors, reference cleanup and count overflow. These simulated passes do not
certify concurrent kernel registration/unregister behavior.

Keep Android's final TAPER_EXT=50 and the Taper sysfs label, as well as the two
PD SPR AVS labels matching their retained enum definitions. The native formatter
previously had no entries for those additions. Importing ACK's sparse Taper
entry without adapting native helpers is unsafe: the first exact lookup stops
at a null slot, and the escaped-name pass dereferences null. Host ASan/UBSan
reproduces this with the final ACK functions. The adaptation scans non-null
entries for exact and escaped matches, skips holes in capability formatting,
retains numeric fallback for holes/unknown values and bounds shifts to the
native unsigned-int mask width. This also prevents unsafe shifts if the sparse
array grows beyond an architecture's word size. Native property get/set,
visibility, extension locking and uevent paths remain intact.

Taper can be reported and parsed through charge_type at its existing value 50.
It cannot fit in the existing unsigned-int charge_types capability mask; the
port documents this limit and rejects it in that mask API, rather than widening
native interfaces or inventing a new value. Other native enums and capability
masks retain their behavior. The sysfs host tests pass 11,834 assertions covering
sparse/negative/out-of-range values, all native charge capability combinations,
exact/escaped names and the complete USB label table. No live sysfs or charger
runtime test was performed; retaining labels does not add physical PD support.

Warm/Cool/Hot health properties are already represented upstream in the header,
text table and ABI documentation. The temporary Pixel6 battery-info exposure
rollback and reapply have exact inverse changed lines; retain native battery
property discovery and current extension handling. The 5.18 merge's two unique
hunks preserve both BYPASS and TAPER_EXT. The 6.17 merge adopts native firmware
node helpers while retaining a transitional OF-only array API, subsequently
superseded by the firmware-node array adaptation. Neither merge resolution
requires reinstating obsolete phandle stubs or reverting newer LTS code.

This completes the system-battery question identified in the preceding shared
header checkpoint. The earlier physical-topology source-selection note for the
core has been replaced with specific generic-interface decisions and test
references. Absence of an established SDK caller is recorded as uncertainty,
not treated as proof that these source APIs are unnecessary.

## Configuration and metadata merge resolutions

Sixteen unique merge resolutions are reviewed individually in
`evidence/metadata-merge-review.json`. Review used the complete remerge patches,
not the commits' first-parent diffs or long lists of imported parent commits.
No source change or new remote resource was needed for this batch.

Native roothash-signature, DM audit and LoadPin object/configuration rules are
preserved alongside separately reviewed Android default-key, BOW and USER
targets. The historical DM_VERITY_AVB handler was removed by final ACK itself;
its old merge resolution does not warrant resurrecting vbmeta invalidation.
Native audit and root-signature options remain available even though the SDK
configuration disables them. The old modpost LTO-object prerequisite resolution
is superseded by native output-symdump/modpost-deps rules, whose complete tail
is unchanged; current mixed-build/signature adaptations remain independently
reviewed and build 190 verifies module metadata.

The root Kconfig retains both native io_uring and external vendor Kconfig
inclusion. Local Kleaf source implements the external-prefix setup; this does
not claim that an optional Kleaf test target was executed. Staging rules retain
Android ashmem while accepting native GDM724X removal. The selected ashmem is
the current Rust implementation, not the historical C driver. Native pidfs is
unconditionally built; removal of the transitional FS_PID option does not
remove process file descriptors. Native INTEL_MEI defaults remain intact, with
the option enabled; a virtual guest Management Engine is not inferred from
that configuration.

Native kernel-parameter documentation has one keep_bootcon and one kunit.enable
entry. The generic KUnit default is enabled and explicitly overridable by
KUNIT_DEFAULT_ENABLED; the SDK sets it off, and native test.c obeys that setting.
Do not replace that accurate generic description with an unconditional Android
default. Native Hyper-V EARLY annotation remains, while the omitted ARM DCC
runtime-enable extension is not documented as available. The only unrelated
parameter-document delta is the separately reviewed DPM watchdog description.

UML IRQ, Qualcomm PCIe delay/unwind/register and ARM PSCI include resolutions
retain exact native platform implementations. Their architecture, firmware and
controller requirements place the Android extensions outside this x86 SDK;
this does not exclude generic IRQ, PCIe or power tracing. Native PSCI power
tracing is retained without the ARM-specific vendor hook family. No physical
controller or ARM runtime behavior is verified.

MAINTAINERS resolutions preserve current native documentation-index and INA233
records without restoring historical Incremental FS contact metadata. Incfs
source/configuration remains independently retained. The only unrelated
MAINTAINERS delta names the retained fsverity trace header. Two merge conflicts
change only ARM64 ABI allowed-break records for task_struct, TLB-batch and UFS
fields. Omit these historical binary-KMI exemptions as required; the merged
functional fixes listed in their messages remain separately inventoried.

## FUSE protocol history, wakeups and request hooks

`evidence/fuse-history-review.json` resolves 27 historical protocol, hook and
merge candidates. The original passthrough skeleton, temporary reverts,
reapplication, capability changes (28 to 31 to 63), pointer-bearing V1 argument
and u32 V2 ioctl are superseded by ACK's own `c26c49ee3b64` merge of upstream
FUSE 6.9. All seven paths and 861 lines of its unique resolution were examined.
The current ACK and port use native capability bit 37, backing-file IDs and
BACKING_OPEN/CLOSE. The supplied libfuse still has legacy fallback code, so this
is not a claim that old interfaces have no consumers. MediaProvider positively
supports the current protocol, selects it only after feature discovery, and
uses the new backing-file registration/close APIs. Do not reintroduce the
retired protocol or collide with native privilege/xattr flags.

The native ioctl dispatcher, clone, backing-open/close and synchronous-init
handlers remain exact LTS, including full command matching, native validation,
fixed-width arguments and ENOTTY for unknown commands. An old resolution that
kept EINVAL for wrong magic is not replayed. The retained Android mixed-open
policy, capability relaxation, required negotiation, backing ownership and
writeback-error fixes remain as described under "FUSE mixed passthrough
ownership and access". The previously checked extracted functions are unchanged;
their hashes were revalidated without claiming another kernel execution test.

Canonical-path opcode 2016 and its daemon-context callback survive beside every
native opcode, including syncfs, tmpfile, statx and copy-file-range-64. The 6.9
merge temporarily removed this interface; final ACK reapplied it. Current
MediaProvider registers a canonical-path callback and libfuse includes the
terminator in replies. The port keeps zeroed allocation, split args.h, errno
and ENOSYS handling, bounded termination and rejection of zero-size replies;
it does not replay the original unchecked size-minus-one indexing. Existing
BPF lookup/reference and canonical fsnotify analyses remain applicable.

Retain synchronous request scheduling hints using a private one-shot request
flag consumed by the native device queue. This adapts the old bool argument
through changed upstream queue APIs without changing native virtio_fs.c.
Forget, interrupt and background paths retain ordinary wakeups; native locking,
disconnect and request completion remain. The three request vendor hooks retain
their final source signatures and GPL exports. Fetch runs under fiq->lock and
must not sleep; send/end expose borrowed request references before the relevant
put. A callback retaining an object must acquire ownership and must respect
core request state. Exact SDK hook registrations and performance effects are
unconfirmed; the generic vendor interfaces are retained conservatively.

Mixed merge resolutions also preserve exact native NFLOG/TRACE/MARK files,
ARM DMA mapping, versatile clock configuration, current UFS core and bootconfig
utility. Native complete_walk uses nd->state, not the incorrect nd->flags from
one historical resolution. Its removed success_walk_trace is not restored.
ACK explicitly reverted the credential hooks in `5e5d81fed553`; current
kernel/cred.c is native except its independently reviewed export namespace.
Physical UFS/ARM hook exclusions retain their previously documented SDK scope,
without asserting that compiled UFS support is disabled.

The read-only Android passthrough feature attribute is required by the concrete
MediaProvider discovery check. Its config gate and sysfs error/unregister paths
are retained, and `a6086a9c7c71` restores the omitted ABI descriptions for this
attribute and the already retained BPF discovery attributes. This description
change affects no executable source. Build 190 compiles FUSE, passthrough and
BPF; no live mount, redaction, concurrency or hook execution was tested. The
broader mixed-mapping/cache and BPF concurrency limits remain unchanged. This
batch does not finalize the large original BPF implementation candidate. Only
local checkouts and recorded local evidence were used.

## Documentation accuracy and architecture-scoped crypto

`evidence/docs-crypto-tail-review.json` reviews 25 remaining documentation and
crypto-wrapper candidates against the delivered implementations. Three complete
documentation patches and one partial patch are recorded in `4d139dba3d8c`:
module symbol flags now describe __kflagstab, x86 fscrypt guidance no longer
recommends the removed CRYPTO_POLYVAL_CLMUL_NI option, dm-verity explains its FEC
input parameters and interleaving, and xHCI DbC documents its actual five state
strings rather than an additional nonexistent stalled state. These changes
affect no executable code. Module GPL/CRC enforcement, cryptographic checks,
FEC verification and endpoint stall processing remain unchanged.

The POLYVAL edit is deliberately partial: HCTR2 selects the common library and
the x86 library implementation is enabled through CRYPTO_LIB_POLYVAL_ARCH, while
the native ARM64 Kconfig/Makefile remain outside this SDK port and still name
CRYPTO_POLYVAL_ARM64_CE. Their existing documentation is retained. This is not a
claim that an ARM kernel can be built from this x86-targeted port. FEC constructor
checks confirm that fec_blocks covers data/hash/optional metadata rather than
parity blocks, and requires equal block sizes and valid roots. The earlier
corrected-block status description remains. The updated FEC text is explanation,
not a new security proof or recovery test.

Native documentation already represents the Maxim TCPCI binding and shared
Type-C partner/cable identity attributes, so their older patches are not replayed.
Keep the native KUnit documentation: the SDK disables its configurable default,
but a blanket claim that built-in tests are unsupported would be inaccurate.
The F2FS LaTeX-only table reformat is omitted; its values/meanings are unchanged,
the emulator workflow does not render LaTeX, and all separately retained Android
F2FS feature descriptions remain.

The protected ARM documents were read in full, including MMIO guard IDs/ranges,
memory relinquish, partial-progress returns, IOMMU DMA ownership, reset-to-blocking
and power-state requirements, guest pvIOMMU operations, device resource tokens,
and VFIO container/topology interfaces. Their specific ARM EL2/HVC64 providers
are outside the selected x86 SDK. Native KVM, VFIO, IOMMU and virtualization
documentation remains; this does not claim protected virtualization unnecessary
for other targets or remove any native isolation check. The Gunyah documentation
similarly describes a separate ARM64/GIC platform with an explicit ARM64 config
gate. URLs embedded in these local documents were not accessed.

Four fips140-module.c changes concern namespace import syntax, a complete crypto
library override table, removal of algorithm-registration exemptions, and a
library-based integrity HMAC. They include functional integrity changes and are
not classified as KMI-only. The ACK module requires ARM64, Clang and module
build, so it remains omitted as a complete platform-specific implementation.
Native FIPS and jump-label code is retained without claiming FIPS certification.
The ARM/ARM64 AEGIS SIMD guard refactor is likewise outside the x86 backend;
the native balanced NEON wrapper remains. Physical UFS TX-equalization binding
extensions are omitted consistently with their provider scope, while native
UFS code and binding remain. No ARM/protected-host, documentation-renderer,
crypto-certification or physical-device execution is claimed. No additional
remote resources were used.
